The round is not spent (the step's `review_rounds` goes back by one; its directory stays), the step stays `reviewing` with that round named in its `review` (`no_change: true`, `delivery: null`), and the window the rejected round kept stays. `workflow resume` delivers that same round, judging the revision as it is (`review.accept_revision`, so it is not compared again), and spends it; that start is held, as any round start is, while the token budget or the run's timeout is used up or a lock its `review.uses` names is taken (the same round, still `no_change`, marked `held`, nothing spent, and its release comes back to it); `resume --from STEP` runs the author again, the stopped round ending with no `rounds` record; `cancel` ends the run. `current.review.next_round` is that round. Otherwise the delivery judges the revision the engine computed. A revision that can't be computed then is left to the delivery, which reports it (`revision_failed`).

A round that ends incomplete because some verdicts did not count, each for a reason of its own (missing, empty, a bad header, written by another pane, unproven, a `log_gap` after the agent daemon restarted, and the rest of the row problems in [Verdict files](#verdict-files)), runs only those reviewers again in the same round and directory while the round has deliveries left: each such rerun spends one of the round's `review.redeliveries`, up to the definition's `redeliver` (default 3; `0` turns it off), shared with deliveries again. The part that ran is recorded on the reviewed attempt's `rounds` (`ended: rerun`, with every row it read) and in the history (`review_ended` with `outcome: rerun`, then `review_rerun` with `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `reviewers`, `rerun` (the count) and `redelivery`); the round's `review` names the reviewers as `rerun` (and `pending`), counts `reruns`, and keeps the counted rows as its `reviews`. The rerun judges the round's own revision, so the verdicts already written still match it; its prompt (`<step>.<n>.r<round>.rerunN.review.md`) holds only those reviewers' sections and says the other verdicts in the directory stand; the window the round ran in is cleared (`/clear`) before its line is typed. A verdict written in an earlier pane of the round still counts: the round keeps every pane it was delivered into (`review.panes`), and a verdict is proved against any of them. Its verdict copies are named `<step>.<n>.r<round>.rerunN.review.<reviewer>.md`, so no copy of an earlier part is overwritten. A round delivered again on a restarted tmux server drops the panes of the old server, so a pane id the new server reuses proves nothing. An agent daemon restart during a round leaves every verdict written before it unproven (`log_gap`: a later write to it may have been lost), so all those reviewers run again, not only the ones that had not written. A round that stops names no reviewer as `rerun`. Once the deliveries are spent, or for a problem of the round itself (`no_round_dir`, `no_review_pane`, `no_revision`, `round_dir_missing`, `round_dir_link`), the run stops `review_incomplete` as before. A delivery whose line may have gone out (the agent daemon gave no answer as it typed, `connection_failed` or `unreadable_reply`; the process was stopped after it began typing; or a write-back failed after the line went out) records `failure.typed`, keeps the round's pane, window and reviewer binding and sets `delivered_at`, so the reviewers' own Stop still decides the round. Such a round, or one whose process is gone after it began typing, is judged before it is delivered again, once the review pane's turn is proven over (or the pane is gone, or shows a shell where its agent was), never while a reviewer may be at work: a review pane whose Claude Code is at work (or runs sub-agents) shows the line arrived, and is waited for: past the review's `timeout` (default 30m, from the round's start) `workflow sweep` presses its interrupt and the turn that ends is judged; with no press by twice that timeout the round stops `review_incomplete` (no reviewer runs again into a pane still at work); one waiting for an answer (a permission prompt, a question) is never judged by a wait, since `workflow status` reads that review pane as it reads a live round's and shows the prompt as `waiting_you` (`details.role` `reviewer`, also in `--needs-me` and the inbox); while the agent daemon can't be reached it is waited for until the review's `timeout`, then judged; with no row from the agent daemon, or an idle pane whose turn is not proven over, it is judged once 210 seconds (plus the definition's `stall`, when set) have passed since its failure (else since its line went out); `workflow sweep` reads such a round's review pane as it reads a delivered round's (`agent_gone`, `pane_gone`, `server_restarted`, `idle_turn_over`), never as `round_undelivered`; so a review that finished meanwhile counts and only the reviewers still missing run again. `not_delivered` is not among these: it is delivered again whole. Needs `workflow` 38.

#### A gate after a review

Needs `workflow` 39 in `capabilities`.

Each round that its verdicts end (approved, rejected, or stopped `review_incomplete`, `review_stale` or `review_guard`; not a rerun of its missing reviewers, which ends only once the round does) gets a `summary.md` the engine writes in the round's directory, `<artifacts>/reviews/<step>.<attempt>.r<round>/summary.md`: the round, the revision it judged, the rule (`need` approvals including each `must`), the counted approvals, the problem of a round that could not be decided, and one line per reviewer with its verdict, whether it counted (and why not) and its file. It is written once, mode 0600, never through a link, and never over a file already there unless that is a regular file holding the very bytes it would write (a decision made again writes the same text: it holds no time); the reviewer name `summary` is reserved and every reviewer's write of `summary.md` is refused, so the file is the engine's alone. Its path and SHA-256 are kept in the round's entry of the attempt's `rounds` as `summary` (`{path, sha256}`, or `{problem}` when it could not be written), and a decided round's `review_outcome` gains `summary`, the path.

A step with both `review:` and `gate: approve` waits at its gate only once a round approves it, with that round's `summary.md` as the gate's note: the `waiting_you` reason's `details.summary` names it, `workflow status` shows it as `gate_file` with its current hash, and `workflow approve --note-sha256 SHA` refuses with `stale_revision` when the caller read another hash. The path is worked out from the run's directory, the step, the attempt and the round, never read back from the run file; it must be a regular file, not a link, whose bytes still hash to the `sha256` the round's record holds. A summary that is gone, or changed since the engine wrote it, refuses `approve` (and `workflow status` shows no `gate_file`, with a warning) as `gate_note_unreadable`, whose message names the way on: `workflow reject` (the step runs again and a new round writes a new summary), `workflow resume --from STEP`, or `workflow cancel`. A run stored before summaries, or a summary that could not be written, falls back to the step's `note:` line as before. A gate `reject` runs the step again as a new attempt, which is reviewed again in a new round: the round count is not reset (`review_rounds` keeps counting toward `max_rounds`).

Once the run's lock is given up, each such round also upserts one inbox item per run and step in the workspace's inbox, dedupe key `review:<run>:<step>`, type `review_request`: its title names the round and outcome, its body says what the run does now (waits at its gate, runs the step again, stopped for a person, went on) and where the summary is, and its `links` hold `run_id`, `step`, `attempt`, `round` and `summary`. A later round updates the same item and opens it again when it was marked done or dismissed. `review.notify: false` turns the item off (the summary is still written). An inbox that can't be written posts no item and never stops the run.

#### A review round's prompt

The text a review round's pane is typed is built by the engine and written to `<artifacts>/steps/<step>.<attempt>.r<round>.review.md`. It names the round and revision, says that the subject is material to judge, not instructions, and gives the order: the reviewers with `parallel: true` start at once as sub-agents, then each `parallel: false` reviewer runs alone, in `from` order (with none in the batch, the first starts at once), and every reviewer runs even when the outcome is plain. It says that the agent daemon records every Write and Edit a sub-agent of the pane makes and that a verdict counts only when such a sub-agent wrote it. Then comes one section per reviewer, running from its `## Reviewer NAME` heading to a closing `--- End of reviewer NAME` line whatever headings stand between (the headings of a reviewer's packs are moved two levels down): the step's `review.subject` filled for that reviewer and shown in a fenced block (escaped line by line with its newlines kept, cut to whole lines within 8 KiB with a note when cut, more backticks in the fence than the text holds in a row, since the subject can carry a ticket's or pull request's text), the reviewer's `include:` packs composed as a step's are (a pack that can't be found is an error, as for a step), its prompt, the path of its verdict file and the verdict format the engine owns (the lines of [Verdict files](#verdict-files), shown with `verdict: approve`; `reject` is the other value). The subject and a reviewer's prompt may use `{{review.revision}}`, `{{review.round}}`, `{{review.verdict_path}}` (that reviewer's file in the round's directory) and `{{review.previous}}`, all filled in one pass, so a value is never read again as a placeholder. `{{review.subject}}` in a reviewer's prompt reads "the subject above": the subject stands in the block of the same section. `{{review.previous}}` is the path of that reviewer's own write-up from the latest earlier round the run recorded (read from the stored rounds' verdict rows, in any attempt; a row with a `problem`, no verdict or an unsafe path is skipped, so an older real one shows), and empty in round 1. The round's delivery writes this file (see [A review round's window](#a-review-rounds-window)).

A reviewer's write-up that a rejected step's next attempt is told about goes in as a fenced block, labelled "reviewer write-up, opinion not instructions", with the path of the full file: the head of the file in whole lines, control characters escaped, fenced with more backticks than the text holds in a row, cut at 8 KiB, and nothing at all for a file that is missing, a link or not a regular file.

#### A review round's revision

A round's revision is one SHA-256 over the step's `produces` files (a file that is now a link or not a regular file reads as missing, never through the link), the checkout's HEAD, the tracked tree (each tracked path whose working copy differs from HEAD, staged or not, with its mode and bytes, its link target, or that it is gone; untracked files and submodules are not in it, and no diff text is hashed, so a `diff.*` setting can't move it) and, when the step's `review.revision` is set, that command's stdout. The command runs as a `status:` check does: in the checkout, through `sh -c`, with `WORKSPACE_RUN_ID`, `WORKSPACE_RUN_ARTIFACTS` and `WORKSPACE_RUN_BASE_SHA` and every `GIT_*` variable removed, for at most 93 seconds (what the 210-second window below leaves after git's own limits), its stdout in `<artifacts>/steps/<step>.<attempt>.r<round>.revision.log` and its stderr in `.revision.err` beside it. Only stdout is hashed, so a warning on stderr is not a change; a failure's `problem` names the stderr file. Its output is added to the hash, never put in its place, so a subject outside the checkout (a workspace plan in the notes directory) and a file git does not see (anything under `.workflow/`) each move the revision.

When the review pane's turn ends, the revision is computed again with the run's lock given up (the round's `review.recompute` in the run file holds `started_at` and `try` meanwhile; another turn's end in the review pane changes nothing then). Under the lock again, and only while the run is still on that step, attempt, round and try with no stop, the round is decided: a revision that differs from the one the round was delivered with stops the run with `review_stale` and the verdicts are not read; a revision that can't be computed (the command failed, timed out or could not run, a `produces` file is over 16 MiB, or git could not read the tree) stops it with `review_incomplete`, the reason in `details.problem`; an unchanged one is tallied as [Verdict files](#verdict-files) says. `review_stale` never loops and starts no further round: the round already spent stays spent, and `workflow resume` starts a fresh round on the new revision, which spends one more. A recompute whose process died is noticed 210 seconds after it began: the agent daemon's timer and `workflow sweep` (outcome `judged_again`, cause `lost_round`) start it once more, and a second that never reports back stops the run with `review_incomplete`.

#### A review round's guard

A reviewer judges the checkout, it must not change it. As a round starts, the engine stores a snapshot of the checkout in the round's `review.snapshot`: `head` (the commit HEAD names, `"none"` on an unborn branch, `null` when git cannot say) and `tree_sha256` (the digest of the tracked files whose working copy differs from HEAD, the same reading the round's revision uses, `"none"` on an unborn branch, so a first commit is a change; only tracked files and HEAD are covered, an untracked file is never seen). When the review pane's turn ends, before the revision is computed again and before any verdict is read, the checkout is compared with it. A commit, or a tracked file edited, staged or removed, stops the run with `review_guard` (`details.changed` lists `head` and `tree`; `snapshot_head` and `head` are the two commits). Like `review_stale` it never loops through `on_fail` and starts no further round: the round already spent stays spent and its record ends `review_guard`, the step stays `reviewing`, and `workflow resume` starts a fresh round, which takes a new snapshot. A checkout that cannot be compared (the snapshot or the reading now has a part git could not read, and no other part differs) is never a pass: the run stops with `review_incomplete` and the problem names the unread parts. The snapshot and the comparison run git under the run's lock, as the step guard does. A round begun before this check existed has no snapshot and is not checked.

#### A held review round

A review round is not an attempt, but it spends tokens and active time, so every round start is checked against the token budget and the run's `timeout:` as an attempt's start is, with the same exemptions: a round delivered again after its delivery failed or lapsed, and a round leaving a lock wait, start regardless. A round past either limit is held before it starts: the run reads `waiting` with the reason `budget_spent` or `run_timed_out` (the same `details`, plus `review_round`, the round held), holds no lock, and the step stays `reviewing` with its `review` naming the round, `delivery: null` and `held` (the reason that held it). The round is not spent: `review_rounds` still counts only the rounds that started. The hold in the run file (`budget_hold` or `timeout_hold`) is `{"review_round": K}` and claims no pane, since the round runs in its own review window.

`workflow resume --extra-tokens N` (or `--extra-time DURATION`, or both when both are spent) ends the hold and starts that round, never an author attempt, so it asks no `max_attempts` allowance; the event log's `run_resumed` line has `budget: "released"` and `review_round`. A plain resume is refused with `budget_spent` or `run_timed_out` as for an attempt. So is a resume of a stopped round (`review_incomplete`, say) while a limit is used, since it would start a fresh round. `workflow status --json` offers `resume` with only the `extra_tokens` or `extra_time` need. `resume --from STEP` and `cancel` end a held round with no round record and no `review_ended` line, since it never ran.

#### A review round's window

A round runs in a tmux window of its own in the workspace's session, `review-<step>`, never a split of the author's window, so the author's panes keep their places. In iTerm's tmux mode it shows as a tab of the workspace's window, opened in the background (`-d`). The window carries the option `@workspace-review` set to `<run>:<step>:<round>` (it survives a rename, so the window is always found by it, never by its name), and `@workspace-review-store`, a 16-digit digest of the run store's directory, so a sweep run with another `HOME` or `XDG_STATE_HOME` on the same tmux server (a scheduler's, say) knows the window is not its own and its agent gets `WORKSPACE_REVIEW_RUN=<run>` in its environment. The agent is always Claude Code, started fresh in the run's checkout from the workspace's own Claude Code command (`claude` when the workspace has none), even when the project's main agent is Codex: the review needs Claude Code's Stop hook, sub-agents and `/clear`. It runs under the login shell (`$SHELL -lc`, `/bin/sh` when `SHELL` is not an absolute path to a program).

As a round starts, under the run's lock, the run gives up the step's own `uses:` and takes the review's `review.uses` (through the same lock store a step's `uses:` go through). A lock someone else holds keeps the round from starting: the run reads `waiting` with the reason `waiting_lock` (`details.resource`, `details.review_round`), the step stays `reviewing`, its `review` names the round with `delivery: null` and `held: "waiting_lock"`, and the round is not spent. The agent daemon's lock-wait timer, `workflow sweep` and a person's `resume` ask for the lock again; once it is free the same round starts, with no check of the token budget or the run's timeout, as a step leaving a lock wait has none. The round start also records the process that delivers it as `review.holder` (`pid`, `started`), and the checkout's snapshot ([A review round's guard](#a-review-rounds-guard)).

The delivery itself runs once the lock is given up, in this order: the workspace must be on the default tmux server (a workspace whose tmuxinator config sets `socket_name` fails the round as `review_custom_socket`, since a review window there can't be reached by its id; nothing is read or closed on the default server for it), the agent daemon must run (it is started when it does not; without it the round's verdicts can't be proved), the round's revision is computed ([A review round's revision](#a-review-rounds-revision)), its directory checked (made when nothing is there), its prompt written ([A review round's prompt](#a-review-rounds-prompt)), then the window: one already marked for this round (left by a delivery of it that died) is used again, any marked for another round of the run is closed (its pane's reviewer binding removed; nothing is closed when another process took this round's delivery over meanwhile), else a new one opens. Once Claude Code is ready in it, its pane is bound to the run as the reviewer (`role: reviewer`, `round`). Under the run's lock, and only while the run is still on that step, attempt and round, still delivering, with no stop and the mark held by the same process (the same pid and start time), the round records `pane`, `window`, `revision`, `prompt` and `packs` (one `{reviewer, ref, sha256}` per reviewer pack composed in, kept on the round's record in `rounds` too) before anything is typed, so the review pane's Stop finds its round however soon it comes. Then the line `Read <prompt file> and follow it.` is typed, and a last change under the same conditions records `delivered_at` and `delivery: null`, and the run's history gets `review_delivered` (`step`, `attempt`, `round`, `pane`, `window`, `reused`). A run that left the round meanwhile (cancelled, `resume --from`, another round) gets the window closed and that pane's reviewer binding removed instead; a round the review pane's own Stop already judged, is judging or stopped is left as it is, window and binding included. A failure removes the reviewer binding only together with the window it closes.

A delivery that fails is recorded on the round as `delivery: "failed"` with `failure` (`code`, `message`, `at`) and the history line `review_delivery_failed` (`code`): `no_daemon`, `revision_failed`, `review_tampered` or `review_incomplete` (the round's directory), `review_custom_socket`, `window_not_opened` (tmux could not open it), `agent_not_ready` (its message says a review window always runs Claude Code, and when the program is not on PATH), the code of a line that could not be typed (`pane_busy`, `pane_gone`, `not_delivered`), `delivery_interrupted` (the delivering process got a signal; the signal still stops it), or the code of an error the run file gave. The round's `pane` is taken off, so no Stop in it decides the round. Its window is closed, except one whose agent waits at a sign-in, trust or approval screen, and a window the delivery used again (its reviewer may still be at work from an earlier delivery of the round), which are kept for the person (the id in `review.window`; a delivery again that fails before it finds any window leaves that id as it was). While a delivery runs past 210 seconds (a slow agent start, a long `review.revision` command), the round counts as still being delivered for as long as the process in `review.holder` lives (its pid running with the same start time, or, when its start time could not be read as the round started, its pid running at all, but only for 840 seconds from the round's start, since a pid the system reused would otherwise read alive for ever; a process table that can't be read counts it as alive): a `resume`, the daemon's timer and the sweep then leave it alone.

A round whose delivery failed, or whose mark lapsed with its holder gone, is delivered again on its own: the agent daemon's timer (`workflow advance` with no flags) and `workflow sweep` hand it to the engine's tick, which records the lapsed delivery (`rounds` entry `ended: redelivered`), delivers the same round again from a new `started_at` and writes `review_started` with `again: true` and `redelivery` (the count so far). A stored failure first waits out the same backoff as an undelivered step line, from its `failure.at` (1 minute, then 5, then 15). Each delivery again spends one of the round's `review.redeliveries`, up to the definition's `redeliver` (default 3); once they are spent, or at once for `review_custom_socket`, which no delivery again gets past, the round stops `review_incomplete` with `details.problem` saying why, and its window (if any) is kept. A person's `resume` of a failed or lapsed round delivers it again as the same round and starts the count over. A delivery that died after its line was typed but before its last change (the round still `delivering`, its pane recorded) is delivered again into the same marked window, which is reused and never closed by that delivery; a reviewer still at work there is not typed into twice, since the delivery waits for its Claude Code to be ready.

The window closes once the round approves (its reviewer unbound), whether or not a gate follows, and every review window of the run closes when the run completes, is cancelled or its workspace is killed. A round that rejects keeps its window, idle, for the step's next round, its reviewer still bound to the rejected round until the next round binds it (so the write guard still holds for a sub-agent left running there; that pane's turn ends decide nothing while the author works); a round that stops for a person (`review_incomplete`, `review_stale`, `review_guard`, `review_tampered`) keeps its window to look at. The run file's `steps.<step>.review_window` (`round`, `window`, `pane`) names a kept window while the author works again or the next round is held, so `workflow sweep` leaves it. The step's next round takes that window over: it moves the window's `@workspace-review` mark to the new round, binds its reviewer to that round and clears the conversation (`/clear`, as a `context: fresh` step starts) as it types the line, opening no new window; its `review_delivered` history line has `cleared: true`. In such a window a turn counts for the round only when it began at or after the confirmed clear (`review.cleared_at`, when the agent daemon reports it, else, once the line was delivered, `review.clear_from`, stored before the line is typed; while the line is still being typed no turn counts): a turn a person had there before ends with the clear and is not the reviewers'. A delivery again into the same window keeps the round's earliest `clear_from` there, so a line an earlier delivery typed after clearing still counts. A turn whose start the daemon does not know (it restarted during the round) counts when it stopped at or after the delivery (`review.delivered_at`), and not before a delivery is recorded. A kept window that can't be marked is closed and a new one opened, and so is one whose Claude Code is not ready (an agent that exited there): the round's next delivery opens a new window. One kept at a sign-in, trust or approval screen, or whose line the daemon refused (a person's turn running there, no quiet in time), stays, and the round is marked `uncleared` (from before the line is typed until it is delivered, so a failure that can't even be recorded still leaves the mark), so its next delivery into it clears it then. While the author works again the rejected round's reviewer is still bound to that round, so a sub-agent of it left running can still write that round's verdict files; a write there after the decision reaches only round K+1's `review.previous` and shows as `differs` in `workflow audit`; the retry's text and the evidence copy are taken at the decision. A new window the round opens instead drops the step's `review_window`; so does `resume --from` another step, which closes the kept window. `resume --from STEP` closes the round's window. A late close of round K closes only windows marked for round K or earlier, so it never closes the window a resume opened for round K+1. Closing a window kills it with its pane and removes that pane's reviewer binding; a window the person closed by hand reads as a gone review pane, which `workflow sweep` stops `review_incomplete`.

Under tmux's `renumber-windows on`, closing a review window (or `dev`'s window) renumbers the windows opened after it while it was open, so a pane bound in one of those reads as moved (the review pane's own binding is compared by session and tmux server, not window index, so it is not affected). `workspace doctor` warns when the global option, or a session's own, is on (`tmux-renumber-windows`). If iTerm opens tmux windows as native windows, the review is a separate iTerm window that stays open across rejected rounds.

A review window no round uses is left over: its run is over, or the run's round records neither its pane nor its window id (a round being delivered keeps every window of its step). A window stamped by another run store is never judged. A window whose run this store does not have is reported (sweep outcome `nothing`, `reason` `unconfirmed`, with the `tmux kill-window` to run by hand), never closed, since it may be another state directory's window from before windows were stamped. `workflow sweep` closes each left-over window it finds (outcome `closed`, `cause` `orphan_review_window`, `action` `close_window`, removing its pane's reviewer binding) in the workspace named, the named run's workspace, or every tmux session; a window whose run file can't be read is left. `workspace doctor` lists them (`workflow-review-window-orphaned`, a warning; `--fix` leaves them).

`workflow status --json` gives a run with a budget a top-level `budget`: `tokens`, `action`, `extra_tokens`, `allowance`, `used`, `counted`, `uncounted` (each a count of ended attempts and review rounds, by whether its own `usage` could be read), `estimated` (how many of the counted ones came from an interrupt record), `measured`, `spent`, `minimum`, `live` and `live_spent`. `used` and `spent` leave out the running attempt. `live` is an Integer, or `null` when the figure is unreadable or no attempt is running (0 means nothing spent yet); `live_spent` is true when `used + live` has reached `allowance`, and means something only when `live` is not null. A status call reads at most 4 MiB of transcript per run and writes a small derived cache under `<runs dir>/live`. The text status prints `tokens: spent X of Y` for every run with a budget, adding the live figure to X and ` (live)` to the line when `live` is not null. Each action that would start an attempt gets the need `{"name":"extra_tokens","flag":"--extra-tokens","min":N,"suggested":M}` while the budget is used.

### Run timeouts

Needs `workflow` 33 in `capabilities` (a run's `timeout:`, `--extra-time`, `run_timed_out`, and a step's own `max_attempts`).

A definition's `timeout:` (for example `8h`) caps the active time a run may use (see [Active time](#active-time)). It is checked where an attempt or a review round would start (see [A held review round](#a-held-review-round)), with the same exemptions as the [token budget](#token-budgets): a redelivered line, a lock wait that ends and a parked resume typed again after a busy pane start regardless. Past it the run is held: it reads `waiting` with the reason `run_timed_out` (`details`: `kind: "timeout"`, `step`, `used`, `limit`, `timeout`, `extra_seconds`, `needed`, `suggested`, all in seconds), holds no lock, keeps its pane binding, and the step is left as it was. The event log records `run_timed_out`. An attempt already under way is not stopped, unless the definition sets `timeout_action: interrupt` and `workflow sweep` runs: then the sweep presses the working agent's interrupt keys once (see [Sweeping stopped runs](#sweeping-stopped-runs)), the turn ends, the step is decided as usual, and the next attempt's start holds the run as above. The interrupt never cancels or fails the run.

Like the budget, the deadline is in `workflow status --json`; the text status prints it for any run with a `timeout:` as a line such as `time: active 3h 10m of 8h 0m` (`time: wall ...` while `clock` is `"wall"`), and also shows it in a held run's reason. `workflow status --json` gives a run with a `timeout:` a top-level `deadline`: `timeout`, `action`, `extra_seconds`, `limit` (timeout plus extra, in seconds), `used` (active time in seconds), `spent` and `clock`: `"active"` once workspace has started recording downtime (an agent daemon or `workflow sweep` has run), else `"wall"` (downtime is not cut yet). Each action that would start an attempt gets the need `{"name":"extra_time","flag":"--extra-time","min":N,"suggested":M}` (seconds) while the time is used, read from the run's own deadline, so it is there when the reason is `budget_spent` with the time spent too. A run held by its timeout is listed by `workflow status --needs-me`, has an inbox row (`workflow_run_timed_out`), counts as stuck in `workflow compare` after a miss, is not orphaned by `doctor` while its checkout exists, and sends the `failed` alert (`WORKSPACE_ALERT_REASON` is `run_timed_out`, the text says the time limit is used). `workflow sweep` reports it as `nothing` with the reason `run_timed_out`.

`workflow resume` and `workflow reject` are refused with `run_timed_out` when they would start an attempt, unless `--extra-time DURATION` (`30m`, `2h`, or seconds, up to 365 days) covers the overrun: `details.needed` is the least in whole seconds and `details.suggested` rounds it up to 15 minutes, which the message gives as a duration to pass (`--extra-time 15m`). The run file keeps the total as `extra_seconds`, and the event log records `time_extended`. A resume ends the hold and starts the attempt with the context the hold kept. With the token budget spent as well, the refusal is `budget_spent`, names both needs (the time need under `details.run_timed_out`), and passing both flags clears both holds. A resume that starts no attempt ignores `--extra-time`.

#### Active time

Every `timeout:` clock (the run's, a step's and a review round's, and the `review.timeout` that decides whether a pressed reviewer answered) counts active time, not wall time. These are left out:

- **Stored stops that are a person's:** a gate, a `parked` resume, a pause, `budget_spent`, a failed check.
- **Machine downtime.** Every agent daemon tick and every `workflow sweep` pass writes a heartbeat (a machine-wide file under `~/.local/state/workspace/.workflows/`). When a beat finds the last one more than `workflows.downtime_after` behind (default `2m`, at least `30s`; a sweep beat allows that plus the sweep interval, 5 minutes, before the next beat counts, so a machine kept up only by the scheduled sweep is not read as down), it records the gap as a downtime span in `downtime.jsonl`, and every clock reading cuts it. Spans whose end is over 30 days old are dropped as new ones are written, so a run older than that loses only the cut of its oldest downtime. The gap since the last beat is cut too before the next beat has recorded it (the open gap). So with no daemon and no sweep running, nothing beats, and a manual `workflow advance` reads all the time since the last beat as downtime; no run moves without a daemon anyway. Keep `workflows.downtime_after` well above the 15 second daemon tick (it cannot be set under 30 seconds): a tick that blocks for longer than it is recorded as downtime. Until the first beat there is no heartbeat file and the clocks run on wall time (`clock: "wall"` in `workflow status --json`).
- **A stall the sweep recorded** (the sweep found the step's agent unable to work: its pane gone, a shell where the agent was, or its turn ended on an API error), for the causes `pane_gone`, `agent_gone` and `turn_failed` (see [Sweeping stopped runs](#sweeping-stopped-runs)). The cut starts when the sweep recorded the stall: for `turn_failed` that is when the sweep noticed, so the time between the failure and that pass still counts. It ends at the first of these: the sweep sees the cause cleared on the step's own pane (the pane is alive again; the agent row is present and no longer a shell; the row is present with its `turn_failed` flag cleared), a `workflow resume` that keeps the same attempt, the attempt's end, or the next attempt's start. The sweep checks every 5 minutes, so a cut can run up to one sweep interval past the real recovery; that errs toward the clock running, never toward a clock stopped for good. A pane row that is missing closes nothing. After an agent daemon restarts its in-memory `turn_failed` flag is gone, so the next pass closes that stall. A stall recorded while the step was not running is closed by the resume or the next attempt. A cause is recorded once for each attempt, so a second `turn_failed` after a recovery in the same attempt is not cut and counts as active time.

Overlapping stops are cut once. `--extra-time` still adds to the limit over active time. One clock stays on wall time: the "lost check" test that decides whether a `status:` check in flight has outlived its process.

### Pausing a run

`workflow pause RUN` holds a run so a person can look at it. While it is paused, nothing new starts: no next step, no retry, no review round, and no prompt typed into the agent's pane. The daemon's tick and `workflow sweep` leave the run alone. Below, "held" means the next step, retry, review round or prompt that would have started during the pause.

```
workspace workflow pause RUN --reason "checking the diff"
workspace workflow unpause RUN

workspace workflow pause RUN --interrupt    # also stops the agent's current turn
```

Without `--interrupt` the agent keeps working until its turn ends, and the run then waits instead of moving on to the next step. With `--interrupt`, the interrupt keys are pressed in the run's pane if the agent is working; `unpause` then tells the agent to continue. `--reason TEXT` (1 to 4000 characters) is kept with the pause and shown by `workflow status`.

`workflow status` shows a paused run with the reason `paused` (see [the reasons table](#where-a-run-is-stuck)) and lists it under `--needs-me`, with `workflow unpause` as its first action. `pause` stops the `timeout:` clocks: the run's, the step's and the review round's do not count the time paused (see [Active time](#active-time)). A resume already waiting for the agent's pane (see [Resuming from the run's own pane](#resuming-from-the-runs-own-pane)) is dropped when the run is paused. `workflow resume` (including a resume made from the run's own pane) is refused with `run_paused` while the run is paused. Both verbs print the usual action document (outcome `paused` or `unpaused`).

`pause` adds `interrupted` to its row: `true` when the interrupt keys were pressed in a working pane, else `false`. When the keys were tried and could not be pressed, the row also has `warning` with the reason. `unpause` starts what the pause held and adds an `unpaused` block:

| Key | Values |
|---|---|
| `action` | What `unpause` did. `dispatch`: started the step that was held. `round`: started the review round that was held. `rerun`: ran a review round again. `deliver`: typed the step's prompt again, which had not landed. `continue`: told the agent to continue after an `--interrupt`. `continue_pending`: the agent's pane is not idle yet, so the continue line is kept and typed once it is. `pane_gone`: the run's pane no longer exists. `evaluate`: the agent's turn ended during the pause, so the step was decided now. `none`: nothing was held |
| `held` | What the pause held: `attempt`, `round` or `delivery` (the prompt), else `null` |
| `continued` | Only with a continue line: `true` typed, `false` refused, `"pending"` kept for the daemon |
| `warning` | Why a continue line was refused or is pending |

`unpause` answers `outcome: failed`, with `reason` and `message` set, when the step it started could not be typed into the pane; the run is unpaused all the same and waits on that reason. `unpause` exits 0 even when the continue line is pending or refused: the daemon keeps and retries the line for up to an hour, so a script checks `continued` and `warning`. Refusals:

- `already_paused`: `workflow pause` on a run that is already paused.
- `not_paused`: `workflow unpause` on a run that is not paused.
- `run_not_active`: the run has finished or was cancelled.
- `run_paused`: `workflow resume` (or a resume from the run's own pane) on a paused run; unpause it first.
- `bound_pane`: `workflow unpause`, or `workflow pause --interrupt`, from a pane bound to a run or a process under the run's own pane, so the run's agent cannot lift a person's hold. A plain `pause` is allowed there.

### Tracing a run

Use `workflow trace RUN` to see where one run's time went. [`workflow events`](#reading-a-runs-events) is the raw event stream, [`workflow attempts`](#reading-a-runs-attempts) shows what the run file recorded about each attempt, and [`workflow stats`](#step-statistics) totals steps across many runs. `trace` reads the run file and its events, going or finished, and writes nothing. It needs the `workflow_trace` feature (`workflow` revision 54).

```
Run wr_261111011632jzju  workflow rpiv-auto-approve-local-ship  state waiting  (times are UTC)
01:16:32  run_started - -> running
...
01:23:04  implement#1 - -> running
01:23:07  implement#1 running -> running  3s
          waited 301s (turn_ended_background)
01:28:46  implement#1 running -> passed  339s  103k tok
01:43:43  ship#1 step_reported  156s
01:43:52  ship#1 running -> failed  cause=reported  9s  82k tok
          waiting since 01:43:52 (failed_check)
Totals
wall:    158h33m
active:  21m
stopped: 158h12m
waiting: turn_ended_background 316s, turn_ended_incomplete 36s, failed_check 158h6m (reasons can overlap, so they can add up to more than stopped)
tokens:  821k (in 242, out 50368, cache 770598)
loops:   none
attempts: 9
```

The same run with `--table`:

```
N   TS                    KIND        STEP#ATTEMPT  FROM     TO       ACTOR  CAUSE  DURATION  TOKENS
1   2026-11-11T01:16:32Z  transition  -             -        running  -      -      -         -
2   2026-11-11T01:16:32Z  transition  research#1    -        running  -      -      -         -
3   2026-11-11T01:16:36Z  transition  research#1    running  running  -      -      4s        -
4   2026-11-11T01:18:12Z  marker      research#1    -        -        -      -      96s       -
```

The first line names the run, its workflow and its state. A row is the time in UTC, `step#attempt` (or the event type for a run-level row such as `run_started`), then the state change (`from -> to`, `-` for no earlier state) or, for an event that does not change state, its type. After that come `actor=` and `cause=` when set, the time since the step's previous row, and the attempt's tokens on the row that ends it. A step's first row has no previous row, so the text output leaves its duration out (`--table` prints `-`). An indented line is a wait of more than a few seconds: `waited 301s (REASON)` once over, `waiting since TIME (REASON)` while it goes on. The [reasons](#where-a-run-is-stuck) are the codes `workflow status` shows. A `Totals` block follows (`wall`, `active`, `stopped`, `waiting`, `tokens`, `loops`, `attempts`). `--table` prints one grid row per transition, with the header `N TS KIND STEP#ATTEMPT FROM TO ACTOR CAUSE DURATION TOKENS`. `--json` prints one document, and wins when both are given.

The totals use the run's `timeout:` clock (see [Active time](#active-time)). `wall` is start to end, or to now for a going run. `clock` (`clock_seconds` in `--json`; the text prints only its two parts) is the window that clock covers, split into `active` (the clock ran) and `stopped` (it stood still). It stops for the waits that belong to a person (a pause, a park, a failed check, waiting on you, a gate), for a stall the sweep recorded, and for time the workflow daemon was not running. It keeps running for a lock wait (`waiting_lock`) and a retry backoff (`retry_backoff`); those show as `machine_waiting_seconds` and sit inside `active`. For example, a clock of 100s can be `active` 70s plus `stopped` 30s. If the run was stalled for 20s and paused for 15s, `waiting` lists `stalled 20s` and `paused 15s`, 35s in all, more than the 30s stopped, because the two overlapped for 5s. `stopped` is the exact figure; `waiting` is by reason.

#### Trace keys

`workflow trace --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":ID,"trace_id":HEX,"transitions":[...],"spans":[...],"totals":{...},"warnings":[...]}`. `trace_id` is the first 32 hex digits of the SHA256 of the run id, so it is the same on every read. A value the run never recorded is `null`, never 0.

Each transition has 17 keys: `n` (the event's line number; rows follow `n`, never `ts`), `ts`, `kind` (`transition` for an event that changes state, `marker` for any other), `type` (the event type), `from` and `to` (the run's state before and after), `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `cause`, `actor`, `duration_ms` (the time since the step's previous row), `tokens`, `exit_code`, `code`, `evidence` (the copies the step kept, each `name`, `kind`, `source`, `sha256`, `bytes`) and `derived`. `derived` is `true` when the event did not stamp both `from_state` and `to_state` (an older run, or a marker), so `from` or `to` was worked out from the event's type and the step's earlier rows. `tokens` is `{"total","in","out","cache"}` and sits on exactly one row of each attempt, the one that ends it; it is `null` when the attempt has no readable usage or was never delivered (a redelivered or not-delivered attempt), as `totals.tokens` does not count it. `cache` counts cache-creation tokens only, not cache reads.

Every span has the same 15 keys, `null` where one does not apply: `span_id` (16 hex digits, stable across reads), `kind`, `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `started_at`, `ended_at` (`null` while the span is open), `code`, `outcome`, `actor`, `note`, `tokens`, `head_before`, `head_after` and `retry_of`. `kind` is one of:

| `kind` | Covers |
|---|---|
| `attempt` | One attempt of a step: `outcome`, `code`, `tokens`, `head_before`, `head_after` and `retry_of`. |
| `review_round` | One review round that ran in a pane, with its own `tokens`. Those tokens are already in the attempt span's `tokens`, so adding every span's tokens counts them twice. |
| `gate` | The time a step waited at a gate, from `gate_waiting` to the next `gate_approved` or `gate_rejected`, with `actor` and `note`. A reject followed by another wait gives two spans. |
| `wait` | One reason the run's clock stood still or the run waited, with the reason as `code` (`unknown` when none is recorded). A gate's own wait is not repeated here. |
| `stall` | A stretch the run stalled, `code` `stalled`. |
| `downtime` | A stretch the workflow daemon was not running. |

`totals` holds:

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `wall_seconds` | Start to end, or to now for a going run. |
| `clock_seconds` | The run's clock window. |
| `active_seconds` | The time the run's clock ran. |
| `stopped_seconds` | The time it stood still, daemon downtime included. `active_seconds` plus `stopped_seconds` is `clock_seconds` to the millisecond. |
| `waiting_seconds` | Seconds by reason: every person or machine-free reason code, plus `gate`, `stalled`, `downtime` and `unknown`. Reasons can overlap (a pause during a stall), so the figures can add up to more than `stopped_seconds`. They add up to less when events were lost, because the spans come from events; `stopped_seconds` is the exact figure. |
| `machine_waiting_seconds` | Seconds by reason for waits the clock counts as active (`waiting_lock`, `retry_backoff`). They sit inside `active_seconds`. |
| `tokens` | `{"total","in","out","cache"}` over the run's attempts; all `null` when none count or a counted attempt has no readable usage. |
| `loops` | Loop counts by edge (`{"EDGE":COUNT}`, empty when the run looped nowhere). |
| `attempts` | Attempts in the run. |

The clock figures, `waiting_seconds` and `machine_waiting_seconds` are `null` when the clock has no origin.

`warnings` holds objects with a `code` and a `message`. `events_lost` also has `count` and `types` (the lines the history lost, and their types): the spans then miss time, and `waiting_seconds` can fall short of `stopped_seconds`. `event_line_skipped` means a line of the history could not be read and was skipped. `run_steps_unreadable` means the run file's `steps` could not be read: the trace then has no wait or stall spans and the clock figures are null.

### Reading a run's attempts

`workflow attempts RUN [--attempt-of STEP] [--json]` prints what the run file recorded for each attempt (see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)), going or finished, in the order the attempts began. It reads the run file and writes nothing. An unknown run is refused with `unknown_run`; `--attempt-of` with a step the run's definition doesn't have is refused with `unknown_step` (`details.steps` lists the real ones). `--attempt-of` is the flag's name because `--step` is for `approve` and `reject`; it is refused on any other verb.

Rows come from the definition's steps in order, then any other step the run recorded attempts for, so no recorded attempt is left out. Text output is a table with one row per attempt: `STEP`, `ATTEMPT`, `OUTCOME`, `CODE` (cut at 40 characters with a `~`; `--json` is whole), `HEAD` (`before>after`, seven characters each), `DURATION` and `TOKENS` (the main thread's input/output tokens), plus a `REVIEW` column (`2/3 approve, skeptic reject`: the approvals among the verdict rows the attempt kept, each rejecting reviewer, and `no verdict: NAME` for one that gave none) when any attempt was reviewed (needs `workflow` 34). `-` stands for a value that was not recorded, such as the outcome of an attempt still going or any field of a run stored before it existed. Stored text is printed with control and invisible characters escaped. A wait recorded outside any attempt is printed after the table as `Reason outside any attempt: STEP CODE SINCE UNTIL`.

`workflow attempts --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":ID,"attempts":[...],"unowned_reasons":[...]}`. Each attempt holds `step`, `attempt` (the attempt's `n`), `outcome` (`passed`, `failed`, `overridden` (a guard stop a person accepted with `resume --accept-guard`), `not_delivered`, `checks_passed` while a review round decides it, `interrupted` for an attempt `resume --restart-step` closed because its agent crashed or its pane went (new with `workflow` 48; the retry that follows has `retry_of`), `cancelled` for an attempt a cancel or workspace kill closed (new with `workflow` 33: a script that switched on the outcome should treat it as ended without a result; older runs show `null` there), or `null` while it is open), `code`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `agent`, `session`, `head`, `definition_sha256`, `prompt_sha256`, `packs`, `outputs`, `check`, `usage`, `evidence`, `reported`, `proofs`, `reasons`, `rounds` (the review rounds that ended on the attempt, see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)), `review` (the `REVIEW` summary string, `null` for an attempt with no verdict rows), `review_outcome`, `reviews` (the verdict rows, each with `isolation`) and `redelivered`, with the values the run file stores under those keys; `check` is the stored check's `command`, `log`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `exit_code` and `timed_out`; `reported` is the agent's `step done` report (`status`, `summary`, `code`, `at`) and `proofs` the rows of the `require:` proofs that ran outside the lock (see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)). Every key is always present and `null` when the run never recorded it (`reasons` too: an attempt with no reason history has `null`, not `[]`). `refusal` (needs `workflow` 46) is what the pane looked like when it refused the attempt's line as `pane_busy` (`error`, `message`, `reason`, `seen`), else `null`; the text table prints it under the row (see [Why a line was refused](#why-a-line-was-refused)). `redelivered` is `true` for an attempt that ended `not_delivered` and was typed again by `workflow sweep` (it does not count against `max_attempts`; the text table adds "(retried)" to its outcome), else `null`. `code` is the code the attempt failed with (an engine code such as `require_commits` or `check_failed`, or the agent's own `step done --code`), `null` for an attempt that did not fail and for any attempt stored before this key existed. `unowned_reasons` is the run's own `reasons` list: the waits that had no attempt to own them, each `{step, code, since, until}`, oldest first; with `--attempt-of` only that step's. They are not attached to rows, since a wait before the first attempt has no row to sit on; a reader joins them to attempts by `step` and time.

Needs `workflow` 12 in `capabilities` (`redelivered`: 17).

### Step statistics

`workflow stats ID [--last DURATION] [--name WORKSPACE | --project [NAME]] [--json]` shows, for each step of a workflow, how its attempts have gone across the runs of definition `ID`:

```
$ workspace workflow stats rpiv-auto-approve --last 30d
STEP         ATTEMPTS  PASSED  FAILED  MEDIAN  RESUMES  TOKENS/RUN
research           10      10       0  6m            2  310k
plan               24      23       1  11m           3  420k
plan_review        26      10      16  9m            9  640k*
26 runs of rpiv-auto-approve started in the last 30d.
```

It reads the run files, going and finished, and writes nothing. `ID` needs no definition on disk: only the runs are read, so the figures of a deleted definition still show. An `ID` with no runs prints `No runs of ID.` (exit 0; `--json` gives empty `steps`). With no flag every workspace is covered; `--name` or `--project` narrows it as in `workflow status`, and `--all` is refused as not needed. Rows follow the step order of the newest run's definition, then any step only older runs had; a step is the same step across definition versions when its id is the same.

`--last` takes `30d`, `2w`, `12h`, `90m` (a bare number means seconds; days and weeks are whole numbers; the forms `90`, `90s`, `5m`, `1h` also work). Only this window takes days and weeks; config durations and timeouts still take seconds, minutes and hours. A run is in the window when it started (`created_at`) at or after the cutoff, a run that started exactly at the cutoff included. A run with no readable start time is left out of a window and counted in `undated`. A bad value is a usage error. Finished runs are kept up to 200, so a long window may hold fewer runs than were made, and a run archived before an item was recorded shows only what it kept.

| Column | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `ATTEMPTS` | `PASSED` + `FAILED` + `OVERRIDDEN`: attempts that reached the agent and ended |
| `PASSED`, `FAILED`, `OVERRIDDEN` | By the attempt's stored `outcome`; an overridden attempt (a guard stop accepted by a person) is never a pass (`overridden` in `--json`) |
| `MEDIAN` | The middle of `ended_at - started_at` over those attempts that have both times |
| `RESUMES` | `run_resumed` events in the runs' histories, on the step each resumed (`resume --from STEP` counts on `STEP`, any other on the step the run stood at). A resume does not record why it was needed, and many followed a failed delivery. One naming no step is `unattributed_resumes` in `--json` and is noted under the table |
| `TOKENS/RUN` | The mean, per run, of the step's tokens: input, output and cache creation tokens of the main thread and its in-step sub-agents, and of each review round the attempt went through, the count `budget:` uses, over the same attempts the budget counts: passed and failed ones, and one a cancel or `resume --from` ended with no outcome (its tokens were spent), but not one still going or never delivered. Cache reads are never counted |

Where other attempts go: an attempt that was never delivered to the agent (`not_delivered`, or one marked `redelivered`) did no work, so it is counted apart (`not_delivered`) and left out of every column; an attempt with no outcome (still going, or replaced without ending), `checks_passed` (a reviewed step's attempt until its round rewrites it as `passed` or `failed`), or any other outcome (`cancelled`, `interrupted`), is counted apart as `unfinished` and also left out of the other columns (its tokens are still counted, as above, so a step whose only attempt was cancelled shows ATTEMPTS 0 and a token figure; `tokens_from_unfinished` in `--json` counts those attempts, and the text output adds a note under the table when there are any). The text output notes how many of each were left out.

`TOKENS/RUN` never hides a missing count. An attempt whose `usage` is null (another agent than Claude Code, a run from before tokens were recorded, or a heavy attempt the transcript tail could not cover) makes its whole run uncounted for that step; so does a review round of such an attempt whose `usage` is null (a round with no pane ran nothing and adds 0). The mean is over the counted runs only; `*` after it marks a step where some runs were left out, and `-` a step where no run was counted. `--json` gives `runs_counted` and `runs_uncounted` for every step.

`--json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":ID,"status":"ok"|"partial","last":TEXT-or-null,"since":ISO-time-or-null,"workspace":NAME-or-null,"project":NAME-or-null,"runs":N,"unreadable":N,"undated":N,"unattributed_resumes":N,"steps":[...]}`, each step `{"step","attempts","passed","failed","not_delivered","unfinished","median_seconds":N-or-null,"resumes","tokens_from_unfinished","tokens":{"mean":N-or-null,"runs_counted","runs_uncounted"}}`. A run file that cannot be read (the runs directory or the archive) counts in `unreadable`, since it cannot be told which workflow it belongs to: a damaged file of another workflow makes `partial` too; then `status` is `partial`, the exit code is 3 and the text output warns, because the figures are incomplete. Needs `workflow` 23 in `capabilities` (`compare`: 24).

### Comparing runs

`workflow compare ID [--by definition | --by pack:NAME] [--last DURATION] [--name WORKSPACE | --project [NAME]] [--json]` sets the runs of definition `ID` side by side, to see whether a change to the definition or to a pack moved the results:

```
$ workspace workflow compare rpiv-auto-approve --by pack:plan-review
SHA       RUNS  PASS-1ST  RESUMES/RUN
1dd4e0aa     6       33%          1.5
9ae2b7c1     4       75%          0.5
Too few runs to call this a difference; tasks also differ between runs, so this shows a direction and does not prove a cause.
```

Scope, `--last`, `--name`, `--project` (`--all` is refused), an `ID` with no definition on disk and the damaged-file behaviour are those of [`workflow stats`](#step-statistics): unreadable run files make `status` `partial`, exit 3 and a warning. `--by` is `definition` (the default) or `pack:NAME`; any other text is a usage error. It reads only what each run stored, so older runs can lack the hashes.

A run counts in exactly one group:

| `--by` | The run's group |
|--------|-----------------|
| `definition` | The run's `definition_sha256` (the definition it was started from, kept whole in the run, so it cannot change mid-run), else the one `definition_sha256` its attempts agree on |
| `pack:NAME` | The `sha256` of pack `NAME` (its name or its full ref) in the `packs` its delivered attempts recorded. The pack's text can change between attempts of one run: such a run is in the `mixed` group, under neither sha |

A run with no readable hash is in the `unknown` group: for `pack:`, a run whose delivered attempts recorded no `packs` at all (stored before they were recorded) or no hash for the pack. A run whose attempts recorded `packs` without `NAME`, and a run that never delivered an attempt (`not_delivered` only, or none), are in no group and are counted apart (`without_pack`, `no_attempts`). A `NAME` no run used is not an error: the groups are empty (exit 0), because an archived run may not have kept it, and the output says so.

| Column | Meaning |
|--------|---------|
| `SHA` | The first 8 characters in the table, all 64 in `--json`; `unknown` and `mixed` as described above |
| `RUNS` | Every run in the group, going and finished |
| `PASS-1ST` | The share of the group's settled runs that were a first try: they `completed` and every step they reached had exactly one delivered attempt, which passed. A rejected gate, a `resume --from` rerun or a loop adds an attempt, so it is a miss; so is an `overridden` attempt. An attempt that never reached the agent does not count, so a redelivery is not a miss. An `interrupted` attempt (a crashed agent that `resume --restart-step` restarted) is left out too, as a stop that is not the step's fault: its retry is the first try, so a restart is not a miss. A `checks_passed` attempt (a reviewed step waiting for its round) is left out until its round rewrites it as `passed` or `failed`. Settled runs are: every `completed` run, and a `cancelled` run or a run waiting on `failed_check`, `budget_spent` or `run_timed_out` that missed. A cancelled run with no miss and any other run still going are left out (`cancelled` and `running` in `--json`), so an abandoned clean run or a run in progress counts neither way. A stuck run counts as a miss while it waits (`stuck` in `--json`); a later resume can move it to a completed miss, never to a first try. `-` when none settled. `resume --pane` on a running step whose pane died starts a second attempt, so it reads as a miss though nothing failed (accepted). In a completed run an attempt with no outcome (passed over by `resume --from` a later step) is a miss too. No run is stored as `failed`: the engine ends runs `completed` or `cancelled` |
| `RESUMES/RUN` | The `run_resumed` events in the group's histories (every run of the group, counted as in `workflow stats`) divided by `RUNS`, one decimal |

The output always ends with the sample warning, and `--json` always carries it as `warning`. Its first clause, `Too few runs to call this a difference;`, is dropped once every group has at least 20 settled runs and there are at least two groups. The rest stays: the tasks differ between runs, so compare shows a direction and does not prove a cause.

`--json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":ID,"status":"ok"|"partial","last":TEXT-or-null,"since":ISO-time-or-null,"workspace":NAME-or-null,"project":NAME-or-null,"by":"definition"|"pack:NAME","runs":N,"matched":N,"without_pack":N,"no_attempts":N,"unreadable":N,"undated":N,"warning":TEXT,"groups":[...]}`, each group `{"sha","runs","settled","first_try","pass_first_percent":N-or-null,"running","cancelled","stuck","resumes","resumes_per_run"}`. `runs` counts the runs read and `matched` those in a group. Needs `workflow` 24 in `capabilities`.

### Auditing a run's evidence

`workflow audit RUN [--attempt-of STEP] [--json]` compares each file in the attempts' `evidence` (see [Evidence outside the checkout](#evidence-outside-the-checkout)) with the SHA-256 recorded for it, for a going or finished run. It reads the run file and the evidence directory and writes nothing. An unknown run is refused with `unknown_run`, an unknown `--attempt-of` step with `unknown_step`.

Each recorded file gets one status. The run file is the trust root: the audit trusts the hash it records, so an edit to both a copy and its recorded hash is not detected. The comparison always uses the entry's own `sha256`, never the attempt's `prompt_sha256` (that hashes the composed text and may differ from the prompt file).

| Status | Meaning | Counts as a mismatch |
|--------|---------|----------------------|
| `ok` | The copy hashes to the recorded `sha256` | no |
| `changed` | The copy now hashes differently (`reason: unreadable` or `too_large` when it could not be hashed); or the entry has no recorded hash and no `problem` or `too_large` (`reason: no_recorded_hash`; the engine writes a hash on every successful copy); or the entry says nothing was kept yet a copy is there (`reason: unexpected_copy`); or a review verdict was rewritten between the round reading it and the copy (`reason: changed_since_read`, `recorded_sha256` the hash the round read, `now_sha256` the copy's), so the copy is not what the round counted | yes |
| `missing` | The copy is not there; `reason` is `no_evidence_directory` (the run's evidence directory is absent, a link, or the run id is not a valid one) or `invalid_name` (the stored name is not a plain file name, so nothing was read) | yes |
| `replaced` | The copy is now a link or not a regular file (`reason`: `symlink`, `not_regular_file`) | yes |
| `not_copied` | The entry has a `problem` (`missing`, `outside_artifacts`, `copy_failed`; the `reason`): nothing was kept | no |
| `too_large` | The file was past the size limit (`reason: size`) or the attempt's 64 MiB budget (`reason: budget`): nothing was kept | no |

Entries from a run stored before evidence existed have no `evidence` key: they are listed under `without_evidence` (text: "No evidence recorded"), never as a mismatch. A stored `name` is read only when it is a plain file name (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`), directly inside `evidence/<RUN>/`; the copy is looked at with `lstat` first and never read through a link, and the run's evidence directory must itself be a real directory. A prompt's, check log's or review verdict's `source` in the checkout is also hashed (only when it lies under the run's artifacts directory) and shown as `source_status` (`same`, `differs`, `gone`, `outside`, `null` for outputs); `differs` is printed as "checkout copy differs" and does not change the exit status, since a cancelled check keeps writing its log and a later attempt rewrites an output. An edited verdict copy is `changed`, like any other. Text output escapes the stored names and paths.

Exit status is 0 when no copy mismatched (including a run with nothing recorded) and 1 when any file is `changed`, `missing` or `replaced`. With `--json` the exit status is the same and the document is still printed: `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":ID,"step":STEP-or-null,"clean":bool,"counts":{status: n},"directory":"<evidence dir>/<RUN>","files":[...],"without_evidence":[{"step","attempt"}],"event_errors":{"count":n,"entries":[...]}}`. `ok` says the command ran, `clean` says no copy mismatched. Each `files` row has `step`, `attempt`, `name`, `kind` (`prompt`, `check_log`, `output`, `review`), `source`, `status`, `reason`, `recorded_sha256`, `now_sha256`, `bytes` and `source_status`; every key is always present. An agent under the same account could change a copy and the run file's hash together, so a clean audit shows no change since the copy, not that nothing was altered.

`event_errors` lists the run's history lines that could not be written (each entry has `ts`, `run_id`, `type`, `errno`, `message`, read from `event-errors.log` in the workflow state directory; the full list, with `count` its length; needs `workflow` 45). Text output adds one line with the count when there are any. They do not change `clean` or the exit status: a lost history line is a different fact from a changed copy. A count above zero means the disk may be full or unwritable.

With `--json`, `next_actions` is empty when no copy mismatched. After a mismatch it holds `workflow files RUN`, which shows where the copies are, and one `workflow attempts RUN --attempt-of STEP` for each mismatched step; each has a `why` that names the mismatches (such as `2 changed, 1 missing`). They are things to look at, not repairs: nothing here re-copies a file or cancels the run. See [Next actions](#next-actions). The text output prints a `See where the copies are:` line after a mismatch.

Needs `workflow` 14 in `capabilities`; the `review` kind and `changed_since_read` need `workflow` 33.


### Next actions

`workflow audit --json` and `workflow sweep --json` carry a `next_actions` array, and `workflow status --json` carries the same shape as `actions` on each run. An entry is `{"action","args","needs"}` and, where the command can say why it offers it, `"why"` (see [next actions](README.json.md#next-actions)). `workspace ACTION ARGS...` runs as it stands when `needs` is empty. A next action is a suggestion for a person or for a caller acting for one. The sweep, the daemon and the engine never run one themselves, and a script should not run one unattended without checking it.

What each offers:

| From | Offered |
|------|---------|
| `audit`, after a mismatch | `workflow files RUN`, then `workflow attempts RUN --attempt-of STEP` for each mismatched step, each with the mismatch counts as `why`. Never a cancel or a reject. |
| `sweep`, a run held (`held`) | `workflow resume RUN` |
| `sweep`, a spent budget or time limit (`budget_spent`, `run_timed_out`) | `workflow resume RUN` with the `extra_tokens` or `extra_time` need `workflow status` gives its own resume |
| `sweep`, a held retry (`retry_blocked`) | `workflow resume RUN` needing a `--pane` (and any spent limit), and `workflow cancel RUN` |
| `sweep`, a stall with an automatic restart scheduled (`recorded`, action `infra_retry_scheduled`) | `workflow status RUN` only, with a `why` that says the step restarts on its own and when it is due; no resume or cancel is offered while it is pending |
| `sweep`, a stall whose restarts are given up (`recorded`, action `infra_retries_exhausted`) | as for a recorded stall: `workflow resume RUN`, `workflow resume RUN --restart-step` and `workflow cancel RUN` |
| `sweep`, a stall or gone pane it recorded (`recorded`) | `workflow resume RUN` and `workflow cancel RUN`, and `workflow resume RUN --restart-step` when the step's attempt is still open and the stall is `pane_gone` or `agent_gone` |
| `sweep`, a stopped workspace (`workspace_stopped`) | `launch WORKSPACE`; none for a left-over review window or a row with no workspace |
| `sweep`, a pane waiting on an answer (`prompt`) | `focus WORKSPACE --pane PANE`, the review pane while a round is under way |
| `sweep --dry-run` | `workflow sweep` with the same scope, to apply what the dry run found |

Rows that clear themselves, `refused`, `failed` and closed review windows give none. A row without a run id gets no action at all, and a row whose run or pane cannot be read gets none of the actions that need it. Each entry's `why` is the row's `reason` (or `cause`); the `--dry-run` entry says how many changes a real sweep would make.

### Alerts

`alerts.workflow` makes the project's `alerts.notify` command run for a run's events, which nothing else tells a person about. It is a comma-separated list of `gate` (a step waits at a gate), `failed` (a step failed, or its check did: `failed_check`; or a run was held by its [token budget](#token-budgets), `budget_spent`, or its [run timeout](#run-timeouts), `run_timed_out`), `stalled` (the sweep found a stall and recorded it; held while an [automatic restart](#automatic-restarts) is pending, and sent once if the restarts are given up) and `finished` (the run completed). Unknown names are refused by `config set`. Unset means none, so nothing new runs.

```console
$ workspace config set alerts.notify 'say "$WORKSPACE_ALERT_TEXT"'
$ workspace config set alerts.workflow gate,failed,stalled,finished
```

The alert is sent by the process that makes the change (the CLI, `workflow advance` or the sweep), after it gives up the run's lock, and needs no agent daemon. It is read from the change itself, so an event fires once for each episode: a gate when the step starts waiting (not on each `status`), a failure once for each step attempt (a failure whose `on_fail` retry is coming, straight away or after a `backoff:`, is not one: only a run that waits for a person is), a stall once for each attempt and cause (the sweep's marks), a finish once. A new attempt, or a gate asked for again after a reject, is a new episode. The settings are read each time, with no restart. The caller never waits for the command: it starts a detached supervisor process (its own process group), which runs the command, stops it with SIGTERM and then SIGKILL to its group after 10 seconds, and exits, so a hung command is stopped even when the process that changed the run has long gone. Each supervisor is bounded by that limit, so no cap on how many run is needed. A command that fails or is stopped is reported in `workflow-alerts.log` in the state directory (past 1 MiB it is renamed to `workflow-alerts.log.1`, replacing the older one, so at most two files of about that size are kept); a supervisor that can't be started is a line on the stderr of the process that changed the run. Neither fails or holds up the change. `config set alerts.workflow` warns when no `alerts.notify` is set for the workspace, since it sends nothing without one.

The command gets `WORKSPACE_ALERT=workflow`, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_EVENT` (`gate`, `failed`, `stalled` or `finished`), `WORKSPACE_ALERT_RUN_ID`, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_WORKSPACE`, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_STEP`, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_TEXT` and, when the run has one, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_REASON` (the reason code) and, for `stalled`, `WORKSPACE_ALERT_CAUSE`. A workflow alert has none of the pane variables (`WORKSPACE_ALERT_PANE`, `_PANE_ID`, `_KIND`, `_SECONDS`, `_MESSAGE`): a script that treats every `WORKSPACE_ALERT` but `waiting` as idle must check for `workflow` first. The values come from the run file, so they are escaped (control characters become `\n`, `\e` and so on) and each is cut to its first 200 characters before escaping (so an escaped value can be longer), and reach the command only as environment variables, never in its command line. The run's `next_action` is not passed: it is a suggestion for a person, and nothing runs it. The scheduled [sweep](#sweeping-stopped-runs) runs with the short `PATH` its plist records, so a notify command that the sweep may send (for a `stalled` alert) must be an absolute path, such as `/usr/local/bin/notify-me`, not a name found on your login `PATH`. A run stored before this setting works as it did.

Needs `workflow` 20 in `capabilities`.

### Sweeping stopped runs

`workflow sweep [RUN | --name WORKSPACE] [--dry-run] [--json]` is one pass over the runs still going (every workspace's, unless you name a run or a workspace). It is for the failures nothing else sees: a daemon that died, a pane that closed, an agent whose turn ended while no daemon was watching. It needs no agent daemon, so a scheduler can run it, and a run that another process is changing is skipped and looked at on the next pass.

You do not have to run it yourself. A workspace's agent daemon starts `workflow sweep --name WORKSPACE` for its own workspace on the first tick that finds a run going there, then at most every 5 minutes while one is. While a run with a `budget` of `action: interrupt` has a running step, the daemon also starts `workflow sweep --name WORKSPACE --budget-only` about once a minute: it looks only at that budget (no pane is read until the budget is spent) so a runaway step is pressed within about a minute. A budget-only pass reports a row only for a run it acted on or evaluated, and its `--json` document carries `"budget_only": true`; an empty `results` there does not mean a full sweep found the runs healthy. A sweep that cannot start does not stop the daemon from advancing runs. The scheduled entry below is still worth installing, since it also covers a workspace whose daemon is not running. A definition with no `stall` is decided after the global `workflows.default_stall` (10 minutes unless you set it; see [Definitions](#definitions)).

The sweep acts whenever the workspace's agent daemon is running, with or without the schedule: a step past its `on_timeout` is interrupted or failed, and a run past its `timeout:` with `timeout_action: interrupt` has its agent interrupted.

For a step that reads `running` (or, for the delivery row, waits on an undelivered line), the sweep takes the first row of this table that applies:

| It finds | It does | Writes (in the run's history) |
|----------|---------|-------------------------------|
| The run's pane is gone | Records it. The run keeps its checkout and locks until `workflow resume` or `workflow cancel`, unless an [automatic restart](#automatic-restarts) is scheduled | `step_stalled` cause `pane_gone`, then `infra_retry_scheduled` or `infra_retries_exhausted` |
| The workspace's agent daemon is not running | Starts it (once for each workspace in a pass) and does nothing more to that run in the pass, since a new daemon's reading is not evidence; the next pass reads the pane. It also starts one for a run that is checking, parked or waiting for a lock. A start that fails is tried again on the next pass. A workspace with no tmux session gets none (`workspace_stopped`, not a failure) | `step_stalled` cause `daemon_down`, then `step_recovered` action `daemon_respawned` (written whenever a start succeeds and it is not yet written, once for each attempt) |
| The step is held (it has a reason, such as `turn_ended_incomplete`, with or without `details.invalid`, or `turn_ended_background`), its line is still being delivered, or its pane waits for an answer | Nothing. A person decides a held step. One exception: a step past its `timeout` with `on_timeout: fail` that is held on `turn_ended_incomplete` is failed (`step_timed_out`, then `on_fail`), because otherwise the timeout is never enforced. A `turn_ended_background` hold has its own 90-minute limit and is never touched | none (the failure writes `step_stalled` cause `timed_out` and `step_recovered` action `failed`) |
| The failed step's `on_fail` retry waits out a `backoff` (reason `retry_backoff`) | Nothing before the time (outcome `nothing`, `reason` `backoff`, `cause` `retry_backoff`): this is not a stall, and `stall` and `timeout` do not apply. Once the time has passed it starts the retry, as the daemon's timer or a resume would (outcome `retried`). The retry types a line, so a dead agent daemon is started first (outcome `respawned`, `cause` `daemon_down`, no stall recorded) and the retry follows on the next pass; `--dry-run` only reports the respawn. Before the time, no daemon is needed. A resume, a cancel or a tick that got there first stands | `step_retry_started`, then the step's own events |
| The step waits on a line that was never delivered (reason `waiting_you`, kind `dispatch`, error `pane_busy`, `restart_in_progress` or `no_daemon` raised before a request was sent: the failures that prove the line never reached the pane), the run has no park and the definition's `redeliver` is above 0 | Waits out a backoff after the failed attempt ended (1 minute, then 5, then 15 for the third and later), then, with the daemon running (else it starts one and tries on the next pass) and the pane showing an idle agent (idle at least 5 seconds), types the line again exactly as `resume` would; the typing goes through the same checks as every step line (`pane_busy` if the screen holds a draft or a busy row). Nothing retries unless something runs the sweep, and the installed schedule runs it every 300 seconds, so a 1 minute backoff waits up to 5 minutes. The failed attempt is marked `redelivered` and does not count against `max_attempts`; a retry that fails too is retried again, up to `redeliver` times. When the retries run out the run stays as it was, waiting for a person (the inbox row shows it), and the sweep records `redelivery_exhausted` once. A gone pane, a missing checkout, an unreadable pack and every failure that does not prove the line was never typed (`not_delivered`, where a slow paste may have landed; `connection_failed` and `unreadable_reply`, where the daemon may have typed it; `prompt_unconfirmed`) are never retried; a person's `resume` starts the count again | `step_stalled` cause `undelivered` (`code`, `retry`, `of`), `step_recovered` action `redelivered`, then the step's own events; or `step_stalled` cause `redelivery_exhausted` |
| The pane shows a shell where the agent was | Records it, and schedules an [automatic restart](#automatic-restarts) when one applies | `step_stalled` cause `agent_gone`, then `infra_retry_scheduled` or `infra_retries_exhausted` |
| The agent's turn ended on an API error, and [API recovery](README.agentd.md#recovering-from-an-api-error) gave up for that failure or the failure is older than `workflows.turn_failed_after` (default 30m; `off` or `0` turns the time limit off) | Records the stall and presses and types nothing: the step cannot go on while the agent cannot work. A step past its `timeout` with `on_timeout: fail` is failed at once instead. Before either holds, the row has `reason` `turn_failed` and nothing is written. Details in [Where a run is stuck](#where-a-run-is-stuck) | `step_stalled` cause `turn_failed`, once for each attempt |
| The agent's turn is over, and the pane has been idle for the definition's `stall`, or the step is past its `timeout` with `on_timeout` `interrupt` or `fail`, or the sweep interrupted it earlier | Decides the step as `workflow resume` does when given the same proof (its files, `require:` proofs and check), so it passes, fails by `on_fail`, or is held; with `on_timeout: fail`, a decision that is a `turn_ended_incomplete` stop fails the step instead.
