The pane is read again just before the change and must still show the turn over and idle for at least 5 seconds | `step_stalled` cause `idle_turn_over` (`idle_for`), `step_recovered` action `evaluated`, then the step's own events |
| The agent is working and the step is past its `timeout` | `on_timeout: interrupt` presses the agent's interrupt keys once for the attempt; `fail` presses them and then fails the step (`step_timed_out`, which then follows `on_fail`); `warn`, the default, does nothing and writes nothing, as before. The press is claimed under the run's lock, made with the lock given up, and confirmed under the lock; it is made only if the pane, read again just before, still shows the same agent working and still belongs to the run, and a claim whose press never finished can be taken over after 60 seconds. If the press fails under `on_timeout: fail`, the step is not failed: the claim is given back, and each pass retries and exits non-zero until the keys go through | `step_stalled` cause `timed_out`, then `step_recovered` action `interrupted` or `failed` |
| The agent is working, the run's budget has `action: interrupt`, and its stored spend plus the live figure has reached the allowance | Presses the agent's interrupt keys once for the attempt, under its own cause and mark (`budget_interrupt`), and records the figures on the attempt as `budget_interrupt`. Never fails the step or cancels the run: the turn ends, the step is decided as usual, and the next attempt's start holds the run as `budget_spent`. A null live figure never presses. `workflow sweep --budget-only`, which the daemon runs once a minute, does only this | `step_stalled` cause `budget_spent` (`used`, `allowance`, `live`), then `step_recovered` action `interrupted` |
| The agent is working and the run has used its definition's `timeout:` with `timeout_action: interrupt` | Presses the agent's interrupt keys once for the attempt, claimed, pressed and confirmed as for a step timeout, but under its own cause and mark (`run_interrupt`), so a step's `on_timeout: interrupt` on the same attempt does not collide with it. When a step's own timeout with `interrupt` or `fail` is due too, that one presses and the run's is not pressed again. It never fails the step or cancels the run: the turn ends, the step is decided as usual (a turn over after the press is decided with cause `interrupted`), and the next attempt's start holds the run as `run_timed_out`. `timeout_action: hold` presses nothing. An attempt that starts past the deadline (an exempt redelivery, lock wait or parked resume) is pressed on the next pass with no grace. If the run's press came first, the step's own `on_timeout` press can still follow on the same attempt. A turn that ended on its own with the run's time used (a missed Stop) is decided on the next pass, for `hold` too, so the hold is reached | `step_stalled` cause `run_timed_out` (`elapsed` is the machine time used, `timeout` the limit), then `step_recovered` action `interrupted` |


Before it takes a row, the sweep closes any stall it recorded earlier in the same attempt whose cause has cleared (see [Active time](#active-time)): the pane is alive again (`step_recovered` action `pane_back`), the pane's row is present and no longer shows a shell (`agent_back`), or the row is present with its `turn_failed` flag cleared (`turn_recovered`). `pane_gone` closes as soon as the bound pane is alive again, with no daemon needed. `agent_gone` and `turn_failed` need the daemon's pane row: a missing row, a down daemon, a shell still shown or a flag still set closes nothing. `--dry-run` writes nothing. The pass then goes on as usual, so the same pass can still time the step out. The step's, run's and round's timeouts, `on_timeout` and `timeout_action` all read active time, so none of them fires for time the machine was off or a stall was open. The same holds for the review rows below and for the check that a pressed agent answered: downtime does not count against its time to answer. The `stall` idle time is the pane's own idle reading and is not cut.
#### Automatic restarts

When a step's agent exited or its pane is gone, the step starts again on its own in a new Claude Code pane, after 60 seconds, then 300 and 900. Turn this off with `workspace config set workflows.infra_retry.max 0`. When the restarts run out, the run waits for you: `workflow resume RUN` (`--restart-step` starts the step again in a new attempt) or `workflow cancel RUN`.

The details follow. The first time the sweep records a step's `pane_gone` or `agent_gone` stall for an attempt, it schedules a restart in the same change, with the `infra_retry_scheduled` event (`step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `n`, `max`, `due`). `workflows.infra_retry.max` (default `2`; `0` turns it off) caps the restarts a step gets; the waits are 60 seconds, 300 seconds, then 900 seconds, counted from when the stall was recorded. The sweep schedules only when the workspace's tmux session is running; a dead session is never restarted. The agent daemon's tick (`workflow advance`) carries the restart out once it is due, so it needs a daemon running; a run nothing advances waits.

What it covers: a step's `agent_gone`, and a step's `pane_gone` in a live session. It does not cover `turn_failed` (API recovery has its own rules), a review round, the `pane_gone` of a line that was never delivered (redelivery already retried that), a dead session, or a stall recorded before a retry could be scheduled (the session was down then, `max` was 0, or the stall predates this feature): those are never retried, and the run waits for a person as before.

The restart splits a new Claude Code pane and restarts the step in it, as `workflow resume --restart-step` would, with the same fresh conversation and brief. It never types into the old pane or a shell the agent left there, and leaves that pane open. Only Claude Code agents restart; any other agent kind gives up with `agent_kind_unsupported`. A restart counts toward `max_attempts` and the run's budget, so those still cap a run; it does not go through `on_fail`. One due retry is claimed under the run's lock before any pane is made, so concurrent `workflow advance` calls start one pane, not several. Pausing or parking a run, or a line still being typed, holds a pending restart: it is not claimed while the run is paused, and runs at its due time once the run goes on (at once if the time passed), provided the stall is still open.

The count of restarts a step has used resets when the step passes, and when a person resumes the run (`workflow resume`, with or without `--restart-step`), so a person acting on a run gives it a fresh set. A retry lives only as long as its stall: when the pane or agent comes back, a person resumes and keeps the attempt, or the attempt is decided or interrupted, the retry ends (`infra_retry_ended`).

While a restart is pending the `stalled` alert is held, and `workflow status --needs-me` and the inbox's `workflow_stalled` row leave the run out, since nothing is waiting on a person. When the restarts are given up, the run stays as it was, stalled and resumable, and the `stalled` alert fires once. A stall that recovers while its restart is pending sends no `stalled` alert.

| Event (in the run's history) | Written when |
|---|---|
| `infra_retry_scheduled` | the sweep recorded the stall and scheduled the restart (`step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `n`, `max`, `due`) |
| `infra_retry_claimed` | a `workflow advance` took the due restart (same keys; `taken_over: true` when it took over a claim that was older than 180 seconds or whose process had died) |
| `infra_retry_released` | the restart found the agent working again and gave the claim back, once for each retry; `due` moves one wait on (`step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `n`, `due`) |
| `infra_retry_ended` | the stall ended without a restart (`step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `n`, `reason`: `pane_back`, `agent_back`, `resumed`, `evaluated`, `interrupted`). The restart's own close of the stalled attempt does not write it |
| `infra_retries_exhausted` | the restart will not happen (`step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `n`, `max`, `reason`); written once for each attempt |
| `run_resumed` with `infra_retry: N` | the restart ran, as restart `N`; it also has `restart_step: true` |

`reason` of `infra_retries_exhausted`: `retries_spent` (the step used all `max`), `no_session` (the tmux session is gone), `agent_kind_unsupported`, `spawn_failed` (no new pane could be made), `agent_not_ready`, `agent_waiting_sign_in`, `agent_waiting_trust`, `agent_waiting_approval` (the new agent never became ready), `claim_lost` (a restart began and never finished), `still_working` (the agent was working again a second time), `restart_refused` and `restart_error`, or the error code the restart was refused with: `attempts_spent`, `step_attempts_spent`, `budget_spent`, `run_timed_out`, `gate_waiting`, `run_paused`. `workflow status` shows each in words.

For a step that reads `reviewing` (a review round), the sweep reads the round and its review pane, never the author's, and takes the first row that applies (needs `workflow` 35 in `capabilities`):

| It finds | It does | Writes (in the run's history) |
|----------|---------|-------------------------------|
| The round stopped for a person (`review_incomplete`, `review_stale`, `review_rejected`, or any reason but `waiting_lock`) | Nothing, and starts no daemon for it (outcome `nothing`, `reason` `held`) | nothing |
| The workspace's agent daemon is not running | Starts it, as for a running step | as for a running step |
| The round's end was being judged and nothing reported back | Hands it to the engine's tick, which judges it once more (outcome `judged_again`, `cause` `lost_round`) | the round's own events |
| The round waits for the lock its `review.uses` names | Nothing (`reason` `waiting_lock`): the daemon's timer asks for the lock again | nothing |
| The round's delivery failed, or lapsed with its delivering process gone | Before the failure's backoff, nothing (`reason` `backoff`, `cause` `round_undelivered`); then hands it to the engine's tick, which delivers it again (outcome `redelivered`, `action` `redeliver`) or, its `redeliver` spent, stops it `review_incomplete` (outcome `stopped`, `action` `stop`) | `review_started` (`again`, `redelivery`), or the stop |
| The round's delivery failed or lapsed after its line may have been typed (see [An unchanged revision and missing verdicts](#an-unchanged-revision-and-missing-verdicts)) | Read in its review pane as a delivered round is (the rows below); once its short wait is over, handed to the engine's tick (outcome `evaluated`, `cause` `round_maybe_typed`, `action` `evaluate`), which reads its verdicts unless the review pane shows a reviewer at work or waiting for an answer (outcome `nothing`, `reason` `reviewing`); a reviewer at work past a time limit is pressed first as in a delivered round (the rows below), and the daemon's timer stops such a round `review_incomplete` only once twice `review.timeout` has passed from the round's start with no press; one pressed whose reviewer still works the longer of the definition's `stall` and `review.timeout` after the newest press is judged by the timer on its verdicts, no reviewer run again, with `step_stalled` cause `interrupt_unanswered` | the round's own events |
| The round is still being delivered | Nothing (`reason` `delivering`) | nothing |
| tmux can't say what became of the review pane (it can't be reached or read within 5 seconds, or the round recorded no tmux server, so a reused pane id can't be ruled out) | Decides nothing on the pane being gone or its server restarted, and goes on to the rows below, which read the agent daemon's pane row | nothing |
| The review pane is gone (the session's server is the one the round recorded and lists the session's panes without it) | Reads the round's verdicts as the review turn's end would (outcome `evaluated`, `cause` `pane_gone`, `action` `evaluate`): verdicts written before the pane closed count, and a round they leave short stops `review_incomplete` | `step_stalled` cause `pane_gone` with `round` and `pane`, then the round's own events |
| The tmux server the review pane was on was restarted (the round's `review.server_pid` differs from the server that has the pane id now, or from the session's server when the pane is gone) | Delivers the round again in a new window (outcome `redelivered`, `cause` `server_restarted`, `action` `redeliver`), counted against `redeliver`; once that is spent, stops it `review_incomplete` (outcome `stopped`) | `step_stalled` cause `server_restarted`, then `review_started` |
| The review pane shows a shell where its agent was (read again just before the change) | As for a gone pane (`cause` `agent_gone`) | `step_stalled` cause `agent_gone`, then the round's own events |
| The review pane's turn ended on an API error and has stayed so past `workflows.turn_failed_after` (and the delivery grace) | Decides the round on the verdicts already written (outcome `evaluated`, `cause` `turn_failed`, `action` `evaluate`), as for a gone pane: a round they leave short stops `review_incomplete` with its window kept. Nothing is typed into a review pane, and review panes get no API recovery, so with the limit `off` such a pane is never decided this way and the round waits for a person. The review time limits above are decided first | `step_stalled` cause `turn_failed` with `round`, `pane` and `since`, then the round's own events |
| The review pane waits for an answer | Nothing (`reason` `prompt`) | nothing |
| The review turn is over (read again just before the change) after the sweep interrupted the review pane for a time limit (the rows below) | Decides the round at once as the review turn's end does (outcome `evaluated`, `cause` `interrupted`, `action` `evaluate`): verdicts written by then count; a round they leave short stops `review_incomplete` with `details.problem` saying which limit was passed, runs no reviewer again, and keeps its window for the person (a `resume` starts a fresh round and closes it) | `step_stalled` cause `interrupted` with `round`, `pane` and `idle_for`, then the round's own events |
| The review turn is over (read again just before the change) and the pane has been idle for the definition's `stall`, or the round is past a time limit below | Decides the round as the review turn's end does (outcome `evaluated`, `cause` `idle_turn_over`, `action` `evaluate`) | `step_stalled` cause `idle_turn_over` with `round`, `pane` and `idle_for`, then the round's own events |
| The review pane's agent is working and the round is past its `review.timeout` (default 30m), counted from the round's `started_at` (a delivery again or a rerun of missing reviewers starts it again; the same reading as `workflow status`'s `timed_out`) | Presses the agent's interrupt keys in the review pane, never the author's (outcome `interrupted`, `cause` `review_timed_out`, `action` `interrupt`), claimed under the run's lock, pressed with it given up and confirmed under it, as for a step timeout, once for that delivery of the round. The pane is read again just before the press and must still show the same agent working and still be bound to the run; a claim whose press never finished can be taken over after 60 seconds. A pane that is not working is `nothing` (`reason` `not_working`); once pressed, `nothing` (`reason` `already_interrupted`) until the turn ends, which the row above decides. The press also needs the pane bound to the run as its reviewer, so an author's pane that took a reused pane id is never pressed. A pane still at work the longer of the definition's `stall` and `review.timeout` after the newest press of every due cause is decided on the verdicts written by then, no reviewer run again, and stops `review_incomplete` with its window kept when they fall short (outcome `stopped`, or `evaluated` when they decide it or the revision is read again first, `cause` `interrupt_unanswered`, `action` `evaluate`, also under `--dry-run`; `step_stalled` with `waited`); the agent daemon's timer (the engine's tick) decides such a round the same way when no sweep runs again, reading no pane. A sweep that dies after pressing and before confirming leaves only its pending claim: after 60 seconds the round reads as not interrupted, so a later sweep may press again and a turn that ends meanwhile may run missing reviewers again | `step_stalled` cause `review_timed_out` with `round`, `pane`, `elapsed` and `timeout`, then `step_recovered` action `interrupted` with `round` and `cause` |
| The review pane's agent is working and the run has used its `timeout:` with `timeout_action: interrupt` | As for `review.timeout`, under the cause `run_timed_out` and its own mark, so a round past both limits is pressed once for each (the round's own first). The round's verdicts decide it once the turn ends; it never cancels the run: a round that passes or rejects goes on, and the next attempt or round start holds the run as `run_timed_out`; one left short stops `review_incomplete` | `step_stalled` cause `run_timed_out` with `round` (`elapsed` is the machine time used, `timeout` the limit), then `step_recovered` action `interrupted` |

The daemon's pane row does not say whether the review turn's Stop listed background tasks, so a reviewer that left a sub-agent running past `stall` is decided on the verdicts it has written by then.

A change is made only while the run is still on that step, attempt and round, delivered, undecided and in the pane the sweep read. Then the sweep closes the review windows no round uses (see [A review round's window](#a-review-rounds-window); a window of a run this store does not have is `nothing` with `reason` `unconfirmed`, never closed), one result each with the run's `workspace` (the session when the run is unknown), `run_id`, `step`, a null `attempt`, `cause` `orphan_review_window` and `action` `close_window`: outcome `closed`, `dry_run` under `--dry-run`, `failed` (`reason` `close_failed`) when tmux could not close it, or `nothing` (`refused`) from the run's own pane (a window whose run is unknown is judged by its run id). With no RUN or `--name` the sweep looks at every session of the default tmux server; a window whose run's workspace is on its own socket is left.

A run with a live [park](#resuming-from-the-runs-own-pane), a step whose check is running, a run waiting at a gate or for a lock, and a run whose step is not being worked on are left alone. A step with no `stall` and an `on_timeout` of `warn` is never decided for being idle; set `stall` (such as `20m`), or `on_timeout: interrupt`. A step the agent finished while the daemon was down passes on the next sweep once it has been idle that long, and no sooner than 30 seconds after the attempt began.

Every change goes through the run's lock, and is made only if the run is still on the step and attempt the sweep read, so a `resume`, a turn's end or a cancel that got there first stands, and the sweep reports `moved_on`. A cause is recorded once for each attempt: a second sweep writes no event and presses no key again, and a new attempt starts afresh. The marks are the run file's `sweep` key (`step`, `attempt`, `stalled`, `recovered`, `interrupt` for a step's press and `run_interrupt` for the run timeout's); a run stored before the sweep has none. A review round keeps its own marks in its `review.sweep` (`interrupt` for its `review.timeout`, `run_interrupt` for the run's, `stalled`, `recovered`), so an author's press on the reviewed attempt never stands for the round's; a delivery of the round again starts with none. The sweep leaves a run alone when it runs in the run's own pane or under it, as `resume` is refused there. A small window remains between reading the pane and taking the lock: a turn that begins in it is decided as if it had ended, which the second reading, the idle floor and the attempt check keep to a moment.

With `--json`, the document also has `next_actions`: what a person can run about the rows the pass left alone (see [Next actions](#next-actions)). It is always present and is empty when nothing needs a person.

A scheduler runs the sweep with a short `PATH`, which must include a Ruby new enough for workspace (`/usr/bin/ruby` can't load the code) and `tmux`; a missing program is a `failed` result with reason `tool_unavailable` that names it. A run file that can't be read is a `failed` result with reason `unreadable_run`.

`--dry-run` reads everything and changes nothing; its rows have outcome `dry_run` and say the `action` they would take. Text output prints a line for each run it acted on or noted, then a count. With `--json` it prints the [action document](README.json.md) with one result for each run looked at: `workspace`, `outcome`, `reason`, `message`, `run_id`, `step`, `attempt`, `cause`, `action`. Outcomes: `nothing` (`reason`: `healthy`, `idle`, `held`, `paused` (a person paused the run: nothing is read, started or written for it, and its next action is `workflow unpause`), `parked`, `checking`, `reviewing`, `not_running`, `delivering`, `prompt`, `timed_out`, `already_recorded`, `already_interrupted`, `no_interrupt_key`, `refused`, `busy`, `moved_on`, `unknown`, `finished`, `backoff`, `pane_busy`, `not_delivered`, `waiting_lock`, `retry_blocked`, `unconfirmed`), `recorded`, `evaluated`, `interrupted`, `timed_out`, `respawned`, `redelivered`, `retried` (an `on_fail` retry started after its backoff; `cause` `retry_backoff`, `action` `retry`), `stopped` (a review round stopped for a person, `review_incomplete`), `closed` (a left-over review window), `judged_again` (a lost round's end), `dry_run` and `failed` (`daemon_not_started`, `interrupt_failed`, `tool_unavailable`, `unreadable_run`, `close_failed`, or the code of an error reading the run). Other reasons under `nothing`: `workspace_stopped`, `pane_changed`, `not_working`, `no_attempt`. A `failed` result is a daemon that could not be started, an interrupt that failed, a missing program or an unreadable run file; a stalled run found and recorded is a finding, not a failure. The exit status follows the action document: 0 when no result failed, 3 when some did, 1 when all did. A usage error or an unknown RUN (`unknown_run`) prints the error envelope and exits 1, so a script tells it from a failed action by the document's shape (`ok: false` and `code`, against `action` and `results`), not by the exit code. Needs `workflow` 15 in `capabilities`.

#### Running the sweep on a schedule

`workflow sweep --install` writes a per-user LaunchAgent, `~/Library/LaunchAgents/com.workspace.workflow-sweep.plist`, and loads it with `launchctl bootstrap gui/<uid>`. launchd then runs `workflow sweep` every 5 minutes (`StartInterval` 300; it does not run at load). It is never done for you: not by `doctor --fix`, not by any other command, because it changes your login session.

- The plist records absolute paths read at install: the Ruby that ran `--install`, the `workspace` program (the Homebrew `opt` keg's `libexec/bin/workspace`, else the checkout's `bin/workspace`, and only a file with a Ruby shebang; a linked worktree is refused with `sweep_source_worktree`) and a `PATH` of Ruby's directory, tmux's directory and the system ones. launchd's own `PATH` finds the system Ruby (2.6), which cannot load this code. Homebrew `Cellar` paths are rewritten to the stable `opt` ones when those exist. After a Ruby or install move, run `--install` again: it rewrites the plist and reloads it.
- stdout and stderr go to `~/.local/state/workspace/sweep.log` (under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/workspace` when that is set; `Config#sweep_log_path`); `--install` creates its directory. The sweep trims the log to its last 256 KiB (cut forward to a whole line, rewritten in place so the entry's open file keeps working) when it starts if it is over 1 MiB, a `--dry-run` pass too. Each pass but a dry run also writes `~/.local/state/workspace/.workflows/sweep-last.json` (`Config#workflow_sweep_last_path`): `last` is the latest pass of any scope and `last_full` the latest unfiltered one, each `{at, scope, acted, errors}` where `scope` is `"all"`, `{"workspace": NAME}` or `{"run": ID}`, `acted` counts rows whose outcome is not `nothing`, and `errors` lists failed rows' `run_id`, `reason` and `message`. A record that can't be written does not fail the pass. A daemon that could not be started (`daemon_not_started`) or a Ruby crash shows there. Its findings that are events are also in each run's events (`workflow events`). An entry installed before the log existed still sends output to `/dev/null` and reads as out of date (`up_to_date` false) until `workspace workflow sweep --install` runs again. To check the schedule, run `workflow sweep --installed` (`loaded`, `up_to_date`, `stale`) and a manual `workflow sweep --dry-run`. `workspace doctor` reads it and warns when the last full pass is missing, unreadable, or older than twice the 5-minute interval (10 minutes), and when the installed plist is out of date (re-run `workflow sweep --install`).
- Running `--install` again changes nothing when the plist is as it would be written and loaded (`unchanged`); it loads a plist that was written but not loaded (`loaded`), and rewrites one that differs (`updated`). `launchctl` has to answer: when it can't run, times out or fails on the status check (only the text "Could not find service" means not loaded), nothing is changed and the error is `sweep_launchctl_failed`. A `launchctl` failure after the plist was written is `sweep_launchctl_failed` with `details.plist_written` true; run the command again to finish. A plist at that path that this command did not write (it carries a marker comment) is never touched: `sweep_entry_foreign`.
- `--uninstall` boots the entry out and deletes the plist (`removed`; `unchanged` when nothing was there). A failed boot-out keeps the plist so a retry can finish. If the plist is gone but launchd still has the job, `--uninstall` unloads it.
- `--installed` reads and changes nothing: `installed`, `ours`, `loaded` (true, false, or null when `launchctl` could not answer: treat null as unknown, not false; the exit status stays 0), `up_to_date` (the plist matches what `--install` would write now), `stale` (recorded paths that no longer exist), `interval`, `ruby` and `program`. `--dry-run` with `--install` or `--uninstall` says what would be done (`outcome` `dry_run`, `action`); with `--installed` it is refused (a usage error), since that reads only. With the plist missing but the job still loaded, `--installed` says so (`installed` false, `loaded` true).
- The `--json` action documents are named `workflow sweep --install`, `workflow sweep --uninstall` and `workflow sweep --installed`; each has one result with `outcome`, `message`, `path` and `label`. The scheduled sweep keeps its exit status: 0, 3 when some results failed, 1 when all did.
- A `workflow run` or `workflow start` (also `go`) of a definition with a `timeout:` (the run's or any step's) prints one `Warning:` line on stderr when the schedule is not installed (read through `--installed`'s state; a schedule that cannot be read says nothing). The warning says the timeout is then enforced only while the workspace's agent daemon is running. It changes nothing and stdout, `--json` included, is unaffected. `workspace doctor` has a matching `workflow-sweep` row.

Nothing retries an undelivered line unless something runs `workflow sweep`: a person, the installed schedule, or the workspace's agent daemon, each at an interval of 300 seconds.

Needs `workflow` 16 in `capabilities` for the schedule and 17 for the retry of an undelivered line.

### Recovering after a reboot

A reboot or a tmux restart takes the panes of every going run. Run `workspace workflow recover --dry-run` to see what it would do, then `workspace workflow recover`. It takes no arguments and covers every going run in every workspace. Needs `workflow` 51 in `capabilities`.

It goes through the runs oldest first. A run is restarted, restored and woken, waiting on you, or handled elsewhere.

- **Restarted.** The step is mid-turn (`running` with an open attempt) and its session is down or its pane is gone. The workspace is restored, the command waits for the run's own pane to come back and be ready, and the step starts again as `workflow resume RUN --restart-step` does (see [Restarting a crashed step](#restarting-a-crashed-step)). The restart is a new attempt with a fresh agent conversation; the earlier conversation is not continued. Changes already in the checkout stay.
- **Restored and woken.** The step is between turns (reviewing, checking, waiting out a retry backoff, or due a redelivery) in a workspace with no session. The workspace is restored and the run gets one tick, which does whatever is due.
- **Waiting on you.** A paused run, a run waiting at a gate, a parked run, and a run held for any other reason the sweep leaves to a person (a retry blocked, a spent budget or timeout, a review round held; see [Where a run is stuck](#where-a-run-is-stuck)). Nothing is restored for its sake. A parked run in a stopped workspace needs `workspace restore WORKSPACE` by hand.
- **Handled elsewhere.** A run whose session is up and whose pane is alive, or whose step is between turns, which the agent daemon and `workflow sweep` reach; and a step the daemon is already restarting on its own (see [Automatic restarts](#automatic-restarts)). A run whose workspace is removed is also left alone, whatever its state (`skipped/workspace_removed`).

Workspaces are handled one at a time, so a failed restore stops only that workspace's runs, not the others.

Each run gets one row. Its `outcome` is one of:

| Outcome | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `restarted` | The step started again as a new attempt |
| `restored` | The workspace is back and the run was ticked (`reason` `woken`, `nothing_due`, or `backoff` with its `due` time) |
| `skipped` | Nothing was needed or a person holds the run; `reason` says which, for example `workspace_removed`, `nothing_to_restart`, `pane_alive`, `infra_retry_pending`, `moved_on`, `unknown_liveness`, or a held reason such as `paused` |
| `refused` | The run stays stopped on purpose, for example `limit_reached`, or a refusal of the restart such as `attempts_spent`, `budget_spent` or `run_timed_out` |
| `failed` | The run stays stopped because something broke, for example `restore_failed`, `no_pane`, `pane_not_ready`, `step_still_working`, or `unreadable_run` for a run file that can't be read or isn't shaped as a run |
| `would_restart`, `would_restore` | What a `--dry-run` would do |

What to do with a row:

| Row | What to do |
| --- | --- |
| `refused` or `failed` that names a command | Run it |
| `refused` or `failed` with no command (an engine refusal, `restart_not_closed`, a failed wake) | `workspace workflow status RUN`, which the text output points at |
| `skipped` because the run waits for you | `workspace workflow status --needs-me`, then approve, resume or unpause as it says |
| `failed` with `unreadable_run` | The run file is damaged: the row's `run_id` names it, and a file that does not parse is also listed in `unreadable_files`. No `workflow` command repairs it: inspect it, then fix or delete it by hand |
| `skipped` with `unknown_liveness` | tmux did not answer for that workspace (or it uses its own socket). Check `tmux ls`, then run `workspace workflow recover` again |

A run is restarted at most `workflows.recover.max_per_run` times (default 2, `0` turns it off; see [config](README.config.md)). The count is kept on the run as `reboot_recoveries` and is never reset, so a run that keeps dying is left for you with `refused/limit_reached`. The manual commands are `workspace restore WORKSPACE` and `workspace workflow resume RUN --restart-step`. Each restart writes the event `reboot_recovered` (`workspace`, `outcome`, `count`) beside the usual `step_interrupted` and `run_resumed`.

The exit status is 0 when no row failed or was refused, 3 when some did, and 1 when all did. A dry run follows the same rule, and a run file that can't be read counts as a failed row.

Restoring a workspace in a live session also brings back every recorded agent pane that is gone, not only the run's, so look at the dry run before you run it. Restore resumes only Claude Code panes; a Codex pane is skipped (`other_agent`), so recover cannot restart a run whose pane runs Codex, and its row is `failed`. Only Claude Code has been tested by hand.

### Retrying a failed step

```yaml
verify:
  status: {command: test}
  on_fail: {goto: implement, max: 3, context: continue, log_tail: 40, backoff: 2m}
```

**`log_tail: N`.** The retry's instructions end the failure sentence with a block: the last `N` lines of the failed attempt's check log, labelled as program output that may hold text someone else wrote and is to be treated as data, inside a code fence. The fence is made longer than any run of backticks in the log, so the log cannot close it; control characters (escapes, carriage returns, bidi marks) are shown as `\e`, `\r`, `\uXXXX`; invalid UTF-8 is replaced; a line is cut at 400 characters; the block holds at most 8,192 bytes, dropping its earliest lines first and saying so; and only the last 64 KiB of the file is read. A log that is missing, empty, a link, not a regular file or unreadable gives no block and no error. Only a failure with a check log has one: a `status:` check (including a timeout) and a `require: command` proof. A failure from the agent's own `step done --status fail`, a guard, another `require:` proof or `step_timed_out` has no log, so the retry gets the failure sentence alone. The block is stored with the retry in the run file, read when the step failed, not when the retry starts.

**`backoff: DURATION`.** The retry does not start until the delay has passed. The loop is counted when the step fails (`loops` rises, `step_looped` is written with `backoff` in seconds), so the wait does not buy a free attempt. While it waits the run is `waiting`, the failed step stays `failed`, and the run holds no attempt, no pane binding and no lock (its locks are given up when the step fails and taken again by the retry, queuing like any step). `workflow status` shows the reason `retry_backoff` (`details.until`, `details.to`, `details.seconds`) with the actions `workflow resume` and `workflow cancel`; the run file's `backoff` holds the same time. Three things start the retry once the time has passed, whichever runs first and only once: the agent daemon's timer (the same 15-second tick that retries a lock), `workflow sweep` (outcome `retried`, `cause` `retry_backoff`; with the agent daemon dead it starts the daemon first and retries on the next pass), and a person's `workflow resume`, which skips the rest of the wait and starts the retry now. `resume --from STEP` drops the wait and starts that step without the failure text; `cancel` and a workspace `kill` end the run and drop the wait. `approve` and `reject` apply to a gate and refuse a run in a wait (`gate_not_waiting`). The sweep treats a step waiting out a backoff as not stalled: it records nothing and leaves `stall` and `timeout` alone. The retry's own attempt starts as `on_fail` always starts one, so a pane that is busy at that moment makes it `not_delivered`, which `redeliver` then retries. The pane stays the run's during the wait: another run cannot start in it (`pane_has_run`). When the wait starts the run records which pane it was in (`backoff.pane` and `backoff.identity`: the session and window slot of the binding, the facts a binding's stale check compares). The wait holds the pane only while tmux still has it as that pane: a pane that is gone, or whose id a tmux restart gave to another pane, holds nothing. When the retry comes due and the pane is no longer the run's (another run is bound to it, tmux no longer has it, or it is another pane under the same id; a stored wait with no identity can't be shown to be the same pane and is treated alike) nothing is typed and the retry is not spent: the run keeps its wait, marked `backoff.blocked` (`pane_has_run`, `pane_gone` or `pane_changed`) so a tick and the sweep leave it, and reads `waiting` with the reason `waiting_you` (kind `dispatch`, `details.error` the same code) or, for a gone pane, `pane_gone`. `workflow status` offers `workflow resume` with the `--pane` it needs and `workflow cancel`; `workflow resume --pane PANE` names another agent pane and starts the retry (a `step_retry_blocked` event records the hold). The sweep leaves a held wait alone: the pass that finds the retry held, and each later pass, gives one `nothing` result with `reason` `retry_blocked` (no stall is recorded, and it is not a failure and does not change the exit status, since only a person can name the pane). Text output prints it on every pass (`left alone (retry_blocked)`) and counts it as reported, because a person must act. A `workflow resume` of a held wait is decided before anything is written, so a held resume (even with `--note` or a `--pane` that is gone) changes nothing, writes no `run_resumed` or repeated `step_retry_blocked` event, and a hold that has not changed (same code, same pane) keeps its reason as it was. A bare `workflow resume` of a held wait starts nothing: the row's outcome is `unchanged` ("Nothing was done: ...", exit status 0) and the run stays waiting; `--pane` names the proof, so it skips the check that the pane is the one the run was in (it must still exist and be free). A `pane_changed` hold may be a false alarm (closing a pane beside it renumbers its slot), which `--pane` with the same id clears. A tmux that can't be run reads as not the same pane. A stored wait whose `to` is not a step name is ignored, and a resume runs the failed step as it stands. The log tail is read without blocking and never from a link, FIFO or other non-regular file; control, bidi, zero-width and tag characters (U+E0000 to U+E007F), variation selectors and annotation marks in it are shown as `\uXXXX`. A run stored before these keys has neither key in its definition and retries at once with no log, as before.

### Reading a run's files

`workflow files RUN` lists the files a run keeps, going or finished, from the run's own record: for each attempt its prompt (`prompt`), when a check ran its log (`check_log`), when a `command` proof ran its log (`proof_log`), and for each step each file it `produces`. It reads file metadata only and writes nothing. An unknown run is refused with `unknown_run`.

A file that is not there is listed with `exists` false and `bytes` null. A symlink is listed with `exists` true, `bytes` null and `link` true: it is never followed, so its target's size is never shown. A path that is not inside the run's artifacts directory (after resolving `..`, or through a linked directory) is left out. A run recorded before artifacts directories were kept uses `<checkout>/.workflow/<RUN>`; a run with neither answers `artifacts_dir` null and no files.

### Reading a definition's file

`workflow show ID --source` answers the file itself, for an editor: the text exactly as stored, the `sha256` of its raw bytes and every problem the checks find. It works on a preset and on a global file that does not pass the checks (exit 0, where `show ID` fails with `invalid_workflow`), so a broken file can be loaded, fixed and saved. Without `--json` it prints the text alone and writes the problems to stderr. `--source` needs an ID.

### The `rpiv` preset

`rpiv` is a workflow that ships (with [`rpiv-auto-approve`](#the-rpiv-auto-approve-preset), [`rpiv-auto-approve-v2`](#the-rpiv-auto-approve-v2-preset), [`pr-feedback`](#the-pr-feedback-preset), [`investigate`](#the-investigate-preset) and [`explore`](#the-explore-preset)): research (`research.md`), plan (the workspace plan, `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` in the notes directory, in the `implementation-plan` syntax, plus `plan.md` for the gate), which is only done once the plan file exists and has a task (its `status:` check reads `plan status --json`), implement (`implement.md`) and verify (`verify.md`). Verify holds `test-db`, so runs in worktrees of one repository verify one at a time; it is checked with the project's `commands.test`, and a failure goes back to implement up to three times in the same conversation. Its `max_attempts` is 10, which leaves room for those three trips and one rejected plan. `implement` requires `commits: 1` and `plan_status: done`. The agent sets the plan's `status: done` last, and the commit must be made during that attempt, so a rerun (a pass back from `verify`, or a `resume`) has to make a new commit for the fix. The prompt says never to amend or rebase, which makes the proof unproven (`history_rewritten`), and to use `git commit --allow-empty -m '<why>'` when no code change is needed. `verify` requires `command: lint` with `optional: true`, so it passes only when the tests and, if the project sets `commands.lint`, the lint command both exit 0 (a project without one skips that proof, recorded under `proofs`). A project with no `commands.test` is refused before anything starts (code `workflow_command_unset`):

`research` and `plan` are `guard: unchanged`, so `rpiv` needs a clean checkout to start: a dirty tracked file, a stopped git operation or a branch with no commit is refused as `dirty_worktree` before anything is made, and a research or plan step that commits or edits a tracked file fails with the guard code (`head_moved`, `tracked_changed`). The plan and the artifacts are untracked, so writing them is fine. A run that has already started keeps the definition it started with, so runs begun before this guard behave as they did. `workflow run rpiv` (its tree defaults to the workspace's worktree) is refused on uncommitted tracked changes the same way, not only `workflow start`. A project that tracks `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` inside the repository fails `plan` with `tracked_changed`; clean the checkout and `workflow resume`. `workflow resume --from research` or `--from plan` after `implement` has committed stops with `head_moved`.

```sh
workspace config set commands.test "bundle exec rspec"
workspace workflow run rpiv --input task="PROJ-101 invoice PDF export" --dry-run
workspace workflow run rpiv --input task="PROJ-101 invoice PDF export"
```

### The `rpiv-auto-approve-v2` preset

`rpiv-auto-approve-v2` is the recommended preset for a run no person watches. Needs `workflow` 40 in `capabilities`.

Which to pick:

- `rpiv`: a person approves the plan at a gate.
- `rpiv-auto-approve-v2`: nobody watches; reviewers the engine runs approve the plan.
- `rpiv-auto-approve`: only for definitions that already extend it. It stays as it is, so they keep working.

It extends `rpiv` and replaces the `plan` step with one that has no gate and a `review:` of three built-in reviewers (`supportive`, `skeptical`, `implementer`): two of three must approve, the skeptic among them, for up to six rounds. It has four steps (`research`, `plan`, `implement`, `verify`), `max_attempts: 17` (the plan step 8), `timeout: 8h` and a 40,000,000-token budget. These figures are estimates, set before there were runs; replace them with `workflow stats` once there are.

How it differs from [`rpiv-auto-approve`](#the-rpiv-auto-approve-preset):

- The engine runs the review. There are no reviewer steps and no tally step: the old preset's four extra steps and its hand-counted `max_attempts: 68` are gone, and the engine counts the approvals.
- The three reviewers run in one review window of their own, not in the author's pane, as parallel sub-agents of one review conversation. Each writes a verdict file the engine proves against the agent daemon's log. See [Verdict files](#verdict-files).
- A rejected round sends the plan step back as a new attempt with every reviewer's write-up in its instructions, then reviews it again. A retry that changes nothing the review reads stops the run as `review_no_change` instead of spending a round.
- The run is capped: per-step `max_attempts`, a run `timeout` and a token `budget`.

What you see while it runs:

- `workflow status` shows the plan step as `reviewing`, with `current.review` holding the round, `max_rounds`, the approvals so far, the reviewers still pending and the review window's pane.
- Each round that ends leaves a `summary.md` in `<artifacts>/reviews/plan.<attempt>.r<round>/` and one `review_request` inbox item for the run and step (dedupe key `review:RUN:plan`), which later rounds update. The item says what the run does next. The preset has no gate, so nothing waits for your approval; the summary is a record. See [A gate after a review](#a-gate-after-a-review).
- The review window stays open across rejected rounds. See [A review round's window](#a-review-rounds-window) for `renumber-windows` and iTerm native windows.

When it stops for a person:

| Reason | Meaning | What to do |
|--------|---------|------------|
| `review_rejected`, `details.limit` `run` or `step` | The last round rejected the plan and the run (`run`) or the plan step (`step`) spent its `max_attempts` | Read the reviewers' write-ups, then `workflow resume RUN --extra-attempts N`, or `workflow cancel RUN` |
| `review_rejected`, `details.limit` `rounds` | The last round rejected the plan and the step spent its six rounds | Read the write-ups, then `workflow resume RUN --extra-rounds N`, or `workflow cancel RUN` |
| `review_rounds_spent` | The plan reached a review with every round spent | `workflow resume RUN --extra-rounds N` |
| `review_no_change` | The retry changed nothing the review reads; the round was not spent | `workflow resume RUN` reviews that same round as it stands, or `workflow resume RUN --from plan` runs the plan step again |
| `review_incomplete` | A round ended without a decision (a missing or unproven verdict, or the revision could not be computed) | `workflow resume RUN` starts a fresh round, which spends one |
| `review_stale`, `review_guard`, `review_tampered` | The plan (`review_stale`) or the checkout (`review_guard`) changed during a round, or something was already at the round's directory (`review_tampered`) | `workflow resume RUN` starts a fresh round on what is there now, which spends one |

See [The rejection loop](#the-rejection-loop) for how a rejected round retries and [Where a run is stuck](#where-a-run-is-stuck) for every reason. A v2 plan has no gate, so there is nothing to approve or reject. The `--extra-*` flags allow more attempts or rounds, and so more tokens and time: use them only when the person asked.

The workspace's agent daemon runs the sweep while it is up; install the schedule too for an unattended run, so a stopped daemon does not leave it unwatched. For v2, `workflow sweep` presses the interrupt keys of a review pane that is still working past `review.timeout` (30 minutes a round), which ends its turn so the round is decided, and it decides turns that ended while no daemon saw them. It presses the author's pane for the step `timeout` only if the definition sets `on_timeout: interrupt` or `fail` on the step, and for the run `timeout` only with `timeout_action: interrupt`; v2 sets neither, so those limits hold a new attempt or round (`run_timed_out`) and never interrupt. With no sweep at all (no daemon running and no schedule) nothing presses a review pane that keeps working, so such a round waits until a person looks. Once a pane has been pressed, the agent daemon's timer decides a round the pane still holds, so that part needs no sweep.

`workflow sweep --install` writes a per-user launchd entry that runs the sweep every 5 minutes; `--uninstall` removes it and `--installed` checks it without changing anything ([Running the sweep on a schedule](#running-the-sweep-on-a-schedule)). `workflow run` and `workflow start` warn on stderr when a definition with a `timeout:` runs without it.

```sh
workspace workflow sweep --install
workspace workflow start rpiv-auto-approve-v2 --prompt "add a retry to the uploader"
```

### The `rpiv-auto-approve` preset

`rpiv-auto-approve` is `rpiv` with no gate, for a run no person watches. For new unattended work prefer [`rpiv-auto-approve-v2`](#the-rpiv-auto-approve-v2-preset), which has the engine run the review. Eight steps run in order: `research`, `plan`, `review_supportive`, `review_skeptical`, `review_implementer`, `plan_review`, `implement` and `verify`. The three review steps and the tally take the gate's place.

Each reviewer is a step of its own with `context: fresh`, so the engine dispatches three separate conversations. None holds the planner's reasoning or another reviewer's, and the run's attempt records tie each one to its recorded session. A reviewer reads `plan status --json`, takes `plans[0].revision` (the full SHA-256 digest of the plan) and writes `approval-supportive.md`, `approval-skeptical.md` or `approval-implementer.md`: three header lines (`reviewer:`, `verdict:` and `plan_revision:`), then its reasons. Each verdict file is a `produces` file, so the step is done only when that file is newer than the attempt, and a verdict left over from an earlier round does not count.

`plan_review` is the tally, also a fresh conversation. It reads the three verdict files, writes `plan-approval.md` (the plan revision and the three verdicts, then each reviewer's reasons) and posts one `review_request` item to the person's inbox under the dedupe key `rpiv-auto-approve:RUN`, whether the round approved or rejected, so every round leaves a record. Its check validates every verdict file field by field against the live plan: each file's `reviewer` line names its own role, `verdict` is `approve` or `reject`, `plan_revision` is the plan's revision now, and the four summary lines of `plan-approval.md` match the verdicts. It passes only when the skeptic and at least one other approved, so the skeptic can't be outvoted. A missing or malformed file or a rejection fails the check, and `on_fail` sends the run back to `plan` in a fresh conversation, which reads every reviewer's reasons and looks for an approach that meets all of them; the round is reviewed again, at most nine times. A tenth failure stops the run as `failed_check`. Use `workflow resume --from plan` from there, which allows one more round. A rejection round costs five attempts, so `max_attempts` is 68. The run stops where `rpiv` does, on a verified branch, and pushes nothing. Its `implement` and `verify` steps carry the same proofs as [`rpiv`](#the-rpiv-preset).

A plan edited while the round is being reviewed fails the check with its own code, `stale_plan_revision`, which the tally's `on_fail` does not loop on: the run stops as `failed_check` with the plan file untouched (the check reads it, never writes it), so the concurrent edit keeps its wording. A fresh review of the plan as it stands starts with `workflow resume --from review_supportive` — a produced file left over from the earlier round does not count, so all three verdict files and the tally's summary are written again (resuming at `plan_review` alone would re-read the stale verdict files and stop on the same code) — or the run is cancelled. The code is a run-reason value, not a CLI error code.

What is verified: that each reviewer's verdict file was written during its own step's attempt, that each verdict names its role and the plan as it stands now, and that the tally matches the verdicts. The run records each step's session in its attempt record, so afterwards the rounds can be audited, but the check itself reads no session: separate conversations are how the steps run, not something the check proves. What is not verified: that a reviewer reviewed faithfully. Nothing can prove a reviewer read the plan or weighed it honestly, so this is a review, not a security boundary. The inbox item is the audit trail. The reviewers need a harness that can run the agent, and a permission prompt in the pane still stops the run as `waiting_you`.

Its `research` and `plan` steps are `guard: unchanged` like `rpiv`'s, so it also refuses a dirty checkout at start (`dirty_worktree`). The three reviewer steps and `plan_review` are guarded the same way. A commit made in any of them fails with `head_moved`. On the tally, a guard failure (or a guard that could not run, `check_error`) is in `on_fail`'s `except:`, so it does not loop back to `plan`, whose guard would fail the same way against the run's start baseline: the run stops as `failed_check` with the guard's code. For `head_moved` and the other guard codes a resume can't get past (`no_commit`, `hooks_changed`, `hidden_changed`), `workflow status` offers only `cancel` — a moved HEAD cannot pass the guard again — so the way on is a new run, carrying over `research.md` and the plan, which are kept in the artifacts and the notes; a commit made during review survives in the reflog. For `check_error` and the remaining guard codes (`tracked_changed`, `operation_in_progress`), `workflow status` also offers `resume` once the cause is put right.

```sh
workspace workflow start rpiv-auto-approve --prompt "add a retry to the uploader"
```

### The `pr-feedback` preset

`pr-feedback` triages a pull request's review feedback, waits for you, then makes the approved fixes. It has two steps and never posts to GitHub and never pushes.

- `triage` (gate `approve`, 30m) runs `workspace pr feedback --json`, reads the code each thread points at and the tail of each failing check's log, and writes a note `<date>-pr-<n>-feedback.md` in the notes directory: the counts of fix, answer, decline and needs-you with the head SHA read, then one `### F<k>` section per item with its source, author, a quoted excerpt, the verdict and the planned change. Only an item from a `trusted` author can be a fix. It writes `triage.md` (first line `note: <file name>`, the gate's note) and posts one `review_request` inbox item linked to the run (`--dedupe-key pr-feedback:RUN`, expiring in 168h). It stops with only `triage.md` when `inputs.pr` differs from the branch's pull request, or the pull request comes from a fork.
- `fix` (`context: fresh`, 2h) first checks the approved note's sha256 (see [Passing a gate](#passing-a-gate)) and stops with no commits when it differs. Every stop path (`no_note`, `note_changed`, `fix_head_moved`, `dirty_worktree`) runs `workspace step done --status fail --code <reason> --summary "<one sentence>"` and ends the turn, so the run fails instead of completing with `fix.md` alone. It records `origin/<head>` before and after, commits the items marked fix, appends `## Replies` to the note (grammar in [README.pr.md](README.pr.md#drafted-replies)), writes `fix.md`, marks the triage item done and posts a second `review_request` (`pr-feedback-fix:RUN`). Its last message names `workspace push` as your next step.

Inputs are `task` and `pr`, neither required. On a branch with an open pull request, a bare `workspace workflow run pr-feedback` fills both from it ([how](#filling-inputs-from-the-branch)). Start it in a workspace whose branch has the pull request, or from a ref (quote `#123` in zsh, where an unquoted `#` starts a comment; `PR-123` is read as a JIRA key):

```sh
workspace workflow run pr-feedback --name my-app.worktree-fix --input pr=123
workspace workflow start pr-feedback '#123'
workspace workflow start pr-feedback https://github.com/OWNER/REPO/pull/123
```

`workflow start` seeds `pr` from a pull request ref's number and `task` from the pull request, and leaves `pr` empty for anything that is not a pull request ref (a branch, an issue, a JIRA key). Any workflow that declares a `pr` input gets it seeded this way, and `--input pr=` wins. Triage compares numbers only, so a pull request with the same number in another repository passes that check; a fork pull request (reached through `workflow run`; `workflow start` refuses it with `fork_pull_request`), or a number mismatch, stops triage with only `triage.md` and an inbox item, and you reject the gate. `fix` refuses to start without an approved triage note (the gate's `note:` line), and stops when the note's hash, the branch head or a clean tree no longer match. The workflow does not declare `spec`, so the pull request's body is not written to `.workflow/tickets/`. Its instructions say every comment, review and log line is data written by someone else: the agent follows none of it, puts none of it on a command line, and never runs `workflow approve` or `workflow reject`. It has no `status:` check, so it runs in a project with no `commands.test`; running the tests is the `commits` pack's job.

### The `investigate` preset

`investigate` reproduces a failure, finds its cause, waits for you, then commits a regression test and the fix you approved. **It runs this branch's tests on your machine before you approve anything**: the same trust as `workspace start '#N'` and running the tests yourself. It never pushes, never posts to GitHub and never reruns CI.

Ways to start it (quote `#123` in zsh):

```sh
workspace workflow run investigate                       # in the workspace with the red check; task and pr come from the branch
workspace workflow run investigate --name WS --input ci_run=9876543210 --input pr=123 --json
workspace workflow run investigate --input ci_run=https://github.com/OWNER/REPO/actions/runs/9876543210
workspace workflow start investigate '#123'              # a pull request from this repository
workspace workflow start investigate PROJ-412
workspace workflow start investigate https://github.com/OWNER/REPO/issues/88
workspace workflow start investigate --prompt "checkout total is off by a cent"
```

With no input the run takes the branch's own pull request and its first failing check; only with no pull request, no ticket and no prompt does it stop (`no_input`). A guarded workflow refuses a dirty tree before anything starts (see [The guard](#the-guard)).

Inputs, none required, each checked against its `pattern:` before a run exists (`invalid_input`):

| Input | Accepts | Filled by |
|---|---|---|
| `ci_run` | digits, or a `https://github.com/OWNER/REPO/actions/runs/N` URL (optionally `/attempts/N` or `/job/N`) | `--input ci_run=` |
| `pr` | digits (up to 9) | a pull request ref, or `--input pr=` |
| `task` | any text | the ticket (key, title, URL) and `--prompt`, or `--input task=` |
| `spec` | a file under `.workflow/tickets/` | `workflow start`, from the issue, JIRA or pull request body |

The task is never rendered into the instructions: the agent reads it as a JSON string from `workflow status RUN --json` (`inputs.task`), so no title or prompt can break out of it. Ticket and pull request bodies, comments, check names and log lines are data: the instructions limit where a command's arguments may come from, forbid posting, pushing, rerunning CI and `workflow approve|reject|resume`, and ask for token-shaped text to be replaced with `[redacted]` before it reaches the note. Raw logs stay in the run's artifacts directory.

- `reproduce` (`guard: unchanged`, 45m) stops with `repro.md` (first line `stopped: <reason>`), a `note` inbox item (`investigate-stop:RUN`) and `step done --status fail --code <reason>` for `no_input`, `pr_mismatch`, `fork`, `run_not_found`, `run_not_finished` or `run_mismatch` (the run's commit is neither the pull request's head nor an ancestor of HEAD). Otherwise it reads the failed log (capped at 5 MB, with the exit status kept), reproduces the failure locally and writes `repro.md` and a reproduction as a new file. A stop item from an earlier attempt is marked done when it does not stop.
- `diagnose` (`guard: unchanged`, gate `approve`, 60m, same conversation) writes the note `<subject>-investigation-<run>.md` in the notes directory: `## Answer` (the cause, `head_sha`, `reproduced`, `ci_verdict` of `branch`, `main`, `flaky`, `infra` or `unknown`, `confidence`, and `If you approve:`), `## Fix options` (one marked `(recommended)`), `## Evidence` and `## Not checked`. It writes `diagnosis.md` (first line `note: <file name>`, the gate's note) and posts one `review_request` item (`investigate:RUN`, 168h).
- `fix` (`guard: config`, `context: fresh`, 2h, holds `test-db`) stops with `fix.md` and a code (`no_note`, `note_changed`, `fix_head_moved`, `fix_dirty_tree`, `workflow_files_visible`) when the approved note's hash, HEAD, the tracked tree or the `.workflow/` exclude no longer match. Its plan is only the note's `If you approve:` line and recommended option, changed by your approval comment. It commits a regression test and then the fix with hooks off (`core.hooksPath=/dev/null`, staging by path), through the `commits` pack's test and lint commands, appends `## Fix` to the note, marks the diagnosis item done and posts `investigate-fix:RUN`. Its last message names `workspace push` (or `workspace pr create`).

Approve with a note to change the plan ("approve, but use option B"), or reject with a hint and `diagnose` writes the note again, keeping the earlier answer under `## Rejected (attempt N)`. A stop code is a word the agent chose (`details.code` with `code_source` `agent`); a guard code (`head_moved`, `tracked_changed`, ...) is the engine's. Commit in the workspace while the gate waits and `fix` stops with `fix_head_moved`; `base_sha` in `workflow status --json` shows what HEAD should be. The guard bounds HEAD, the tracked tree and git's own files, not the machine: an agent talked into running something before the gate can do what your account can, so use it on code you would run anyway. `workflow run` never refuses a fork pull request; `reproduce` stops with `fork` (`workflow start` refuses it first, `fork_pull_request`).

A workflow's steps can hand the agent shell commands that hold the run's artifacts path, which lies under the checkout. `workflow run` (also `--dry-run`) and `workflow start` therefore refuse a checkout whose path holds a quote, a backtick, a dollar sign, a backslash, a control character or an invisible one (zero-width, bidi, U+2028, U+2029), with `unsafe_checkout_path` (`details.characters` names them, `details.path_kind` says `checkout` or `notes directory`; spaces and non-ASCII letters are fine). `workflow start` works out the path of a worktree it would create (`.worktrees/<branch>`) before anything is made, so a new unsafe path is refused first, also with `--dry-run`: no worktree, check or agent. The notes directory is held to the same rule for a workflow with a gate, since the fix step is handed the note's path (a branch name can reach it); the gate note is checked again when it is read. The refusal applies to every workflow, so a home directory like `/Users/o'brien` blocks them all.

### The `explore` preset

`explore` answers a question and changes no code: it surveys, waits for you to prune what it found, then writes a report. The brief (`--input task=`, or `--prompt`) is sent once, in the run's instructions, so it can run to many paragraphs; the optional `--input topic="upload-resume"` gives the `<topic>` of the note names and inbox titles (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, no spaces, up to 60 characters), and without it the agent chooses two to five words. The notes go in the notes directory and only `survey.md` and `report.md` in the run's artifacts. A requirement in the brief about what the report holds (examples, diagrams) is kept for the `report` step. `report` finds your approval note under `## For this attempt`, and `workflow status --json` shows it as the survey step's `gate_note`. Use it for a gap analysis or design research, where `rpiv` would go on to build something. It needs no `commands.test`.

```sh
workspace workflow run explore --input task="What would make uploads resumable?"
workspace workflow start explore --prompt "What would make uploads resumable?"
```

- `survey` (`guard: unchanged`, gate `approve`, 90m) writes the note `<date>-<topic>-survey.md` in the notes directory: the questions, what exists today, each gap with its evidence, and a ranked list for you to prune. `survey.md` in the run's artifacts names the note on its first line, so the gate shows it, and a `review_request` inbox item (`explore:RUN`) links it. Approve with a note to cut the list or change the emphasis, or reject with a hint and it writes the note again.
- `report` (`guard: config`, 90m) reads the approved note, closes the inbox item, and writes `<date>-<topic>-report.md` in the notes directory with the verdict first, then ranked recommendations, then the detail, has a skeptical sub-agent check the claims against the code, and hands the note back through the `report` pack as a `note` inbox item. `report.md` in the run's artifacts holds the note's file name.

## Required proofs

A step's `require:` is a list of proofs the engine checks when the agent's turn ends, after the `produces` files are accepted and before the `status:` command runs (a step the agent reported as failed is not checked: it already failed). Each entry is one proof; the first that fails stops the check:

```yaml
implement:
  produces: [implement.md]
  require:
    - commits: 1              # at least one commit of the agent's own since this attempt began
    - nonempty: implement.md  # a non-empty regular file inside the run's artifacts directory, not a link
    - plan_status: done       # the workspace plan's status: is active, paused or done
    - tracked_clean: true     # no tracked file differs from HEAD, no rebase/merge/etc. under way (untracked files pass)
    - command: lint           # the project's commands.lint (or commands.test) exits 0
      optional: true          # with this line, an unset command passes the proof instead of being refused
    - inbox_item: "verify:{{run}}"  # the workspace's own inbox holds an item with this key, updated during the attempt
    - remote_unchanged: true  # the branch on its remote is where it was when the attempt began
    - exec: "script/check-changed -- {{changed.files}}"  # a program of your own exits 0 (see below)
      timeout: 10m
```

A failed proof fails the step like a failed `status:` does, so `on_fail` and `workflow status` work unchanged. `details.code` is `require_commits`, `require_nonempty`, `require_plan_status`, `require_tracked_clean`, `require_command`, `require_inbox_item`, `require_remote_unchanged` or `require_exec` (or `require_remote_unreachable`, see below), `code_source` is `engine`, `details.cause` is `require` and `details.detail` says what was required and found, such as `required 1 commit since 4f2a91c, found 0`. `commits` also has `required`, `found` and `base`; `tracked_clean` has `paths`. A proof that raises is `require_unknown` (unproven), with the exception class and message in `details.error`.

`commits` counts from the HEAD recorded when the attempt began (`head.before`, see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)). The base is per attempt: commits an earlier failed attempt made do not count, and a `resume` of the same attempt keeps that attempt's base. It counts the commits on HEAD's first-parent line, merges left out (`rev-list --count --first-parent --no-merges`), and leaves out any commit reachable from another local branch or remote-tracking branch. Two refs are not "another": the branch itself, and its upstream when that holds the branch's own work, meaning a remote-tracking branch that is not the remote's default branch under a different name. An upstream that is a local branch, or `origin/main` for a branch named `feat`, is a shared base and does count as another branch (the default branch is what `refs/remotes/<remote>/HEAD` names, or `main` and `master` when that is not set; an upstream whose remote git cannot read is not exempt). So a merge, a fast-forward or a rebase onto `main` brings nothing in, and a commit also on a sibling branch is not counted. Two known limits: commits pulled from a topic branch's own upstream (`feat` tracking `origin/feat`) during the attempt still count, as does a commit the step pushes to it; and a commit a branch pushes straight to the default branch it tracks (`feat` tracking `origin/main`) is not counted, though a branch named like the default (`main` tracking `origin/main`) counts its own. A detached HEAD excludes nothing. If the base is not an ancestor of HEAD (the step amended, reset or rebased past it) the proof is unproven with `details.reason` `history_rewritten`: a step that rewrites its own attempt's base fails this proof. Empty commits still count, and are not detected. A proof that can't be read fails as unproven (`details.unproven` is `true`) and never passes: an attempt with no recorded starting commit (an attempt stored before HEAD was recorded, or an unborn branch), a base git doesn't know, git not answering within 5 seconds, a plan or notes directory that can't be read. Git runs the way the guard's does (see [The guard](#the-guard)): the global and system config are ignored, inherited `GIT_*` variables are dropped, hooks are off. `commits: N` takes a whole number from 1 to 1000. `nonempty` takes a plain relative path (no control character, no empty, `.` or `..` part, no leading or trailing space or `/`, not starting with `~`; validate refuses the rest) and is stricter than `produces`: the file must be a non-empty regular file and not a link, and its real path must lie inside the artifacts directory's real path, so a linked parent directory fails; a run with no recorded artifacts directory, or a file that can't be resolved, is unproven. `plan_status` reads `IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` in the workspace's notes directory and compares the `status:` as written: a missing or non-canonical value (not `active`, `paused` or `done`) is unproven, even when `active` is required. `tracked_clean` also fails while a rebase, merge, cherry-pick, revert or bisect is in progress; it ignores submodules and cannot see `assume-unchanged` or `skip-worktree` files.

`command` takes `test` or `lint` and runs the project's `commands.test` or `commands.lint` the way a `status:` check does: through `sh -c` in the checkout, with `WORKSPACE_RUN_ID`, `WORKSPACE_RUN_ARTIFACTS` and `WORKSPACE_RUN_BASE_SHA` and no inherited `GIT_*` variable, its output in `steps/<step>.<attempt>.require-<role>.<token>.log` in the artifacts directory. It passes on exit 0 and fails on any other exit (`details.exit_code`, `details.log`). A command that is not set (unless the entry has `optional: true`, which skips the proof and records it in `proofs`; `workflow start` and `workflow run` refuse an unset non-optional one before anything starts, listing every unset command with its step), runs past 5 minutes (it is stopped) or cannot start is unproven. Because it runs a shell, the run's own agent cannot `resume` its run from its own pane (as for a `status:` or `guard:` step).

`exec` runs a program of your own and passes when it exits 0. It is for a check the project's `commands.test` and `commands.lint` do not make, such as a script that reads the files the agent changed. The value is a program and its arguments as one string, split into words the way a shell splits them (quotes group words) but never run by one: there is no `sh -c`, no pipe, no `$VAR`, no glob, no `;`, so a value with spaces or a quote stays one argument. `timeout:` is a duration (`30s`, `10m`) with a default of 5 minutes and a cap of 2 hours (a bigger value fails `validate`); the program is stopped when it runs past it. The cap is low because a proof whose process is lost is only noticed after its `timeout` plus a minute plus the time of its git reads. `optional:` is refused beside `exec` (it means "the project's command is unset", which has no meaning here). A step may hold several `exec` entries; they run in order, and the first that fails stops the check. The program runs in the checkout with the same environment as a `command` proof (`WORKSPACE_RUN_ID`, `WORKSPACE_RUN_ARTIFACTS`, `WORKSPACE_RUN_BASE_SHA`, no `GIT_*` variable), its output in `steps/<step>.<attempt>.require-exec-<index>.<token>.log` (`index` is the entry's place in `require:`), opened without following a link. A non-zero exit fails the proof with `require_exec` and `details.exit_code`, `details.log` and `details.program`. A program that cannot start, runs past its timeout or is lost is unproven. As for `command`, the run's own agent cannot `resume` its run from its own pane.

The placeholders fill in each argument before the program starts. Scalars, which fill in part of an argument or all of it: `{{checkout}}` (the checkout's path), `{{artifacts}}`, `{{workspace}}`, `{{branch}}`, `{{run}}` (the run id), `{{run.base_sha}}` (HEAD when the run began), `{{attempt.head_before}}` (HEAD when this attempt began) and `{{inputs.NAME}}` for each input the workflow declares. Lists, which must be an argument of their own: `{{changed.files}}` (the files this attempt changed), `{{changed.tests}}` (those of them that match `commands.test_files`) and `{{run.changed.files}}` (the files the whole run changed, from `base_sha`). A list expands to one argument per file, in sorted order; an empty list adds no argument. Many tools (`rspec`, `standardrb`) run on everything when given no files, so a program that should do nothing for an empty list needs a wrapper that exits 0 when it gets no arguments. "Changed" means committed since the base, plus staged, unstaged and untracked files that are not ignored, with deleted files left out and a rename counted as its new path. Put `--` before a list (`script/check -- {{changed.files}}`): a changed file whose name starts with `-` would otherwise read as an option, and the engine does not refuse such names, since refusing them would let an agent block a step by naming a file. `validate` warns when it finds a list with no `--` before it. A placeholder is not allowed in the program (the first word), an unknown one fails `validate`, and a list inside a word (`--files={{changed.files}}`) is refused. A leading `~/` in the program is expanded to the home directory, nowhere else. A whole-argument `{{inputs.NAME}}` can hold a value that begins with `-`, which the program would read as an option: put `--` before it, or constrain the input with `pattern:`.

The same lists are also files, for a program that would rather read them than take them as arguments (and for a list too long for the system to start the program with: the failure is unproven with `E2BIG` in its detail, which names the file and the variable to read instead). The engine writes each list it could read to a file in a new private temporary directory outside the checkout, one path after another each ended by a NUL byte (`find -print0` style, so no file name can break a record), and sets `WORKSPACE_CHANGED_FILES`, `WORKSPACE_CHANGED_TESTS` and `WORKSPACE_RUN_CHANGED_FILES` to the path of its file. A variable is set only when its list could be read (`WORKSPACE_CHANGED_TESTS` also needs `commands.test_files`), and one of those names that the daemon's own environment holds is unset first, so a program never reads a stale list. The directory is removed after the proof. It is outside the checkout because the agent can write the checkout, but it is not tamper-proof: the agent runs as the same user, so a process it left running could still change the files while the program reads them. Git reads of the file lists are limited to 32 MiB of output; a list git cannot give inside that and its time limit leaves the proof unproven.

A proof that cannot know its facts is unproven, and its program is not run. A template that uses `{{changed.files}}` or `{{changed.tests}}` when the attempt has no recorded starting commit (`head.before`), or `{{run.changed.files}}` when the run has no `base_sha`, is unproven, since an empty list could let a checker pass on nothing. `{{changed.tests}}` with `commands.test_files` unset is unproven too (see [`commands.test_files`](README.config.md)); `workflow start` and `workflow run` refuse it before anything starts as `workflow_test_files_unset`, with the step. A template that uses none of those lists runs without the base. The program is looked up on the PATH of the process that runs the proof, which is the agent daemon's and can differ from your shell's; `workflow start` and `workflow run` check the program before anything starts and refuse a missing or non-executable one as `workflow_program_missing` (a bare name must be on the PATH, an absolute or `~/` path must be an executable file, a path like `script/check` is looked up in the checkout), and `validate` warns of the same as `exec_program_missing`. A program inside the checkout is the agent's to change: an agent that edits `script/check` can make it pass, so keep a checker you rely on outside the checkout, or review changes to it. The definition stores the split argument vector, so `workflow show --json` prints `"exec"` as an array of words (and `"timeout"` as whole seconds), not the string you wrote.

`inbox_item` takes a dedupe key, which may hold `{{run}}`, `{{workspace}}` and `{{inputs.NAME}}` (other placeholders fail `validate`). It passes when the workspace's own inbox holds an item with that key, in any state, whose `updated_at` is not before the attempt's `started_at` (the inbox keeps whole seconds, so the same second counts). So an item the step posted and closed counts, and one left from an earlier attempt or run does not, even under a key with no `{{run}}`. Only the workspace's inbox is searched: an item added with `--project` or `--global` does not count. Adding to a key whose item is done or dismissed changes nothing, so a step that runs again under such a key must pass `inbox add --reopen`. Anyone closing or editing an old item during the attempt also makes it count. When an agent forgets to post, the step fails with `details.code` `require_inbox_item` and a `details.detail` such as `required an inbox item with dedupe key explore:wr_1 in workspace app's inbox, found none`, and `on_fail` or a resume decides what follows. A key that renders to something that is not a valid dedupe key (1 to 200 letters, digits and `. _ : / # @ -`) is unproven.

`remote_unchanged` compares `git ls-remote` on the remote a `git push` would go to (`branch.<b>.pushRemote`, then the repository's own `remote.pushDefault`, then `branch.<b>.remote`, then `origin`) for the ref the branch tracks (`branch.<b>.merge`, else `refs/heads/<branch>`; on a push remote other than the tracked one, `refs/heads/<branch>`, except that a `pr-N` branch keeps a `refs/heads/` merge ref) with the reading recorded as the attempt's `remote` (`sha` is `null` when the remote has no such branch). Only a step that requires it touches the network. It fails when the sha differs (`details.before`, `details.after`: the step pushed, or deleted, the branch). Each `ls-remote` has a 15 second limit (with the local reads, at most 45 seconds in all) and no prompt (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, ssh in batch mode, only the https, ssh, git and file protocols). The user's and the system's git config are ignored, so the remote needs a non-interactive credential such as an ssh key in an agent: a credential helper, `insteadOf` or `core.sshCommand` is not used, and a private https remote that relies on one reads as unreachable every time. A remote that cannot be read when the check runs is unproven with `details.code` `require_remote_unreachable`. It is unproven as `require_remote_unchanged`, with the reason in `details.detail`, when there was no reading when the attempt began (a detached HEAD, a branch whose upstream is local, a remote that does not exist, an unreachable remote), the attempt was stored with none, or the branch came to track something else.

`command`, `remote_unchanged` and `exec` run with the run's lock given up, the way a `status:` check does, so `cancel`, `kill`, `approve`, `reject`, `resume` and `step done` don't wait for them.
