The step reads `checking` meanwhile. Entries still run in order: the ones after such a proof, then `status:`, run once it passes. A `cancel`, a `kill` or a `resume --from` stops a running `command` or `exec` and its result is dropped; a `remote_unchanged` read is not stopped, and its result is dropped the same way. A process that dies during a proof leaves the step `checking`: past that proof's limit plus a minute (for `exec`, plus the time of its git reads) a `tick` or a `resume` runs the step's proofs again from the first. Each run of a proof has its own token and log; an older run still alive is stopped and its result dropped. The baseline read happens with the lock given up too, after the attempt is stored and before its line is typed; a cancel during the read means nothing is typed, and a process that dies there leaves an attempt with no reading.

The proofs show facts about the repository, not that the work is good: `commits: 1` passes a useless commit. A definition stored before `require:` existed has no `require` in its snapshot and behaves as before. `workflow validate` lists every bad entry (an unknown proof, a repeated proof, a bad value) under `invalid_workflow`. A step with only `require:` has proof, so it does not warn.

The presets use them. `rpiv` and `rpiv-auto-approve` are described above. Every preset step that posts to the inbox on each successful path requires its `inbox_item`: `explore` (`explore:RUN` for `survey`, `explore-report:RUN` for `report`), `pr-feedback` (`pr-feedback:RUN` for `triage`, a stop path included, and `pr-feedback-fix:RUN` for `fix`) and `investigate` (`investigate:RUN` for `diagnose`, `investigate-fix:RUN` for `fix`). Their prompts post with `--reopen`, because a rerun of the step (after a rejected gate or a resume) uses the same key, and an item the person already closed would otherwise be left alone and fail the proof. A step that stops through `step done --status fail` is not checked, and `investigate`'s `reproduce` posts only on its stop paths, so it has no proof. A run already started keeps the definition it was stored with: a change to a preset affects new runs only.

## Denying commands

```yaml
deny:
  - "git push*"
  - "gh pr comment*"
  - "gh pr review*"
  - "gh run rerun*"
```

A `deny:` list stops the run's agent from running a Bash command that matches a pattern. The `session-event` hook that runs before each tool call looks up the run the pane is bound to, reads `deny` from the definition the run kept, and refuses a matching command with exit 2 and this text for the agent: `denied by workflow investigate, step fix: "git push*". Report this and stop.` Each denial writes `command_denied` to the run's history (`workflow events`) and to the [event log](README.event-log.md) (`run_id`, `workflow`, `workspace`, `step`, `pattern`, and the first 300 characters of `command`, control characters escaped). A pane with no binding costs one read of the bindings file. A pane bound to a run also pays the stale-binding check (two tmux calls) and one parse of the run file on each Bash call, whether or not the run has a `deny` list. The run's stored definition is what counts, so editing the definition file mid-run changes nothing for a run in flight. A run that has finished, or a run or binding file that can't be read, allows the command: an error in the check never stops the agent's other tools.

**This is a tripwire, not a sandbox.** It catches an agent that was misled or made a mistake with the plain forms of a command. `bash -c '...'`, a script, an alias, a variable holding the command, `$'...'` quoting, `timeout N`, `nice` or `xargs` in front of the command, a command after `if ...; then`, `!` or `{`, inside `((...))` (without the `$`), `[[ ]]` or a `case` pattern, and any tool other than Bash get past it. Do not rely on it against an agent that means harm. For the push, the proof after the fact is `require: remote_unchanged` on the step (see [Required proofs](#required-proofs)), which fails the step when the remote branch moved.

How a command is matched:

- It is cut into pieces at `;`, `&&`, `||`, `|`, `&`, newlines, parentheses and backticks that are not inside quotes, so `cd x && git push` and `$(git push)` are checked as `git push`. A quoted `;` does not cut: `git commit -m "a; git push"` is one piece and is not denied (and `echo "$(git push)"` is not looked into).
- An unquoted `#` that starts a word begins a comment, skipped to the end of the line. `$(( ))` is only watched so that a `<<` shift in it does not start a here-document; a `$(...)` or backtick inside it is still checked, but a bare command in it (`$((git push) )`) is not.
- Runs of spaces and tabs outside quotes count as one space. The body of a here-document (`<<WORD`, `<<-WORD`, `<<'WORD'`) is skipped up to its terminator line, or to the end of the text when the terminator never comes, so a commit message that contains `git push` is not denied.
- Each piece loses leading whitespace, leading `NAME=value` assignments and the words `sudo`, `command`, `env`, `exec`, `nohup` and `time` (with an option such as `-i` right after one, and the value of `sudo -u/-g` and `env -u`), so `FOO=1 sudo -u root git push` is checked as `git push`. A path before the program (`/usr/bin/git`) is dropped, and so are git's global options (`-C dir`, `-c k=v`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--no-pager`, ...) and gh's `-R/--repo`, so `git -C x push` is checked as `git push`.
- The rest of the piece must match a pattern as a whole, as a shell glob: `*` matches any run of characters (slashes and newlines too) and `?` one character. Nothing in a pattern is run or evaluated; `[abc]` sets work, and `{a,b}` is plain text. `git push*` matches `git push origin HEAD` and also `git pushd`.

`workflow validate` collects a bad `deny` (not a list, an empty or non-text entry, too many, too long, too many wildcards) with the other problems under `invalid_workflow`. The `investigate` and `pr-feedback` presets deny the four patterns above, since neither pushes or posts; `workspace push` is not matched, so the person's next step still works.


## The guard

A step with `guard:` is checked by the engine when the agent's turn ends, before its `produces:` files are looked for (a step the agent reported as failed is not guarded: it already failed). The checkout it is checked against was recorded when the run started: `base_sha` (HEAD) and a snapshot of the repository's config, its hooks directory and its hidden-file flags, in the run file as `base_guard`. `workflow status --json` shows `base_sha` on the run.

- `guard: unchanged` finds any of the failure codes below.
- `guard: config` finds only `no_commit`, `hooks_changed` and `hidden_changed`. A step that commits (a fix) uses it.

Both also look for a changed repository config, which warns and never fails the step (see [A changed config](#a-changed-config)).

A step with `guard: unchanged` also needs a clean checkout to start: `workflow run`, `workflow run --dry-run`, and `workflow start` (also `--dry-run`) into a workspace it reuses refuse with `dirty_worktree` (`details.guard`, `details.files`) when the checkout has no commit, a git operation stopped in it, or a change to a tracked file. Untracked files pass, so a reproduction can be written.

| Code | Found when | Actions offered |
|---|---|---|
| `no_commit` | The run has no base commit (an unborn branch) or HEAD can't be read | `cancel` with no `base_sha` recorded (`resume --accept-guard` or `cancel`); otherwise `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |
| `head_moved` | HEAD is not `base_sha` (a commit, a checkout, a reset) | `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |
| `operation_in_progress` | A merge, cherry-pick, revert, bisect or rebase is stopped in the checkout | `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |
| `hooks_changed` | The hooks directory (`core.hooksPath` of the repository config, else the common dir's `hooks`) changed: a file, its mode, its content or a link's target | `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |
| `hidden_changed` | The `git ls-files -v` lines tagged assume-unchanged or skip-worktree changed from the start (flags that were already set, as in a sparse checkout, are fine) | `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |
| `tracked_changed` | `git status` shows a staged or unstaged change to a tracked file (submodules are not looked at); `details.paths` names up to 20 | `resume`, `resume --from`, `resume --accept-guard`, `cancel` |

`base_sha` is recorded once, at the run's start, and never anew: that would accept whatever moved HEAD, the agent's own commit included. A plain `workflow resume` of a `no_commit` with no `base_sha` (the branch had no commit at start) is refused with `guard_cannot_pass`, as every guarded step compares to a nil base; accept it (below) or cancel. For `head_moved`, `hooks_changed`, `hidden_changed` and a `no_commit` that has a `base_sha`, the person puts the checkout back and resumes: HEAD back at `base_sha` (save any work you want first, on a branch), the hooks restored, the assume-unchanged or skip-worktree flags cleared. For `tracked_changed` and `operation_in_progress` the person cleans the checkout and resumes. A guard stop that can pass again carries the step in the reason's `recovery` (see below).

#### Accepting a guard stop

When the change is wanted (a commit the person made, a hook they edited), `workflow resume RUN --accept-guard --note "why"` takes the checkout as it is now as the run's base: `base_sha` becomes HEAD and `base_guard` is read again. `--note` is required (a usage error without it). Only a run stopped by a guard (`head_moved`, `hooks_changed`, `hidden_changed`, `no_commit`, `operation_in_progress`, `tracked_changed`) can be accepted; any other stop is refused with `guard_not_stopped`. A `no_commit` with no `base_sha` is accepted too: HEAD becomes the base once there is one; with none the step still passes (or runs again) with the note recorded. Tracked files left changed are accepted as they are: `base_guard.accepted_tree_sha256` records them, and a later `guard: unchanged` step passes while they read the same (a further change stops it again with `tracked_changed`, naming every changed path). A git operation still in progress is not put right by accepting it: the next `guard: unchanged` step stops on it again. Every later guarded step measures from the new base, so a cause still there when it runs stops it again in the same way. The new base has to be readable: when git can't read HEAD (a branch that has commits, or a timeout) or the hooks, hidden-file or tracked-file reading, nothing is accepted and the stored base stays (`guard_base_unreadable`, retryable).

Only the guard is accepted. By default the rest of the stopped step's evaluation runs now, the guard left out: its `produces` files, its `require:` proofs and its `status:` check. When they pass, the attempt is marked `overridden` (never `passed`, also when a review round approves it later) and the run moves on as a passed step would: its review round, its gate, else the next step. When they don't, the step stops with their own reason, as after any turn: `turn_ended_incomplete` for a missing file (write it, then `workflow resume RUN`), or `failed_check` with the proof's or check's code. With `--retry` (only with `--accept-guard`, not with `--from`) the step runs again as a new attempt, counted against `max_attempts` (`--extra-attempts` as usual). A retry that would stop the same way is refused with `guard_cannot_pass`: `no_commit` while the branch still has no commit, `operation_in_progress` while the rebase, merge or other operation is still under way; fix that first, or accept without `--retry`. The note is recorded on a `guard_overridden` event (`guard`, old and new base, `note`, the caller's pane) and put at the top of the next attempt's instructions, after one generated line: "A person accepted the `GUARD` stop on `STEP`; the guard now measures from HEAD `SHA12`."

### A changed config

`config_changed` is a warning: the step goes on, and a hooks, hidden-flag or tracked-file failure is still found. The common dir's `config` is shared by every workspace of the repository, so `workspace start` elsewhere must not stop a run. What is compared:

- The common dir's `config` and the checkout's own `config.worktree`, as `git config --includes --list` reads them: comments and layout don't count, an `include.path` is an entry of its own, and an included file's contents count in its place, so a new include or a change inside one warns.
- `info/attributes` and the `refs/replace/` refs.
- Left out: `branch.<name>.*` sections of every branch but the one checked out now (all of them on a detached HEAD), and the `config.worktree` of other linked worktrees. Two-part keys such as `branch.autoSetupRebase` are settings and stay in.

A warning is recorded in the attempt's `warnings`, in `guard_warnings` of `workflow status` (its text output prints a `warning:` line), and as one `guard_warning` event, and posts one `alert` inbox item per run (`dedupe_key` `workflow-guard:RUN:config_changed`, updated by a later change, which also reopens it if it was closed). The run keeps the last reading it warned of in `guard_config_seen`, so each distinct change warns once, at the first guarded step that sees it, and not at each step after. Only a step with `guard:` reads the config, so an unguarded step never warns; a change it makes is seen by the next guarded step. A config back at the start's reading is no change and says nothing (and clears `guard_config_seen`, so changing it again warns). A warning that could not be recorded on the attempt is tried again at the next guarded step. A config that can't be read warns the same way, and a recovery to the start's reading says nothing; git failing on it also fails `hooks_changed`.

A run started before this reading was added holds a digest of the raw files, which never matches: it warns once, and the message says the difference may be no change at all. A run stored as stopped on a `config_changed` failure can be resumed: it is offered `resume` and `resume --from` as well as `cancel`, and goes on with that warning.

Once the effective `filter.*` entries differ from the ones recorded at the run's start (`base_guard.filter_sha256`), the guard's `git status` and the `tracked_clean` proof turn every filter off (`filter.<name>.clean`, `smudge` and `process` empty, `required` false), so a filter written during the step neither runs in the check nor reports an edit as clean. Only then can a file a project's own filter rewrites (Git LFS) read as `tracked_changed`, until `git status` refreshes it. Any other config change leaves filters working, and so does a run with no recorded `filter_sha256` (one started before it existed). A changed filter whose name holds `=` can't be turned off (`git -c` can't carry it), so the step fails `tracked_changed` with no paths. If the `filter.*` entries can't be read, filters are left as they are: git reading the same config for the status fails too, and an unreadable reading is not taken for a changed filter. Other settings that steer `git status`, such as `core.worktree` or `core.ignoreStat`, warn and no longer fail.

The guard is a tripwire for an agent that was misled or made a mistake, not a boundary against one that means harm: a process of your own user can edit the run file, start a process outside the pane (`tmux split-window`), or forge the git index's stat data so an edit looks unchanged. Every git call it makes is the `git` found on `PATH` when `workspace` started, and ignores the global and system git config, drops every inherited `GIT_*` variable, ignores replace refs, and runs with fsmonitor and hooks off (and reads attributes from HEAD when git is 2.41 or newer), so a config the agent wrote can't run a command during the check. A `status:` check runs with every inherited `GIT_*` variable removed too. The guard's own check of a remote (`remote_unchanged`) still reads the repository's `remote.*` settings. The guard does not see untracked files, submodules or anything outside the checkout. A part git can't read at check time fails with its code, even if it could not be read at the start; a guard that raises fails the step as `check_error`.

A plain `workflow resume` (no `--from`) and `workflow advance` of a step that has a `guard:` or a `status:` are refused with `bound_pane` from the pane the run is bound to or a process under it: otherwise the agent could decide its own step by hand with a fake `git` first on `PATH`. The agent daemon starts `advance` as a process of its own, with `$TMUX_PANE` removed.

### Failure codes

Every `failed_check` reason has `details.code` and `details.code_source`. `engine` codes are a closed set: the guard codes above, `require_commits`, `require_nonempty`, `require_plan_status`, `require_tracked_clean`, `require_command`, `require_inbox_item`, `require_remote_unchanged`, `require_remote_unreachable`, `require_exec` (`details.program` names the program), `require_unknown` (see [Required proofs](#required-proofs)), `check_failed` (a `status:` command exited non-zero), `check_timed_out`, `check_error` (the check could not run, or the project's command is unset), `review_rejected`, `review_incomplete`, `review_stale`, `review_tampered`, `review_guard` and `review_no_change` (set for reviews; reserved now, so `step done --code` refuses them), `step_timed_out` (`workflow sweep` failed a step past its `timeout` with `on_timeout: fail`; `details.cause` is `timeout`, with `timeout` and `elapsed` in seconds) and `reported` (`step done --status fail` with no `--code`). For a guard failure `details.guard` repeats `code`, `details.paths` is `[]` except for `tracked_changed`, and `head_moved` also has `base_sha` and `head`. A run stored before these keys existed has no `code` or `code_source` on its stored reasons. `step done --code` refuses the engine's words. An `agent` code is the word the agent gave with `step done --code`, stored as `reported.code`: a word it chose, which each workflow documents for itself. A client maps it to words, never to actions. `details.summary` stays free text, and `details.error` (why a check has no exit code) is not a code.

### A rejected gate

`workflow reject` marks the gated step's `gate` as `rejected`, and the step row of `status --json` keeps `"gate":"rejected"` while that step runs again (the first dispatch after the reject). After `reject --to` an earlier step, the gated step shows `rejected` until it is dispatched again. A later `resume` of a failed rerun clears it, and a rerun that passes replaces it with `waiting`.

## Passing a gate

`workflow approve` passes a waiting gate and `workflow reject` turns it down. Both are for a person, and refuse with `bound_pane` when run from a pane a workflow run is bound to (`$TMUX_PANE`), or from a process whose parent chain holds the run's own pane (the pane's pid, from `tmux list-panes`, among the caller's ancestors in one `ps` snapshot), which holds with `$TMUX_PANE` unset or set to another pane. That refusal's `details.agent` names the calling agent (`pid`, `started`) when one is found. When tmux or the process table can't be read, only the `$TMUX_PANE` check runs. A dev server started from the bound pane is under it, and gets `bound_pane` too.

These guards stop mistakes and a confused agent. They are not a security boundary: an agent with a shell can clear `$TMUX_PANE`, or start a process outside its pane's tree (`tmux new-window`, `tmux run-shell`, `launchctl`, `ssh localhost`), and that process gets past them.

A client that shows the gate builds its own argv from `workflow status RUN --json`, enabling its buttons only when `current.reason.code` is `waiting_you` and `details.kind` is `gate`:

```bash
workspace workflow approve --step=<current.step> --attempt=<current.attempt> --note-sha256=<gate_file.sha256> --json -- <run>
workspace workflow reject --step=<current.step> --attempt=<current.attempt> --note=<reason> --json -- <run>
```

With `--step` (and `--attempt`), a run that is at another step, on another attempt, or whose gate there is not waiting is refused with `gate_not_waiting`: the row is stale, so read the status again rather than retry. `details.step` and `details.attempt` are the run's, `details.expected_step` and `details.expected_attempt` the caller's. Without the flags a gate is passed wherever the run waits, as before. A gate's `actions` in `status` carry `--step` and `--attempt` in their `args`, for terminal clients.

**The gate's note.** When the gated step's first `produces` file starts with the line `note: <file>`, the gate is about that note: `<file>` is a plain file name (no `/`) in the workspace's notes directory ([`notes dir`](README.notes.md)), a regular file and not a link. While the gate waits, `status` adds `gate_file: {name, path, sha256}` to `current.reason.details`, hashed when status is read; it is `null` when the step's first file has no `note:` line, and `null` with a warning when the note can't be read, in which case `current.reason.details.gate_file_error` is `gate_note_unreadable` (else `null`), the same code `approve` refuses with. `approve` hashes the note once, under the run's lock and after the checks above, records `gate: {file: {path, sha256}}` with the approval, and adds `The approved note is <path>, sha256 <hash>.` to the next step's instructions; a note that is missing or can't be read is refused with `gate_note_unreadable`. With `--note-sha256 HEX`, a note whose hash is not `HEX` is refused with `stale_revision` (`details.revision` its sha256 now, `details.path` the note, `details.run_id`, `details.step` and `details.attempt` the gate), and a gate with no `note:` line with `gate_note_unreadable`; the hash recorded is the one compared, so what is recorded is what the caller read. After the approval, the step's row in `steps` has `gate_file: {path, sha256}`, the hash approve recorded, and `note_changed: true` when the note's bytes differ now (or it can no longer be read). When the gated step's first file is missing, a plain `approve` is recorded without a hash, as for a gate with no `note:` line.

Every refusal leaves the run file as it was.

## Where a run is stuck

A run that is not moving has a `reason`: a `code`, `since`, `details`, and `actions`, the commands that resolve it. A guard stop also offers `resume RUN --accept-guard --note ...` and `--accept-guard --retry --note ...` (`needs: ["note"]`), beside `cancel`. One a resume can pass once the checkout is put back also has `recovery`, a string saying what to put back first (for `head_moved`, move HEAD back to the first 12 characters of `base_sha`); it is absent for every other reason, and for `no_commit` with no `base_sha`. Every `actions` entry has `action`, `args` and `needs`:

- `needs: []`: run `workspace <action> <args...>` as written.
- Otherwise the caller supplies one value per entry of `needs` (`name`, `flag`) and appends it after `args`: the flag then the value, or `--` then the value when `flag` is `null`. `workflow reject` needs `{"name":"note","flag":"--note"}`; an action that would be refused with `attempts_spent`, or with `step_attempts_spent` (the step it would start an attempt of has spent its own cap), also lists `{"name":"extra_attempts","flag":"--extra-attempts","min":N}`, where the value must be a whole number of at least `min`, the larger of the run's and that step's shortfall (`min` is only on this need); one refused with `budget_spent` lists `{"name":"extra_tokens","flag":"--extra-tokens","min":N,"suggested":M}` the same way, and one refused with `run_timed_out` (or `budget_spent` with the time spent too) also lists `{"name":"extra_time","flag":"--extra-time","min":N,"suggested":M}` in seconds; and `ask answer` needs `{"name":"answer","flag":null}`. No placeholder is ever in `args`; the text output shows one for a person (`workspace workflow reject RUN --note NOTE`).

| Code | Meaning | `details` |
|---|---|---|
| `waiting_lock` | Queued for a lock a step uses | `resource`, `step`, `position`, `total`, `holder` (`kind`; `run_id`, `step`, `workspace`, `worktree` for a run; `pid` and `worktree` for an agent or a dev environment, `kind: "process"`, which adds `dev down` to `actions`); `actions` also hold `lock status RESOURCE --name WS` when `resource` is a plain name |
| `waiting_you` | A person is needed. `kind` says for what: `gate` (a passed step waits for approval), `prompt` (the pane shows a permission prompt), `ask` (a question asked from the run's pane with [`ask`](README.ask.md) is open), `dispatch` (the step's line could not be typed), `delivery` (the process typing the step's line stopped before the agent daemon answered, and the pane's agent has ended its turn: it may never have had the line) | `kind`, `step`; `artifacts`, `gate_file` and `gate_file_error` (see [Passing a gate](#passing-a-gate)) for a gate; `pane`, `message` for a prompt; `ask`, `question` for a question; `error`, `message`, `pane`, `workspace` for a dispatch; `attempt`, `pane`, `workspace` for a delivery |
| `turn_ended_incomplete` | The turn ended and a file the step produces is missing, is not a non-empty regular file (a symlink, a directory or an empty file), or was left by an earlier attempt and not written again. The next turn's end, or `workflow resume`, looks again. A fresh one is hidden for its first 10 seconds and while the pane is working (the run reads `running`, with no reason), so a real stop can show up to 10 seconds late: a task that settled just before the turn ended starts a new turn on its own. After 90 minutes of a hold (below) it is stored in place of `turn_ended_background`, with `background_tasks` | `step`, `attempt`, `missing`, `stale`, `invalid` (only when a file broke a rule: a list of `{file, rule}`, `rule` being `symlink`, `not_regular_file` or `empty`); `background_tasks` (`id`, `type`) after a hold ran out |
| `turn_ended_background` | The turn ended with background tasks still listed (sub-agents, shells, monitors) and a file the step produces is missing, is not a non-empty regular file, or is stale. Each task that ends starts another turn, so this is not a stop. `workflow status` never shows it: the run reads `running` with no reason and `flags: ["background_work"]`. Only the event log's `workflow_changed` carries the code | `step`, `attempt`, `missing`, `stale`, `invalid` (as for `turn_ended_incomplete`), `tasks` (`id`, `type`) |
| `failed_check` | The step failed and has no loop left | `step`, `attempt`, `cause` (`check`, `reported`, `guard` or `require`), `require`, `detail`, `unproven`, `required`, `found`, `base`, `reason` (`history_rewritten`), `exit_code` and `log` (`command`, `exec`), `program` (`exec`), `before` and `after` (`remote_unchanged`) (a `require` failure; `error` also names a proof that raised), `code`, `code_source`, `guard` and `paths` (a guard failure), `exit_code`, `timed_out`, `log`, `error` (a check that could not be run, or was stopped), `summary`, `loops`, `attempts`, `max_attempts`, `limit` (`step` only when the `on_fail` goto step has used its own `max_attempts` plus `extra_attempts`; absent for other stops), `limit_step` (that step's id) |
| `budget_spent` | The next attempt or review round would start with the run's token budget used; it was not started (see [Token budgets](#token-budgets)) | `kind: "budget"`, `step`, `review_round` (a held round), `used`, `allowance`, `tokens`, `extra_tokens`, `counted`, `uncounted`, `estimated`, `needed`, `suggested`; with the run's timeout also spent, `run_timed_out` holds the time need (`kind: "timeout"`, `needed`, `suggested`, `used`, `limit`, in seconds), so pass `--extra-time` too |
| `run_timed_out` | The next attempt or review round would start with the run's `timeout:` used; it was not started (see [Run timeouts](#run-timeouts)) | `kind: "timeout"`, `step`, `review_round` (a held round), `used`, `limit`, `timeout`, `extra_seconds`, `needed`, `suggested` (seconds) |
| `retry_backoff` | A failed step's `on_fail` retry waits out its `backoff`; a tick, `workflow sweep` or `workflow resume` starts it | `step`, `attempt`, `to` (the step the retry goes back to), `until`, `seconds`, `loops` |
| `paused` | A person paused the run with `workflow pause`; nothing new starts until `workflow unpause`. It shows before every other reason, a gate's `waiting_you` too | `by`, `text` (the `--reason`, when given), `since`, and `details.held` (the reason the pause is holding back, when there is one). Actions: `unpause`, `cancel` |
| `review_rejected` | A review round of a step with a `review` rejected the step's work and the step can't run again by itself: it has spent its `max_rounds`, or the run or the step has spent its `max_attempts` (see [The rejection loop](#the-rejection-loop)); the step and its attempt are `failed` | `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `reviews`, `rounds`, `max_rounds`, `limit` (`run`, `step` or `rounds`), and for `run` and `step` `attempts` and `max_attempts` |
| `review_rounds_spent` | A step reached a review with every round of its `max_rounds` spent (by earlier attempts, through a gate's `reject`, `resume --from` or an `on_fail` loop), so no round starts (`resume --extra-rounds N` adds rounds); the step stays `reviewing` with the round `held` | `step`, `attempt`, `round` (the round that would have started), `rounds`, `max_rounds` |
| `review_incomplete` | A review round ended without a decision: a verdict that is missing or does not count could still change the outcome, the round's directory could not be made (see [Verdict files](#verdict-files)), or its revision could not be computed again ([A review round's revision](#a-review-rounds-revision)); the step stays `reviewing` | `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `reviews`, `problem` (`no counted verdict from NAME (PROBLEM), ...`) |
| `review_stale` | What a review round judged changed while the round ran: its revision computed again when the round ended differs from the one it was delivered with (see [A review round's revision](#a-review-rounds-revision)). The verdicts are not read; the step stays `reviewing` and the round's record ends `stale`. Never loops: `resume` starts a fresh round on the new revision, which spends a round | `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `revision` (delivered), `revision_now` |
| `review_guard` | The checkout changed while a review round ran: HEAD moved, or a tracked file differs from the snapshot taken as the round started (see [A review round's guard](#a-review-rounds-guard)). The verdicts are not read; the step stays `reviewing` and the round's record ends `review_guard`. Never loops: `resume` starts a fresh round on the checkout as it is, which spends a round | `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `changed`, `snapshot_head`, `head` |
| `review_tampered` | A review round could not start because something was already at its directory's path (or the path was reached through a link), so nothing in it could be counted; the round is spent and the step stays `reviewing`. `resume` starts a fresh round | `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `problem` |
| `pane_gone` | The run's pane, or its workspace, is not running | `step`, `pane`, `workspace`; when it was found while starting a step (stored), also `kind: "dispatch"`, `error` and `message`. A delivery answered `no_such_pane` (or `pane_gone`) is recorded as `pane_gone` only when tmux itself no longer lists the pane; if it still does, the reason is `waiting_you` with `error: "no_such_pane"` |

`pane_gone` and the `prompt`, `ask` and `delivery` kinds of `waiting_you` are read from tmux, the agent daemon and the question store when `status` runs; the others are stored in the run. A step past its `timeout` gets `flags: ["timed_out"]` and keeps running. A step that reads `running` with no reason while its pane's agent has ended its turn (the daemon shows it `idle`, or `done` for ten seconds) gets `flags: ["idle"]`: the turn ended and nothing decided the step (an interrupted turn, a daemon that was down, an `advance` that died). The flag is withheld for 30 seconds after the attempt starts, because a pane reads idle for a moment after a dispatch. Run `workflow resume`, which decides the step as the turn's end would have.

A turn that ends with background tasks listed does not stop the run. Claude Code 2.1.145 and later list them in the `Stop` hook input (`background_tasks`, each with a `type` and an `id`); `session-event` forwards only the types `subagent`, `workflow`, `shell`, `monitor`, `MCP task` and `cloud session` and ids of word characters and dashes, and the daemon passes them to `workflow advance` as `--background-task TYPE:ID` (repeatable; internal, not meant to be typed). If files the step produces are still missing, the run stores `turn_ended_background` and keeps `running`; files present, or a step reported failed, decide the step as before. A hold lasts 90 minutes from the last `Stop` that listed tasks. After that the daemon's timer turns it into `turn_ended_incomplete`, with the tasks named in the notice, so a task that never ends or an agent that died cannot hide a stopped run for good; with no daemon running nothing ends the hold, but the run then reads `stuck` in `workspace goal`. Codex and older Claude Code versions send no list and behave as before. A held run has no reason and no `idle` flag, so workspace-ui offers no Resume; a person who knows the agent is dead runs `workspace workflow resume RUN`, which acts on a held run.

A step's attempt is stored, running and bound to its pane, before its line is typed; the line is typed after the run's lock is given up, because the agent daemon may take up to two and a half minutes to answer a fresh-conversation step. A process that dies meanwhile (a `workflow run` that is killed) leaves a run whose next turn's end decides the step as usual. While the line may still be on its way (the daemon's time limit plus a minute after the attempt starts) the step gets `flags: ["delivering"]` instead of `idle`, and `resume` leaves it alone (outcome `unchanged`), so two resumes never type it twice; only `--from` or `--pane` types it again then. After that, a pane whose agent has ended its turn reads `waiting_you` with kind `delivery`: look at the pane, then `workflow resume` decides the step from its files and check as a turn's end would, and `workflow resume --from STEP` types it again in a new attempt.

`workflow resume` does what the reason calls for: asks for the lock again, runs a failed or undelivered step again, looks again at a step whose files were missing, and moves the run to the workspace's Claude pane (or `--pane`) when its own pane is gone. For a step that reads `running` it asks the daemon whether the pane's agent has ended its turn, and decides the step if it has. A resume that does nothing moves no pane and keeps no `--note`. It leaves a step an agent is working on, a step whose line is still on its way, and a check that is still running, alone (outcome `unchanged`), and refuses a run at a gate (code `gate_waiting`) and, without `--accept-guard`, a run stopped on the `no_commit` guard that recorded no base commit at start (code `guard_cannot_pass`; accept it or run `workflow cancel`). Any other guard stop (`head_moved`, `hooks_changed`, `hidden_changed`, `no_commit` with a base) resumes once the checkout is put back; `workflow status --json` gives the step in the reason's `recovery`. It keeps the run in its pane unless that pane is gone.

On a step that reads `reviewing` (see the step key `review`), `resume` never starts the author again: a round is not an attempt, so it asks no `max_attempts` allowance. A round still running is left alone (outcome `unchanged`). A round the token budget or the run's timeout held starts on a resume with `--extra-tokens` or `--extra-time` ([A held review round](#a-held-review-round)). A round that stopped for a person (`review_incomplete`, `review_stale`, or any other stored reason) is ended and a fresh round starts, which spends one of the step's rounds; every ended round's record goes on the reviewed attempt's `rounds` (see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)). A round whose delivery failed or lapsed is delivered again as the same round (the lapsed delivery gets its own `rounds` record, `ended: redelivered`). The `resume --json` row does not say which (its `run` is the status row, which shows no round yet), so after it a script reads the run's history (`events_path` in `workflow status RUN --json`): the newest `review_started` line has `again: true` for a delivery again and no `again` for a fresh round, and its `round` is the round's number (a fresh round's is one above the last). The run file's `steps.<step>.review_rounds` rises only for a fresh round. `resume --from STEP` ends the round first and closes its review window, then starts the step named; neither it nor a gate's `reject` resets the step's `review_rounds`. A round that rejects runs the author again by itself (see [The rejection loop](#the-rejection-loop)); one stopped by a limit leaves the step `failed`, and `resume` runs the author again in a new attempt, held to the attempt allowance as any is. Once the step has spent its `max_rounds`, a resume that would start another round (after `review_incomplete`, `review_stale`, `review_guard` or `review_tampered`, or at the round held as `review_rounds_spent`) or run again a step whose last round rejected it is refused with `review_rounds_spent` and changes nothing, unless `--extra-rounds N` adds N to the step's rounds: N must be at least `details.needed` of the refusal (the rounds made minus the allowance, plus one), the step's `extra_rounds` in the run file and `review_rounds_extended` in its history record it, and the round (or the author's retry of a rejected step) then starts, with the allowance kept for later rounds. `--extra-rounds` is for `resume` alone, applies to the step the resume goes to (`--from STEP`'s, else the current one), is a usage error for a step with no `review:` or with `--accept-guard`, and is ignored (nothing added, no event) when the resume does nothing or the step it goes to has rounds left. With `--from STEP` a spent reviewed STEP needs a covering N too (a short one is `review_rounds_spent` with `details.needed`), though `--from` alone still goes on. `resume --from` still goes on without it, but the step's next review stops the same way, and `cancel` ends the run. Needs `workflow` 37. `workflow cancel` (and a workspace `kill`) ends the round the same way, recording its usage, and closes its review window; the reviewed attempt has already ended (`checks_passed`), so no attempt is left open. A resume run from the run's own pane is not parked while the step is being reviewed: a plain `resume` types into the review pane only, so it is carried out at once, and `resume --from` there is refused (code `step_reviewing`), since a park would hold back the review pane's turn end; run it from another terminal.

A run waiting for a lock needs no `resume`: the workspace's agent daemon asks again every 15 seconds. The same timer starts an `on_fail` retry when its `backoff` is over; `resume` starts it now.

A turn that ends on an API error does not end the step: while the pane's agent shows `turn_failed` in [`sessions --json`](README.sessions.md), `workflow resume` (without `--pane`) leaves the run as it is, and the step stays running. The agent daemon [recovers the agent](README.agentd.md#recovering-from-an-api-error) and the step goes on when a turn completes. A new prompt typed in the pane clears the failed turn too, and so does `workflow resume --pane PANE`, which sends the step to that pane again. While the failed agent is gone and the pane shows a shell (the daemon stopped it to restart it), that send is refused as `pane_busy`: wait for the resumed agent.

**When the sweep records the stall.** `workflow sweep` records `step_stalled` with cause `turn_failed` (once for each attempt) when recovery has given up on the failure, or when the failure is older than `workflows.turn_failed_after` (default `30m`; `off` or `0` turns the time limit off). Recovery counts as given up when its entry for the pane's session has a `gave_up_at` at or after the failure; an older give-up does not count. The age is measured from the latest failure, and recovery clears and stamps it again each time it retypes, so a limit shorter than the 15 minutes recovery can wait between attempts can stall a run while recovery is still retrying. A step past its `timeout` with `on_timeout: fail` is failed at once (`step_timed_out`, then `on_fail`); with `interrupt` the sweep only records the stall and presses nothing. Once recorded, the stall stops the step, run and round clocks (see [Active time](#active-time)), so a step not yet past its `timeout` then is not timed out while it stays stalled: it waits for a person in `workflow status --needs-me`. The stall reaches `alerts.workflow` `stalled`, `workflow status --needs-me` (code `stalled`) and the derived inbox. To move the run on, restart the agent so it can work again or send the step to a working pane with `workflow resume --pane PANE`; a plain `workflow resume` leaves the run alone while the turn is failed.

### Restarting a crashed step

`workflow resume RUN --restart-step` is for a step whose agent crashed or whose pane went away, so its attempt never ended (needs `workflow` 48). A plain `resume` types the step's line again into the same conversation. A restart instead:

1. ends the step's unfinished attempt with the outcome `interrupted`. Its tokens are read from the old pane's session and counted once, in the budget and in `workflow stats`; `workflow compare` does not count it as a miss.
2. starts a fresh attempt of the step, with `retry_of` set to the interrupted one, in a fresh conversation (`/clear`). Its prompt begins with a fenced brief: HEAD now, HEAD when the interrupted attempt started, and the plan's done and open tasks. The brief is data, so text from the plan file cannot give it orders. Your `--note` follows the brief.

The new attempt is a real attempt: it counts toward `max_attempts` and the token budget, and `--extra-attempts`, `--extra-tokens` and `--extra-time` work as they do for any resume.

The pane is chosen in this order: the pane named with `--pane`; else the run's bound pane when its agent is idle or its turn failed; else the single idle Claude Code pane that no run, review or the caller uses. Nothing is changed when a check fails; each refusal says so:

| Code | Cause |
|---|---|
| `no_open_attempt` | the step is not running, or its last attempt has ended; a plain `resume` moves it on |
| `step_still_working` | the agent is still working (a busy bound pane gives this, `details.reason` `working` or `unknown`), or the step's line is still being typed; wait and run it again |
| `pane_ambiguous` | several idle Claude Code panes could take it; name one with `--pane` (`details.panes`) |
| `no_agent_pane` | no idle Claude Code pane is free, or the pane named with `--pane` is not Claude Code; start one or name one |
| `pane_busy` | a pane named with `--pane` is mid-turn, the caller's, or a review pane |
| `pane_has_run` | the chosen pane was bound to another run |
| `restart_not_closed` | the open attempt could not be closed, so no attempt was started; run it again |
| `attempts_spent`, `step_attempts_spent`, `budget_spent`, `run_timed_out` | a limit is spent; add `--extra-attempts`, `--extra-tokens` or `--extra-time` |
| `bound_pane` | run from the run's own pane; a step is restarted by a person, from another pane |
| `usage` | given with `--from`, `--accept-guard`, `--retry` or `--extra-rounds` |

The event `step_interrupted` (`step`, `attempt`, `pane`, `usage_counted`) records the close, and `run_resumed` carries `restart_step: true`. When a reboot recovery makes the restart, the run's `reboot_recoveries` count rises by one and the event `reboot_recovered` (`workspace`, `outcome` `restarted`, `count` the new total) is written with it. A plain `resume` of a step that looks crashed still works, and prints a `hint` naming `--restart-step` on stderr, in text and in `--json` mode; with `--json` it is also the `hint` field of the result row. `workflow status` and `workflow sweep` list `workflow resume RUN --restart-step` among the `next_actions` of a running step whose open attempt has a `pane_gone` or `agent_gone` stall.

### Resuming from the run's own pane

An agent that runs `workflow resume` (or `workflow run`) in the pane the step would be typed into can't be cleared during its own turn: the old path waited 120 seconds on that turn and gave up. The command now stores the request on the run instead and answers outcome `parked` (exit 0), with `parked: {pane, expires_at}` in its row. The `message` tells the caller to end its turn now; a sub-agent should finish and let the main agent end its turn. Nothing is typed, bound or locked yet, and no attempt exists, so a gate or lock the run had keeps what it had.

Once the turn in that pane is proven over, the agent daemon runs `workflow advance` as a process of its own, outside the pane, and the resume is carried out: `/clear` for a fresh step, then the step's line (a `context: continue` step gets the line alone). It starts from the turn's `Stop`, or from the daemon's 15 second timer. A `workflow run` parked in a pane with no binding yet starts on the timer, so it can take up to 15 seconds.

`status` shows a parked run with the flag `parked` and `current.parked` (`pane`, `from`, `requested_at`, `expires_at`; never the note). The run's stored reason is not shown while the park lasts, except for a prompt or question waiting on a person. The run file keeps a `resume_parked` event and, when it ends, `resume_parked_ended` (`how`: `released`, `unchanged`, `failed`, `taken_over`, `moved_on`, `expired`, `ended` or `paused`).

`workflow resume` ends by saying, in one sentence, what the run waits for, and `--json` carries it as `waits` (needs `workflow` 47): `kind` `parked` (the park's `pane` and `expires_at`; also what a second resume says while a park is pending), `backoff` (the retry's `until`), `pane_busy` (the pane and why its line was refused, as in [Why a line was refused](#why-a-line-was-refused)) or `nothing` (the resume did nothing and nothing else is pending; the text output shows its "Nothing was done" message instead), each with the sentence as `text`. In the text output that sentence is the only place the pane, the time and the refusal appear: the `parked:` and `refused:` lines and the `retry_backoff` reason line `workflow status` shows are left out, and the message does not repeat them. `waits` is null when the resume went through.

The turn counts as over only when all of this holds, read from the daemon's snapshot, the transcript and the screen:

- The pane runs Claude Code with hooks, the daemon heard the main agent's `Stop`, and the transcript shows nothing from the main agent after it (a background task's notice can wake the agent after a `Stop`; nothing is typed until that turn has ended).
- No tool is open, no wait or failed turn stands, and the screen shows the prompt with an empty input box and no spinner or other sign of a running turn. A draft stops the release until it is cleared, which is how a person steps in; so does `workflow cancel`.
- With no `Stop` to go by (after Esc, or a daemon restarted since the `Stop`), the pane must read idle with no hook event for 30 seconds. A pressed Esc counts as the turn ending once the screen has been still for 30 seconds, and the conversation is then cleared.

What can go wrong:

- **Time limit.** A park waits at most 1 hour. Past it the run reads `waiting_you`, `kind: dispatch`, error `parked_timeout`, in `status` and in `inbox list --include-derived` (worked out when read, so it shows with no daemon too; a daemon that is up records it). A `--from` or `--pane` on that resume was not applied, and a note is kept for the next attempt. A step still running is decided as usual when its turn ends. A check that finished while the run was parked is not recorded; resume again to run it. A background job that keeps redrawing the pane after Esc keeps the screen moving and ends here.
- **A pane that is gone.** If the pane closed, or its agent quit (a shell for 30 seconds), the park ends the same way with `pane_gone` or `not_an_agent`, and nothing is typed into the shell.
- **A failed release.** A release that can't type the line is parked again, up to 3 times within the same hour, when the code is `pane_busy`, `restart_in_progress`, `clear_not_confirmed` (the `/clear` was typed but not confirmed) or `no_such_pane` for a pane tmux still lists. A pane tmux no longer lists is `pane_gone` and is never parked again. A re-park has no timer or backoff of its own: it keeps the park's original expiry and is released at the next turn end or `advance` tick, so the 3 tries and the 1 hour are the only bounds. Each try is an attempt row (`code` on the row says why it failed), but a re-park is not held to `max_attempts` or the token budget again: only the first release is, so a run can pass its allowance by up to 3 attempts this way. After the last try the run waits as `waiting_you`. Any other failure, such as `not_an_agent`, ends the park and the run waits as `waiting_you` (`dispatch`) with the error. A failure has no caller to read it, so it also goes to the daemon's log and the `inbox` shows a `workflow_undelivered` row.
- **`question_open` after Esc.** Claude Code sends no hook when Esc closes an `AskUserQuestion` or `ExitPlanMode`, so the tool stays open and any wait its notification opened stays too; installing the hooks again changes nothing. The daemon reads the pane instead: the normal input box, empty, with no spinner over it and no dialog hint under it, unchanged for 8 seconds with no hook event (two scans at the default interval). Then it closes the tool and the wait. Until then a release waits, and `agent-run restart` is refused with `question_open`; `--force` does not get past it. So after Esc a park releases within about a minute, and an open dialog, which never draws that box (as measured on Claude Code 2.1.316), is left alone. A status line that keeps moving, or a background sub-agent firing hook events, can hold a parked release after Esc until `parked_timeout`, because a release with no Stop needs a still pane. A draft in the box keeps the refusal until it is cleared.
- **A daemon from before this change** has no `features` key in its snapshot, so the resume takes the old path and fails as it did. Run `workspace daemon restart` once. See [`agentd`](README.agentd.md#workflow-runs).
- **A pane outside window 0** can't be parked: the daemon scans window 0 only. The resume takes the old path.

A second own-pane resume while one is parked changes nothing: it answers `unchanged` and keeps the first request (a `--from`, `--note` or `--pane` on the second is dropped, and the message says so). A resume from another pane takes over from the park, and `cancel` ends it. A resume from a sub-agent of the run's pane parks the same way, and the proof is already there if the main agent is idle.

A resume from another pane over a screen that keeps moving does not park. It waits for the pane as `agent-run restart` does (see [its notes on a moving screen](README.agent-run.md#restart-subcommand)), and when the turn is proven over it types `/clear` anyway. The step's `step_dispatched` event then carries a `warning` that background work may be lost, and `proof_missing` names what was missing when the wait failed. A background shell or sub-agent that was running can be lost when `/clear` goes through. A different `warning` appears when the daemon's monitor, from hooks, saw a sub-agent started and not yet ended in the pane's Claude Code agent as `/clear` was typed, even with the main turn over (a resume run by a background sub-agent while the main agent idles is the usual case): "a sub-agent was still running in pane %N when /clear was typed and may be lost"; `/clear` can end that sub-agent, and the clear is not held back. The moving-screen text is "background work in pane %N was still running when /clear was typed and may be lost"; when both apply only the sub-agent text is used. Either warning is in the `step_dispatched` event only, not in `workflow status --json`. The monitor can't see a background shell, so a shell running alone gives no warning, though it can be lost too.

A run file in `runs/` that can't be read as a run (not JSON, not readable, naming another id than its file's, or with a name that is not a run id, which no verb can name) is not shown; `status` names the file in `warnings`. The lock store still counts that run as going, so what it held stays held: free a lock with `workspace lock clear NAME`, or delete the file. So is one without a state for every step of its definition. Naming such a run to any verb answers `unknown_run` with the file in `details.path`.

## JSON output

`workflow run`, `start`, `resume`, `cancel`, `approve`, `reject` and `step done` print one [action document](README.json.md#actions). Its one result row has `workspace`, `outcome`, `reason` (the reason code, or `null`), `message`, and `run`, the run as `status` shows it (including `worktree`, the stored checkout path or `null`):

```json
{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"action":"workflow run","status":"ok",
 "results":[{"workspace":"api.worktree-pdf","outcome":"started","reason":null,"message":null,
   "run":{"id":"wr_2610041230559x3k","workflow":"rpiv","state":"running","current":{"step":"research","attempt":1,"state":"running","reason":null,"flags":[]}}}],
 "warnings":[],"summary":{"started":1}}
```

`outcome` is `started`, `resumed`, `parked` (a `resume`, `run` or in-place `go` from the pane it would type into: stored, to be carried out once the agent ends its turn; the row also has `parked` with `pane` and `expires_at`, exit 0; see [Resuming from the run's own pane](#resuming-from-the-runs-own-pane)), `unchanged` (a `resume` that did nothing: an agent is working on the step, its line is still on its way, its check is still running, or a resume is already parked; `message` says which, exit 0), `cancelled`, `approved`, `rejected` or `recorded` (`step done`). A plain `resume` of a step that looks crashed adds a `hint` (a sentence naming `resume --restart-step`; needs `workflow` 48). A run that waits (for a lock, at a gate) still has that outcome, with the reason code in `reason`. When a step's line could not be typed the outcome is `failed`, `status` is `failed` and the exit code is 1; the run exists, `message` says why, and `workflow resume` tries again. `workflow start` prints the same action document; its row is the `run` row merged with `branch`, `path`, `created`, `task`, `ticket` (`key`, `title`, `url`; `null` when none was read) and `warnings`. `start --dry-run` prints `status: "dry_run"` with a row of `workflow`, `step`, `project`, `branch`, `path`, `would_create` (`false` when an existing workspace would be reused), `inputs`, `packs`, `warnings` and `ticket`. `run --dry-run` prints `status: "dry_run"` with a row of `workflow`, `step`, `pane` (`null` when the agent daemon is not running yet), `inputs`, `packs` and `inputs_from`, and its `message` is the printed "Would start ..." line, never `null`. The rows of `workflow run` (dry run, started and parked) carry `inputs_from`, the inputs filled from the branch and where each came from (`{}` when none was; see [Filling inputs from the branch](#filling-inputs-from-the-branch)). `step done`'s row has `run_id`, `step`, `attempt` and `reported` (`status`, `pass` or `fail`, and `summary`, as `step status` gives it) in place of `run`. `run_id` is always a run's id, in rows, events and error `details`; `run` is only the run object in an action row.

`workflow status --json` (each run also carries `worktree`, the stored checkout path or `null`; the example leaves it out):

```json
{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"generated_at":"2026-10-04T21:14:03Z","runs":[
 {"id":"wr_2610041230559x3k","workflow":"rpiv","title":"Research, Plan, Implement, Verify",
  "workspace":"api.worktree-pdf","project":"api","state":"waiting",
  "created_at":"2026-10-04T20:30:55Z","started_at":"2026-10-04T20:30:55Z","ended_at":null,"cancelled_by":null,
  "current":{"step":"verify","attempt":null,"state":"waiting",
    "reason":{"code":"waiting_lock","since":"2026-10-04T21:02:40Z",
      "details":{"resource":"test-db","step":"verify","position":1,"total":2,
        "holder":{"run_id":"wr_261004121500ab12","step":"verify","workspace":"api.worktree-tax","worktree":"/src/api-tax","kind":"run"}},
      "actions":[{"action":"workflow status","args":["wr_261004121500ab12"],"needs":[]},{"action":"workflow cancel","args":["wr_2610041230559x3k"],"needs":[]}]},
    "flags":[]},
  "steps":[{"id":"research","title":"Research","state":"passed","attempts":1,"gate":null,"gate_note":null,"gate_file":null,"note_changed":false},
    {"id":"plan","title":"Plan","state":"passed","attempts":1,"gate":"approved","gate_note":null,"gate_file":null,"note_changed":false},
    {"id":"implement","title":"Implement","state":"passed","attempts":2,"gate":null,"gate_note":null,"gate_file":null,"note_changed":false},
    {"id":"verify","title":"Verify","state":"waiting","attempts":0,"gate":null,"gate_note":null,"gate_file":null,"note_changed":false}],
  "base_sha":"3b1d2c4e5f60718293a4b5c6d7e8f9012a3b4c5d","pane":"%19","task":"3f9a1c2e","inputs":{"task":"PROJ-101 invoice PDF export","spec":""},"loops":{"verify->implement":1},
  "artifacts_dir":"/src/api-pdf/.workflow/wr_2610041230559x3k",
  "events_path":"/Users/me/.local/state/workspace/.workflows/runs/wr_2610041230559x3k.events.jsonl"}],
 "warnings":[]}
```

- A run's `state` is `running`, `waiting`, `completed` or `cancelled` (`cancelled_by` is `cancel` or `kill`). A run with a reason reads `waiting`; a stored `turn_ended_incomplete` hidden by a working pane or by its 10-second hold-back, and a `turn_ended_background` hold, leave `state` `running` with no reason.
- `current.flags` lists what `status` read about the step now: `timed_out` (past its `timeout`), `delivering` (its line is still on its way to the agent), `parked` (a resume is stored for later), `idle` (the turn ended unseen and the pane is quiet; `workflow resume` decides it) and `background_work` (a `turn_ended_background` hold). It is `[]` when none applies. For a step being reviewed `timed_out` counts from the round's start against `review.timeout` (the step's own `timeout` stopped at the author's turn end) and `delivering` is the round's delivery mark.
- `current.turn_failed` is present only while the step is `running` and its pane's agent turn ended on an API error (needs `workflow` 44): `since` (ISO time of the latest failure, or `null`), `attempts` (recovery attempts so far, or `null` when recovery has no entry for the session), `max_attempts` (integer) and `gave_up` (boolean). The text status prints `turn failed 12m ago (recovery 4 of 12)`, `(recovery gave up)` or `(no recovery)`. `workflow sweep` records the stall once `gave_up` is true or `workflows.turn_failed_after` has passed (see [Sweeping stopped runs](#sweeping-stopped-runs)); `current.stuck` then shows it while the pane is not `working`.
- `current.infra_retry` is `null` unless the running step has an automatic restart pending, under way or given up (needs `workflow` 50): `cause` (`agent_gone` or `pane_gone`), `n` (this restart's number), `max` (integer), `due` (ISO time, or `null` when it cannot be read), `state` (`scheduled`: waiting or due; `restarting`: claimed by a `workflow advance`; `exhausted`: given up) and `reason` (the give-up reason, e.g. `retries_spent`; `null` unless `state` is `exhausted`). It also shows while the run is paused, since a pause holds the restart. The stall's reason stays `stalled`. The text status prints one line: `step restarts on its own: restart 1 of 2 in 4m` (`due now` once due; `step restart held (run paused): ...` when paused), `restarting the step now (restart 1 of 2)`, `automatic restarts used up (agent exited): resume, resume --restart-step, or cancel`, or `automatic restart stopped (agent exited): WHY`. See [Automatic restarts](#automatic-restarts).
- `current.review` is present only while the step is `reviewing`, stored a round and the run's stored definition still has a `review:` for that step (a finished run never carries it): `round`, `max_rounds` (the allowance: the definition's plus `extra_rounds`) and `extra_rounds` (what `resume --extra-rounds` added to the step; needs `workflow` 37), `need` and `must`, `approvals` (counted from the verdict rows a stopped round read) and `pending` (the reviewers without a verdict yet), `pane` and `window` of the review window (`null` until the round is delivered), `delivery`, `started_at`, `stopped` (the round ended without deciding), `held` (the reason that kept a round from starting) and `next_round` (the round a resume starts: the held round itself, a round `review_no_change` stopped, or the stopped one plus one; `null` for a round under way), `rerun` (the reviewers a rerun of the round runs again, else `null`; needs `workflow` 38), and `reviews`, the verdict rows with `reviewer`, `verdict`, `revision`, `counted`, `problem` and `isolation` (`review_pane`). Each step row also gains `review_rounds` once the step has spent a round. While a round is under way `status` reads the review pane, not the author's: a permission prompt in it, or an open question asked from it, reads `waiting_you` with `details.role` `reviewer` and a `focus` action for that pane. A stopped round (`review_incomplete`, `review_tampered`, `review_stale`, `review_guard`, `review_rejected`, `review_no_change`) offers `resume` (a fresh round on the checkout as it is now, or after a rejection a new attempt), `resume --from STEP` and `cancel`; `reject` needs a waiting gate, so it is not offered. A run stored before reviews existed has none of these keys. Needs `workflow` 34.
- A step's `state` is `pending`, `waiting` (not started: a lock, or a line that could not be typed), `running`, `checking` (its check, or a `require:` proof that runs outside the run's lock, is running), `reviewing` (its attempt ended `checks_passed` and a review round decides it; see the step key `review`), `passed` or `failed`. `gate` is `waiting`, `approved`, `rejected` or `null`. `gate_note` is the text the person gave to `approve` or `reject` with `--note`, as stored, or `null` when none was given (and for a run stored before the field was shown); it is the same text the next attempt's instructions carry under `## For this attempt`. `gate_file` is `{path, sha256}` as approve recorded it, or `null`; `note_changed` is true when that note's bytes differ now (see [Passing a gate](#passing-a-gate)).
- `warnings` are strings: a workspace's agent daemon did not answer, so a permission prompt would not show; a run will not move because its workspace has no agent daemon; a run file can't be read or shown.
- `guard_warnings` lists what the guard warned of on every attempt, oldest first: `{code, at, message, step, attempt}`. The run went on past each.
- `events_path` is the run's own history, one JSON line per transition (`run_started`, `resume_parked` and `resume_parked_ended` (a resume stored for later and how it ended), `run_paused` (a person paused the run: `step`, `text` when given, and `interrupt: true` when `--interrupt` found the agent working and its keys are pressed), `pause_interrupt_failed` (the press failed, so no continue line will be typed: `step`, `attempt`, `pane`, `message`), `run_unpaused` (the pause ended: `step`, `action` (`dispatch`, `round`, `rerun`, `deliver`, `pane_gone`, `continue` (one continue line typed into the turn `--interrupt` stopped), `continue_pending` (the pane is not yet idle; the daemon's tick types the line once it is, for up to an hour), `evaluate` or `none`: what the unpause did) and `held` (`attempt`, `round` or `delivery`: the kind of dispatch the pause held, absent when none)), `continue_pending_ended` (the tick took a pending continue line: `step`, `attempt`, `pane`, `how` `pane_idle` (the line is typed), `pane_replaced` (nothing typed) or `expired` (the pane did not prove idle within an hour; nothing typed, and a step still stuck is left to the sweep)), `step_continued` (a continue line was typed or not: `step`, `attempt`, `pane`, `delivered`, and when not typed `code` (`run_paused` when the run was paused again first, `moved_on` when it ended or its attempt was decided first), `message` and `pending` (true when the line was kept for the daemon's tick to try again), plus `reason` and the `seen_*` keys when refused as `pane_busy`), `guard_overridden` (a person accepted a guard stop: guard, old and new base, note, pane), `guard_warning` (the guard warned of a changed config; `step`, `attempt`, `code`, `inbox` true when the item was written), `step_dispatching` (the attempt is stored and its line about to be typed), `step_dispatched` (the daemon answered; `delivered` says whether the line landed, when refused as `pane_busy` `error`, `message`, `reason` and `seen_*` keys for what the pane showed (`seen_state`, `seen_state_since`, `seen_changed_by`, `seen_turn_failed`, `seen_turn_failed_since`, `seen_turn_failed_kind`, `seen_kind`, `seen_last_event_at`, `seen_refusal_reason`, `seen_at`, `seen_read_after`; each only when known), `warning` when background work may have been lost to the `/clear` (a moving screen, or a running sub-agent), `proof_missing` when a moving screen wasn't accepted), `step_reported`, `check_finished`, `proof_finished` (a `require:` proof run outside the lock: `proof`, `passed`), `step_checks_passed` (a step with a `review` passed its own checks), `review_started` and `review_ended` (a review round: `step`, `attempt`, `round`; `review_ended` with `outcome` `approved`, `rejected` or `incomplete`, or `cancelled`, `from` or `resumed` for a round a person ended before it was decided; `review_started` with `again: true` for a round delivered again), `review_retry` (a rejected step run again: `step`, `attempt` (the rejected one), `round`, `rounds`, `max_rounds`), `review_rounds_spent` (a review reached with every round spent: `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `rounds`, `max_rounds`), `review_delivered` (the round's prompt was typed into its review window: `step`, `attempt`, `round`, `pane`, `window`, `reused`) and `review_delivery_failed` (`step`, `attempt`, `round`, `code`, and `reason` and `seen_*` when refused as `pane_busy`; see [A review round's window](#a-review-rounds-window)), `step_passed`, `step_failed`, `step_looped` (`backoff` in seconds when the retry waits), `step_retry_started` (the wait ended; `from`, `to`), `step_retry_blocked` (the wait ended but its pane is no longer the run's, so nothing was started; `from`, `to`, `code`), `gate_waiting`, `gate_approved` and `gate_rejected` (with `note` when the person gave one; `gate_approved` with `sha256` when it recorded a note), `run_resumed` (`restart_step: true` for a restart; `infra_retry` is the number of the automatic restart that made it), `infra_retry_scheduled`, `infra_retry_claimed` (with `taken_over: true` for a claim taken over), `infra_retry_released`, `infra_retry_ended` and `infra_retries_exhausted` (see [Automatic restarts](#automatic-restarts)), `step_interrupted`, `run_completed`, `run_cancelled`), each with `ts` and `type`.

`workflow show --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflows":[...]}` (each `id`, `title`, `description`, `source` (`project`, `global` or `builtin`: the first place the id is found, see [Definitions](#definitions)), `project` (the project's name for a `project` definition, else `null`), `path`, `sha256`, and `problems`, empty for a valid file), or with an ID `{"workflow":{...}}`: the definition with defaults filled in, `steps` as a list in run order, plus `source`, `project`, `path` and `sha256`. `show` takes the project from `--name`, else the current workspace. `workflow show ID` prints a `Stall:` line: the definition's own `stall`, or `Stall: 10m (default)` (`off (default)` when the default is turned off) from `workflows.default_stall`. In `--json` the workflow also has `effective_stall` (seconds, or `null` when off) and `stall_source` (`definition` or `default`).

`workflow show ID --source --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":{...}}` with `id`, `source` (`project`, `global` or `builtin`), `project`, `path`, `text` (the file's text, or `null` when its bytes are not UTF-8), `sha256` (of the file's raw bytes, whatever they are) and `problems` (empty when the file is valid). When `problems` is empty the definition's own fields (as `show ID` gives them) are added to the same object.

`workflow validate --json` on a valid text prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":{...}}`: the definition with defaults filled in (as `show ID` gives it), plus `source` (`file`, or `stdin`), `path` (absolute, or `null` for stdin) and `sha256` of the text's bytes. A top-level `warnings` array sits beside `workflow` (empty when there are none); each entry is `{"code":CODE,"step":STEP_ID,"message":TEXT}` (`step_without_proof`, `on_timeout_without_timeout`, `on_fail_exceeds_max_attempts`, `on_fail_exceeds_step_attempts`, `step_attempts_exceed_run`, `review_dropped`, `review_rounds_reach_step_attempts`, `review_rounds_exceed_run_attempts`, `unknown_pack` or `command_unset`; `step` is `null` for the workflow's own `include:` and the default packs). `not_checked` has the same entry shape for what could not be checked: outside a git checkout, and, for `commands_not_checked`, when the project's config file doesn't parse (`pack_not_checked`, `commands_not_checked`). On an invalid one it prints the error envelope with `code: "invalid_workflow"` and `details: {"workflow": ID, "path": PATH or null, "problems": [...]}`, and exits 1.

`workflow check --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":ID,"step":STEP,"worktree":PATH,"passed":BOOL,"command":TEXT,"exit_code":INT-or-null,"timed_out":BOOL,"error":TEXT-or-null,"timeout":WHOLE-SECONDS,"duration":SECONDS,"log":PATH,"artifacts":PATH,"artifacts_created":BOOL,"base_sha":SHA-or-"","tail":TEXT,"warnings":[{"code":"not_in_checkout","message":TEXT}]}` and exits 1 when `passed` is false (`tail` is the last 40 lines of the log, unescaped; needs `workflow` 26; see [trying a check](#trying-a-steps-check)).

`workflow render --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":ID,"step":STEP,"workspace":NAME,"worktree":PATH,"inputs":{},"gate":"approve"-or-null,"packs":[REF,...],"instructions_path":PATH,"previews":[{"name":"run","value":"wr_preview"},...],"warnings":[{"code":"not_in_checkout","message":TEXT}],"text":TEXT}` (`text` is the instructions unescaped; `instructions_path` is where a real attempt's file would go; needs `workflow` 25; see [rendering](#rendering-a-steps-instructions)).

`workflow graph ID --json` (the same as `--format json`) prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"graph":{"nodes","edges","legend"}}`, the model under `graph` (see [the graph document](README.json.md#actions); needs `workflow` 52). It is not an action document.

`workflow simulate --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"workflow":ID,"script":PATH,"inputs":{},"outcome":"completed"|"failed"|"cancelled"|"stopped"|"capped","stopped":{"step","attempt","reason"}-or-null,"capped":BOOL,"max_actions":200,"actions":N,"run":{"state","current","reason","loops"},"steps":[{"id","state","attempts":[{"n","outcome","code"[,"reviews":[{"reviewer","verdict","counted","problem"}]]}]}],"budget":{...}-or-null,"transitions":[{"n","at","type",...}]}`. `at` is seconds into the simulated time; each transition carries the keys of the run-history event of that `type`, plus `simulated_agent`, `simulated_reviewers`, `simulated_person`, `simulated_wait` and `simulated_refused` lines for the fake agent's and person's own moves. `reviews` is present only on a reviewed attempt (absent for an unreviewed step), taken from the attempt or, for a round that stopped `review_incomplete`, from its last round. `simulated_reviewers` carries `step`, `attempt`, `round` and `reviews` (the verdicts the script gave, a map of reviewer to verdict), and `rerun` for a rerun of the round's missing reviewers. `budget` is the run's token reading (`used`, `allowance`, `spent`, ...) when the definition declares one.

`workflow update --json` prints the same row with `outcome` `updated` (or `dry_run`) and the new `sha256`. Refusals print the error envelope: `invalid_workflow`, `unknown_workflow`, `builtin_workflow` (`details.workflow`), `stale_workflow` (`details.workflow`, `details.path`, `details.sha256` of the current file, or `null` when unreadable) or `usage`. For example `{"schema_version":1,"ok":false,"error":"The workflow file /home/me/.config/workspace/workflows/ship.yml changed: its sha256 is 9f2c..., not ab12.... Nothing was changed.","details":{"workflow":"ship","path":"/home/me/.config/workspace/workflows/ship.yml","sha256":"9f2c..."}}`.

`workflow remove --json` prints the same row with `outcome` `removed` (or `dry_run`), `workflow` and `path` (the file removed, or that would be). Refusals print the error envelope: `invalid_workflow` (an id that could not be a file name, or a directory at the path), `unknown_workflow`, `builtin_workflow`, `confirmation_required` or `usage`.

`workflow add --json` prints an [action document](README.json.md#actions) with one row: `outcome` `added` (or `dry_run`, with the document's `status` `dry_run`), `workspace` `null`, `workflow` (the id), `path` (the file written, or that would be) and `sha256` (of the text's bytes, as `show ID --source` will report it). Refusals print the error envelope: `invalid_workflow` (with `details.problems`), `workflow_exists` (`details.workflow`, `details.path`) or `usage`.

`workflow events --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":ID,"events":[...],"truncated":false,"next_since":N,"warnings":[]}`. Each event is `{"n","ts","type", ...}`: `n` the line number from 1, then the line's own keys (`ts`, `type` and the event's details; `n` in a line is ignored). A `gate_approved` or `gate_rejected` line has `note`, the text given to `approve --note` or `reject --note`, and no `note` key when there was none. In text output a value holding a line break or other control character is printed as a JSON string, so each event stays on one line. `next_since` is the last event's `n` when `truncated`, else the number of the last line read (or `--since` when nothing was read), so it is always the value to ask for next. For example, `workflow events wr_2610250930abcd --since 1 --json` on a run whose third line is not an object:

<!-- workflow-events-example -->
```json
{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":"wr_2610250930abcd","events":[{"n":2,"ts":"2026-10-25T09:30:00.000Z","type":"step_dispatched","step":"research","attempt":1},{"n":4,"ts":"2026-10-25T09:30:00.000Z","type":"step_done","step":"research","attempt":1,"status":"pass"}],"truncated":false,"next_since":4,"warnings":["Line 3 of the history is not a JSON object; skipped."]}
```

`workflow files --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":ID,"artifacts_dir":PATH,"files":[...]}`. Each file is `{"path","kind","step","attempt","exists","bytes"}` plus `"link":true` for a symlink, and for a `produces` file `"problem"`: the rule it breaks (`symlink`, `not_regular_file` or `empty`, else `null`; always `null` for a run started before the rules existed), from the same check the engine uses: `path` is absolute; `kind` is `prompt`, `check_log`, `proof_log` or `produces`; `attempt` is the attempt number, or `null` for `produces`; `bytes` is `null` when the file is missing, a link or not a regular file. For example, on a run whose first step has one attempt, a `produces` file that is there, one that is missing and one that is a symlink:

<!-- workflow-files-example -->
```json
{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"run":"wr_2610250930abcd","artifacts_dir":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd","files":[{"path":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd/steps/research.1.prompt.md","kind":"prompt","step":"research","attempt":1,"exists":true,"bytes":12},{"path":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd/steps/research.1.check.log","kind":"check_log","step":"research","attempt":1,"exists":true,"bytes":3},{"path":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd/research.md","kind":"produces","step":"research","attempt":null,"exists":true,"bytes":11,"problem":null},{"path":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd/plan.md","kind":"produces","step":"research","attempt":null,"exists":false,"bytes":null,"problem":null},{"path":"/work/app/.workflow/wr_2610250930abcd/notes.md","kind":"produces","step":"research","attempt":null,"exists":true,"bytes":null,"link":true,"problem":"symlink"}]}
```

`step status --json` prints `{"schema_version":1,"ok":true,"step":{...}}` with `run_id`, `workflow`, `workspace`, `step`, `attempt`, `state`, `instructions`, `artifacts_dir`, `produces` (each `path`, `exists`, `problem` (`symlink`, `not_regular_file` or `empty`, else `null`; always `null` for a run started before the rules existed) and `current`: written during this attempt and passing the rules), `check` (whether the step has one), `reported` (`status` and `summary`, or `null`) and, once a proof has run outside the run's lock, `proofs` (the attempt's rows, see [What an attempt records](#what-an-attempt-records)).

Failures print the [error envelope](README.json.md). Codes: `unknown_workflow`, `no_default_workflow` (`workflow start` with no ID and no default for the ref's kind), `invalid_workflow`, `workflow_exists`, `stale_workflow`, `builtin_workflow`, `input_required`, `workflow_command_unset`, `workflow_program_missing` and `workflow_test_files_unset` (a `require: exec` entry whose program is not found, or that uses `{{changed.tests}}` with `commands.test_files` unset; `workflow run` and `workflow start`), `invalid_input`, `dirty_worktree` (`workflow run`, `run --dry-run`, and `workflow start` into a reused workspace, for a `guard: unchanged` step), `fork_pull_request` (`workflow start`), `no_agent_pane`, `pane_has_run` (also `workflow resume --restart-step` when the chosen pane was bound to another run), `checkout_has_run`, `no_daemon`, `unknown_run`, `unknown_step` (`workflow attempts`), `run_not_active`, `already_paused` (`workflow pause`), `not_paused` (`workflow unpause`), `run_paused` (`workflow resume`, or a parked resume, of a paused run), `attempts_spent`, `step_attempts_spent`, `budget_spent` and `run_timed_out` (`workflow resume`, `reject`, `--extra-attempts`, `--extra-tokens` and `--extra-time`), `gate_not_waiting`, `gate_waiting`, `guard_cannot_pass` (`workflow resume` of a run stopped on `no_commit` with no base commit), `step_reviewing` (`resume --from` from the run's own pane while its step is being reviewed), `no_open_attempt`, `step_still_working`, `pane_ambiguous` and `restart_not_closed` (`workflow resume --restart-step`, see [Restarting a crashed step](#restarting-a-crashed-step); `no_agent_pane` and `pane_busy` too), `review_rounds_spent` (`workflow resume` of a step that has spent its review rounds), `bound_pane`, `not_bound`, `review_pane_bound` (`step done` or `step status` from a run's review pane), `step_not_running`, and `usage`.

## Events

Every change to a run writes one `workflow_changed` to the [event log](README.event-log.md), under the run's workspace, with `run_id`, `workflow`, `workspace`, `state`, `step` and `reason` (the stored reason code, or `null`).
